Steps 3.8–3.9 complete)*\n\n[Full review →](Detailed_Review_Report.md)\n```\n\n> **F-ID anchor links:** Each `[F<N>]` in `Review_Report.md` links to the corresponding finding heading in `Detailed_Review_Report.md` using GitHub-style heading anchors. Derive the anchor slug from the finding heading `#### Finding: [F<N>] – SHORT TITLE` by lowercasing, replacing spaces/brackets/dashes with `-`, and stripping special characters. Example: heading `#### Finding: [F3] – MISSING BOUNDS CHECK` → anchor `#finding-f3--missing-bounds-check`. If the anchor cannot be reliably derived, omit the anchor and link to the file only: `[F<N>](Detailed_Review_Report.md)`.\n\n**Cross-document link:** Both files reside in `.github/reviews/`. Use same-directory relative path: `[Detailed_Review_Report.md](Detailed_Review_Report.md)`.\n\n**SBOM traceability:** For CRITICAL or MAJOR findings in third-party subtrees (`Intel/`, `FatBinPkg/`, `ShellBinPkg/`, or other non-AMI packages), locate the nearest-ancestor SBOM file: check the sub-package directory and its ancestors for a `*.sbom.yaml` file; fall back to the project-level SBOM at the workspace root (typically named `<ProjectName>.sbom.yaml`) only when no sub-package SBOM exists on the path. Check the located SBOM for the component name and version; note it in the Fix row (format: `[SBOM: <component>@<version>]`). Skip for AMI-owned packages — no SBOM match expected.\n\n**Clean APPROVE shortcut:** CRITICAL=0, MAJOR=0, MINOR≤2: omit Issues and Limitations.\n\nAfter writing `Review_Report.md`, **must** run the Execution Plan Gate before writing `Result.txt`:\n\n**Execution Plan Gate — mandatory precondition for `Result.txt`:**\n\n1. **Read** `$SESSION_DIR/execution_plan.json` using `read_file`.\n   - **File not found** or `read_file` fails → gate **FAILS**. Treat all mandatory steps as pending. Do **NOT** write `Result.txt`. Reconstruct and execute all mandatory steps, then re-check.\n   - `RUNTIME_MODE = MEMORY-ONLY`: read from the in-memory execution plan list maintained since Step 0.2D.\n2. **Scan** every entry in the `steps` array for entries where `\"mandatory\"` is `1` **and** `\"status\"` is `\"pending\"`.\n   - **No such entries found** → gate passes; proceed to write `Result.txt` with the actual verdict.\n   - **One or more such entries found** → gate fails. **Must not** write `Result.txt` yet:\n     1. Execute each pending mandatory step in `step_id` ascending order (`s3-8` → `s3-9` → `s3-10` → `s3-11` → `s3-12` → `s3-13`).\n     2. After each step completes, update `execution_plan.json` via `replace_string_in_file` (change `\"status\": \"pending\"` → `\"status\": \"done\"` for that step's line).\n     3. Re-read `execution_plan.json` and confirm zero `mandatory=1, status=pending` entries remain before proceeding.\n     4. Write `Result.txt` with the actual verdict once the gate passes.\n     5. If any pending step cannot be completed (e.g., agent interrupted mid-execution), append `[GATE-INCOMPLETE: <step_id list> could not complete]` under the **Known Limitations** section of `Review_Report.md`. **Do not write `Result.txt` until all mandatory steps are done.** `Result.txt` must only ever contain `APPROVE` or `BLOCK`.\n\nThis gate is a **structural precondition of the output** — not a separate step — making it impossible to publish a verdict with unfinished mandatory steps.\n\nAfter the gate passes, **must** write `.github/reviews/Result.txt` (single-line verdict for CI/automation, always overwrites):\n\n**Content:** One line only, no markdown:\n- `BLOCK` — if verdict is 🛑 BLOCK or 🔴 CONDITIONAL BLOCK or 🟡 CONDITIONAL APPROVE\n- `APPROVE` — if verdict is 🟢 APPROVE\n\n**Example:** For a 🟡 CONDITIONAL APPROVE verdict → write `BLOCK` to `Result.txt`. This file is overwritten on every review run to provide the latest verdict for automation.\n\nAfter writing `Result.txt`, **must** append `Stage 3 Status: ✅ COMPLETE` to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file` — **must not** use `create_file` for this append.\n\n### Step 3.8 — AMI Module Developer's Guide Standards Validation (MANDATORY)\n\n**Trigger:** After `Stage 3 Status: ✅ COMPLETE` is appended to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` and before temp file cleanup.\n\n**Skip conditions:** None. Module Developer's Guide validation is MANDATORY and runs on all review sizes (Lite, Standard, and Full).\n\n**Action:**\n\n1. **Invoke skill:** Load `/ami-module-developer-standards` skill for MANDATORY AMI Module Developer's Guide standards.\n\n2. **Scope:** Validate **only** files that were reviewed in the main review (files listed in `cc_registry.md` with Review Status ≠ `NA`). Do not review files that were skipped or excluded.\n\n3. **Check mandatory standards:** For each in-scope file, verify:\n\n   **MANDATORY-MAJOR violations (blocking):**\n   - **Raw C types present** (`int`, `char`, `void`, `unsigned`, `long`) - use UEFI types (`UINT8/16/32/64`, `CHAR8/16`, `VOID`) instead\n   - **AMI OVERRIDE markers missing** in Intel/EDK2 files - all AMI modifications must be wrapped\n   - **Wrong customization layer** - changes in wrong package or violating layer hierarchy\n\n   **MANDATORY-MINOR violations (quality impact):**\n   - File header format (AMI copyright banner, current year, `@file` block)\n   - Source file organization (section order, `//------` separators, include order)\n   - Naming conventions (functions PascalCase, variables `g` prefix, constants ALL_CAPS)\n   - INF file structure (section order, `MdePkg` first in `[Packages]`)\n\n   **INFO observations (informational only):**\n   - Style suggestions, optional improvements\n\n4. **Severity classification rules:**\n   - **MANDATORY-MAJOR**: Added to main review MAJOR count; triggers verdict recalculation (may change APPROVE → CONDITIONAL/BLOCK)\n   - **MANDATORY-MINOR**: Added to main review MINOR count; impacts score calculation\n   - **INFO**: Informational only; no verdict impact\n\n   > **Critical distinction:** Raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`) are **always MANDATORY-MAJOR** because they cause ABI incompatibility, build failures, and runtime crashes. Naming convention violations (case, prefix) are MANDATORY-MINOR because they're style issues.\n\n5. **Focus:** Check **AMI-specific mandatory standards only**. UEFI/PI/EDK2 spec compliance was already covered in Stages 1-2. Do not duplicate findings from §1–§13 review dimensions.\n\n6. **Record findings:** Append a new section to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file`:\n\n```markdown\n---\n\n## AMI Module Developer's Guide Standards (MANDATORY)\n\nFiles validated against AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide mandatory standards (`.github/skills/ami-module-developer-standards/`).\n\n### Summary\n- AMI-owned files validated: X\n- Third-party files with AMI overrides validated: Y  \n- Third-party files skipped (no AMI code): Z\n- **MANDATORY-MAJOR findings:** X\n- **MANDATORY-MINOR findings:** Y\n\n### Findings\n\n**[File Path]** (AMI-owned / Third-party with AMI overrides)\n- [Standard]: [Description]\n  - Location: [file.c:line](../../path/file.c#Lline)\n  - Severity: **MANDATORY-MAJOR** / **MANDATORY-MINOR** / INFO\n  - Fix: [Concrete action]\n\n**Example:**\n**[AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c]** (AMI-owned)\n- File Header: Missing current year in copyright (2025 vs 2026)\n  - Location: [NvramDxe.c:3](../../AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c#L3)\n  - Severity: **MANDATORY-MINOR**\n  - Fix: Update copyright year to 2026\n- Naming Convention: Raw C type used\n  - Location: [NvramDxe.c:147](../../AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c#L147)\n  - Severity: **MANDATORY-MAJOR**\n  - Fix: Replace `int bufferSize` with `UINT32 BufferSize`\n\n*(Repeat for each file with findings. If no findings: write \"> All reviewed files comply with AMI Module Developer's Guide standards.\")*\n\n### Module Developer Standards Verdict\n✅ **COMPLIANT** / ⚠️ **NON-COMPLIANT (impacts main verdict)**\n\n**Severity Impact on Main Review Verdict:**\n- **MANDATORY-MAJOR**: X findings added to main review MAJOR count\n- **MANDATORY-MINOR**: Y findings added to main review MINOR count\n- **INFO**: Z informational observations (no verdict impact)\n```\n\n6b. **Also append summary to `Review_Report.md`:** After appending findings to `Detailed_Review_Report.md`, append a condensed summary to `Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file`, targeting the `## AMI Coding Standards  *(appended after Steps 3.8–3.9 complete)*` placeholder:\n\n```markdown\n## AMI Module Developer's Guide (MANDATORY)\n**Verdict:** ✅ COMPLIANT / ⚠️ NON-COMPLIANT | **MANDATORY-MAJOR:** X | **MANDATORY-MINOR:** Y | **INFO:** Z\n\n| File | Location | Finding | Severity | Fix |\n|------|----------|---------|----------|-----|\n| `path/file.c` | [file.c:line](../../path/file.c#Lline) | One-line description | MANDATORY-MAJOR / MANDATORY-MINOR | Verb-phrase fix |\n\n*(Omit table if no findings — write \"> All reviewed files comply with Module Developer's Guide standards.\")*\n\n## AMI Firmware Coding Standards  *(appended after Step 3.9 completes)*\n```\n\n7. **Update Agent State counters:** Add MANDATORY-MAJOR findings to the main review MAJOR count and MANDATORY-MINOR findings to the main review MINOR count.\n\n8. **Recompute verdict:** If MANDATORY-MAJOR or MANDATORY-MINOR findings affect the verdict (per Step 3.2 verdict table):\n   - Recompute score: add (MANDATORY-MAJOR × 5) + (MANDATORY-MINOR-A × 2) + (MANDATORY-MINOR-B × 1)\n   - Re-evaluate verdict table Rows 2–10\n   - If verdict changes from APPROVE → CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK:\n     - Update `Result.txt` using the Step 3.7 format rule: write `BLOCK` for 🟡 CONDITIONAL APPROVE or any 🔴/🛑 BLOCK verdict; write `APPROVE` only for 🟢 APPROVE. **Never write `CONDITIONAL APPROVE` literally to `Result.txt`.** **Monotonicity rule:** never downgrade — if `Result.txt` already contains `BLOCK`, do not overwrite.\n     - Append note to verdict section in `Detailed_Review_Report.md`:\n       ```markdown\n       > **Verdict Updated:** Module Developer's Guide validation added X MANDATORY-MAJOR and Y MANDATORY-MINOR findings. Verdict changed from [OLD] to [NEW].\n       ```\n\n9. **Classify MANDATORY-MINOR sub-tiers:**\n   - **MANDATORY-MINOR-A** (security-adjacent): Raw C types in non-critical paths, missing override markers in non-security files → Score: 2 points each\n   - **MANDATORY-MINOR-B** (code quality): Copyright year, naming case, section order → Score: 1 point each\n\n10. **Continue to Step 3.9:** After appending Module Developer Guide section and updating verdict if needed, proceed to Firmware Coding Standards validation (informational).\n\n### Step 3.9 — AMI Firmware Coding Standards Validation (INFORMATIONAL)\n\n**Trigger:** After Step 3.8 (Module Developer's Guide validation) completes and before temp file cleanup.\n\n**Skip conditions:** Module Developer's Guide validation was skipped.\n\n**Action:**\n\n1. **Invoke skill:** Load `/ami-firmware-coding-standards` skill for informational firmware coding style recommendations.\n\n2. **Scope:** Validate **only** files that passed Module Developer Guide validation in Step 3.8. Do not review files that were skipped.\n\n3. **Check firmware coding standards (all INFO severity):**\n   - Comment style (function headers, inline comments, TODO markers)\n   - Code readability (line length, function length, nesting depth, variable scope)\n   - Error handling patterns (consistency, resource cleanup, error message clarity)\n   - Performance considerations (unnecessary allocations, repeated calculations, string ops in loops)\n   - Maintainability (code duplication, magic numbers, complex expressions)\n   - Firmware-specific patterns (boot path optimization, memory footprint, S3 safety)\n\n4. **All findings are INFO severity:** No CRITICAL/MAJOR/MINOR findings from this skill. All observations are style recommendations only.\n\n5. **Record findings:** Append a new section to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file`:\n\n```markdown\n---\n\n## AMI Firmware Coding Standards (INFORMATIONAL)\n\nFiles validated against AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards style recommendations (`.github/skills/ami-firmware-coding-standards/`).\n\n### Summary\n- Files reviewed for style: X\n- Informational observations: Y\n\n### Findings\n\n**[File Path]** (AMI-owned / Third-party with AMI overrides)\n- [Category]: [Description]\n  - Location: [file.c:line](../../path/file.c#Lline)\n  - Severity: INFO (style recommendation)\n  - Suggestion: [Improvement recommendation]\n\n**Example:**\n**[AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c]** (AMI-owned)\n- Comment Style: Function header missing parameter description\n  - Location: [NvramDxe.c:147](../../AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c#L147)\n  - Severity: INFO (style recommendation)\n  - Suggestion: Add `@param` Doxygen tags for parameters\n- Code Readability: Function exceeds recommended length (215 lines)\n  - Location: [NvramDxe.c:200-415](../../AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c#L200-L415)\n  - Severity: INFO (maintainability)\n  - Suggestion: Consider refactoring into smaller functions\n\n*(If no findings: write \"> All reviewed files follow firmware coding style recommendations.\")*\n\n### Firmware Coding Standards Verdict\n✅ **STYLE COMPLIANT** / ℹ️ **STYLE OBSERVATIONS** (informational only)\n\n> **Note:** Firmware Coding Standards violations are **INFORMATIONAL ONLY** and do not affect the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK). Findings are tracked for post-merge cleanup or next revision.\n```\n\n5b. **Also append summary to `Review_Report.md`:** After appending findings to `Detailed_Review_Report.md`, append a condensed summary to `Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file`, targeting the `## AMI Firmware Coding Standards  *(appended after Step 3.9 completes)*` placeholder:\n\n```markdown\n## AMI Firmware Coding Standards (INFORMATIONAL)\n**Verdict:** ✅ STYLE COMPLIANT / ℹ️ STYLE OBSERVATIONS | **INFO observations:** X\n\n| File | Location | Category | Suggestion |\n|------|----------|---------|------------|\n| `path/file.c` | [file.c:line](../../path/file.c#Lline) | Category | One-line suggestion |\n\n*(Omit table if no findings — write \"> All reviewed files follow firmware coding style recommendations.\")*\n\n> **Note:** Informational only — no verdict impact.\n```\n\n6. **Do NOT update verdict:** Firmware Coding Standards findings are INFO only and never impact the APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK verdict or score. The `Result.txt` file remains unchanged.\n\n7. **Continue to Step 3.10 or cleanup:** When `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE`, proceed to Step 3.10 (Unit Test Standards Validation) before temp file cleanup. When `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = FALSE`, proceed directly to temp file cleanup.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3.10 — Unit Test Standards Validation (MANDATORY when IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE)\n\n**Trigger:** After Step 3.9 completes and `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE`. **Skip entirely when `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = FALSE`** — do not load the skill or append any section.\n\n**Skip conditions:** `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = FALSE`.\n\n**Action:**\n\n1. **Invoke skill:** Load `/unit-test-review` skill (`.github/skills/unit-test-review/SKILL.md`) for unit test standards validation. The skill is the authoritative source for all checks — follow it fully.\n\n2. **Scope:** Validate all files in the diff where the path contains `UnitTest/`, `GoogleTest/`, or the filename matches `*UnitTestHost.c`, `*UnitTestMock.c`, or `*UnitTestHost.inf`. Skip production source files and firmware INFs unrelated to unit test builds.\n\n3. **Score unit test findings into the main verdict:** Use the standard scoring table (MAJOR = +5, MINOR-A = +2, MINOR-B = +1, ×2 multiplier for P1/P2 files). **Severity cap: MAJOR maximum for `HOST_APPLICATION` code** — do not assign CRITICAL without FDF/DSC inclusion confirmation.\n\n4. **Record findings:** Append a new section to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file`, following the output format in Section 6 of the skill. Tag all finding IDs in this section with `[UNIT-TEST]`.\n\n5. **Append summary to `Review_Report.md`:** Append the condensed summary following the summary format in Section 6 of the skill.\n\n6. **Update `Result.txt` if verdict changed:** After adding unit test findings to the score, re-evaluate the verdict table (Step 3.3). If the new verdict is worse than the verdict current at the start of Step 3.10 (which already incorporates any Step 3.8 upgrades), overwrite `Result.txt` using the Step 3.7 format rule: write `BLOCK` for 🟡 CONDITIONAL APPROVE or any 🔴/🛑 BLOCK verdict; write `APPROVE` only for 🟢 APPROVE. **Never write `CONDITIONAL APPROVE` literally to `Result.txt`.** **Monotonicity rule:** never downgrade — if `Result.txt` already contains `BLOCK`, do not overwrite.\n\n7. **Continue to cleanup:** Proceed to temp file cleanup.\n\n**Temp file cleanup — mandatory:** **Must** delete all `$SESSION_DIR` temp files after `Review_Report.md`, `Detailed_Review_Report.md`, and `Result.txt` are confirmed written. **Must not** delete the final output files (`Review_Report.md`, `Detailed_Review_Report.md`, `Result.txt`). If cleanup fails, **must** record `[CLEANUP-FAILED: <reason>]` under Stage 3 Known Limitations and continue — a cleanup failure must not suppress the verdict.\n\n*PowerShell 5.1 (default — `$SESSION_DIR` is the session-scoped path from Step 0.2):*\n```powershell\nRemove-Item \"$env:REPO_ROOT/$SESSION_DIR\" -Recurse -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n```\n\n<details><summary>bash/WSL alternative</summary>\n\n```bash\nrm -rf \"$REPO_ROOT/$SESSION_DIR\"\n```\n\n</details>\n\nFinal output files (`Review_Report.md`, `Detailed_Review_Report.md`, and `Result.txt` in `.github/reviews/`) are permanent artifacts and are never deleted by the agent.\n\n**Verdict examples:** CRIT=0 MAJ=1 MIN=7 Score=12 → 🟡 Row 8 · CRIT=0 MAJ=2 MIN=3 (P1/P2) Score=26 → 🔴 Row 4 · CRIT=0 MAJ=0 MIN=4 Score=4 → 🟢 Row 10 · CRIT=0 MAJ=1 (P1) MIN=3 (P1) Score=16 → 🟡 Row 8\n\n---\n\n### Step 3.11 — MR Comment Summary Generation (CI/CD dimension only)\n\n**Trigger:** After temp file cleanup completes AND `ACTIVE_TAGS` contains `CI/CD`. **Skip entirely when `CI/CD` is not in `ACTIVE_TAGS`.**\n\n**Mandatory execution:** When `CI/CD` is in `ACTIVE_TAGS`, Steps 3.11 and 3.12 are **not optional** — they are pre-committed as `mandatory = 1` entries in the execution plan at Step 0.2D and are enforced by the Step 3.7 gate before `Result.txt` is written. They execute automatically after temp cleanup without requiring user prompting.\n\n**Purpose:** Pipelines that post the full `Detailed_Review_Report.md` or `Review_Report.md` as a GitLab MR comment produce unreadable walls of text. This step generates a compact, structured summary — saved as a separate file — that the pipeline posts as the MR comment instead.\n\n**Action:**\n\n1. **Generate summary** from data already in context memory (no new source reads or tool calls).\n2. **Write to** `.github/reviews/MR_Comment_Summary.md` using `create_file` (overwrite if file already exists).\n3. **Format — follow exactly. Do NOT add extra rows, metadata, or fields beyond what is shown below:**\n\n````markdown\n## 🤖 UEFI Code Review — Automated Analysis\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| **Verdict** | <🛑 BLOCK \\| 🔴 BLOCK \\| 🟡 CONDITIONAL APPROVE \\| 🟢 APPROVE> |\n\n### Finding Summary\n\n| Severity | Count | Blocking? |\n|----------|-------|-----------|\n| 🔴 CRITICAL | <N> | Yes |\n| 🔶 MAJOR | <N> | Yes (if score threshold met) |\n| 🔷 MINOR-A | <N> | No |\n| 🔷 MINOR-B | <N> | No |\n| ℹ️ INFO | <N> | No |\n\n### 🔴 Issues Requiring Attention\n*(omit this section entirely when verdict is APPROVE — replace with the ✅ line below)*\n\n| F-ID | File | Severity | Summary |\n|------|------|----------|---------|\n| F<N> | `path/file.c:<line>` | CRITICAL/MAJOR | One-line description |\n\n> ✅ No blocking issues found. All CRITICAL and MAJOR checks passed.\n*(use this line instead of the table above when verdict is APPROVE)*\n\n---\n<details>\n<summary>📄 Full report details</summary>\n\n> Full findings: `AI_Detailed_Review_Report-<REPORT_PREFIX><TIMESTAMP>.md` in `.github/reviews/` on this branch.\n> Executive summary: `AI_Review_Report-<REPORT_PREFIX><TIMESTAMP>.md`\n\n</details>\n\n<!-- COMMENT_SUMMARY generated by UEFI Code Review Agent v1.4-->\n````\n\n4. **Issues table:** Include **all CRITICAL and MAJOR findings** — no row cap. Order: CRITICAL first, then MAJOR; within each severity P1/P2 files first.\n5. **Record:** `[MR-COMMENT-SUMMARY: WRITTEN — .github/reviews/MR_Comment_Summary.md]` in context memory.\n6. **Emit to chat:** Print the full content of `MR_Comment_Summary.md` to the chat panel so the user can copy-paste it as an MR comment immediately if needed.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3.12 — Jira Comment & Attachment Post (CI/CD + Jira ID only)\n\n**Trigger:** After Step 3.11 completes AND `CI/CD` in `ACTIVE_TAGS` AND `JIRA_ID` is not empty.\n\n- **`CI/CD` not in `ACTIVE_TAGS`:** Skip silently — no note needed.\n- **`CI/CD` in `ACTIVE_TAGS` but `JIRA_ID` is empty:** Emit to chat and append to `Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file` at the end of the file:\n  ```markdown\n  ## Note\n  > **Jira Comment Skipped:** CI/CD tag is active but no Jira ID was resolved. Provide `Jira Id: <KEY>` in the prompt or ensure the commit message contains a Jira ticket key (e.g. `AOC-123`).\n  ```\n  Then stop this step.\n\n**Purpose:** Post the compact MR summary as a Jira comment and attach both full report files to the Jira ticket. Fully non-interactive — no user prompts at any point.\n\n**Core functionality impact: NONE.** This step runs after `Stage 3 Status: ✅ COMPLETE` is written, after temp file cleanup, and after Step 3.11. The review verdict, score, and all output files are already finalized before this step begins. A failure at any sub-step is recorded in context memory but never modifies the verdict, report files, or `Result.txt`.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.12.1 — Credential Resolution\n\n**Cross-platform approach:** Scripts live permanently in `.github/scripts/`. Runtime values are passed as environment variables — no file creation or deletion needed.\n\n**Action:**\n\n**Auto-detect Python** — run the following to try `python` first, then `python3`, store as `PY_CMD`, and run `jira_creds.py`:\n\n   **PowerShell (Windows):**\n   ```powershell\n   $PY_CMD = $null\n   foreach ($cmd in @('python','python3')) {\n       $test = & $cmd --version 2>$null\n       if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) { $PY_CMD = $cmd; break }\n   }\n   if ($PY_CMD) { & $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_creds.py } else { Write-Host 'PYTHON_NOT_FOUND' }\n   ```\n\n   **bash/WSL:**\n   ```bash\n   PY_CMD=''; for cmd in python python3; do if command -v $cmd &>/dev/null; then PY_CMD=$cmd; break; fi; done\n   if [ -n \"$PY_CMD\" ]; then $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_creds.py; else echo 'PYTHON_NOT_FOUND'; fi\n   ```\n\n   Store the working command as `PY_CMD` in context memory.\n\n- **`OK:<email>:<base_url>`** → Store `ATLASSIAN_EMAIL`, `ATLASSIAN_API_TOKEN`, `ATLASSIAN_BASE_URL`, `PY_CMD` in context memory. Proceed to Step 3.12.2.\n- **`MISSING`** → Record `[JIRA-COMMENT: SKIPPED — no credentials (set ATLASSIAN_EMAIL + ATLASSIAN_API_TOKEN env vars, or populate .github/.ami-credentials.json)]`. **Skip to Step 3.12.5.**\n- **`PYTHON_NOT_FOUND`** → Record `[JIRA-COMMENT: SKIPPED — Python not available (neither python nor python3 found in PATH)]`. **Skip to Step 3.12.5.**\n\n> **CI/CD pipeline:** Set `ATLASSIAN_EMAIL` and `ATLASSIAN_API_TOKEN` as pipeline secret variables. Optionally set `ATLASSIAN_BASE_URL` (default: `https://oneami.atlassian.net`).\n> **Local dev:** Create `.github/.ami-credentials.json` → `{\"email\":\"you@ami.com\",\"api_token\":\"<token>\",\"base_url\":\"https://oneami.atlassian.net\"}` and add `.github/.ami-credentials.json` to `.gitignore`.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.12.2 — Verify Ticket Exists\n\nRun with `JIRA_ISSUE_ID` set to `<JIRA_ID>`:\n\n   **PowerShell:** `$env:JIRA_ISSUE_ID=\"<JIRA_ID>\"; & $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_verify.py`\n\n   **bash:** `JIRA_ISSUE_ID=<JIRA_ID> $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_verify.py`\n\n- **`OK:<summary>`** → Ticket confirmed. Proceed to Step 3.12.3.\n- **`FAIL:404`** → Record `[JIRA-COMMENT: SKIPPED — ticket <JIRA_ID> not found]`. **Skip to Step 3.12.5.**\n- **`FAIL:*`** → Record `[JIRA-COMMENT: SKIPPED — ticket verification failed: <error>]`. **Skip to Step 3.12.5.**\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.12.3 — Post MR Summary as Jira Comment\n\nRun with `JIRA_ISSUE_ID` set to `<JIRA_ID>`:\n\n   **PowerShell:** `$env:JIRA_ISSUE_ID=\"<JIRA_ID>\"; & $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_comment.py`\n\n   **bash:** `JIRA_ISSUE_ID=<JIRA_ID> $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_comment.py`\n\n- **`OK:<id>`** → Record `[JIRA-COMMENT: POSTED — id <id> on <JIRA_ID>]`. Proceed to Step 3.12.4.\n- **`FAIL:*`** → Record `[JIRA-COMMENT: POST FAILED — <error>]`. Proceed to Step 3.12.4 regardless.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.12.4 — Attach Report Files\n\nRun with `JIRA_ISSUE_ID`, `REPORT_PREFIX`, and `REPORT_TIMESTAMP` set:\n\n   **PowerShell:**\n   ```powershell\n   $env:JIRA_ISSUE_ID=\"<JIRA_ID>\"; $env:REPORT_PREFIX=\"<REPORT_PREFIX>\"; $env:REPORT_TIMESTAMP=\"<TIMESTAMP>\"; & $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_attach.py\n   ```\n\n   **bash:**\n   ```bash\n   JIRA_ISSUE_ID=<JIRA_ID> REPORT_PREFIX=<REPORT_PREFIX> REPORT_TIMESTAMP=<TIMESTAMP> $PY_CMD .github/scripts/jira_attach.py\n   ```\n\n| Output | Action |\n|--------|--------|\n| Both lines `OK:<filename>` | Record `[JIRA-ATTACHMENTS: both reports attached to <JIRA_ID>]`. |\n| Any line `FAIL:*` | Record `[JIRA-ATTACHMENTS: PARTIAL/FAILED — <error>]`. Non-blocking. |\n| `LABEL:OK` | Record `[JIRA-LABEL: CopilotReviewed added to <JIRA_ID>]`. |\n| `LABEL:FAIL:*` | Record `[JIRA-LABEL: FAILED — <error>]`. Non-blocking. |\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.12.5 — Emit Result to Chat and Append to Report\n\n**Emit to chat:**\n```\n[JIRA-REVIEW-POST: <JIRA_ID>]\n  Comment     : <POSTED (id=<id>) | FAILED — <reason> | SKIPPED>\n  Attachments : <BOTH ATTACHED | PARTIAL — <file> failed | FAILED | SKIPPED>\n```\n\n**Append to `Review_Report.md`:** After emitting to chat, **must** append the Jira delivery result to `Review_Report.md` using `replace_string_in_file`, targeting the final `[Full review →](...)` line. This creates a durable audit trail that persists after the conversation ends — covering both success and failure cases.\n\n```markdown\n## Jira Delivery (<JIRA_ID>)\n| Action | Result |\n|--------|--------|\n| Comment | <POSTED (id=<id>) \\| FAILED — <reason> \\| SKIPPED — <reason> |\n| `<Review_Report filename>` attachment | <OK \\| FAILED — <reason> \\| SKIPPED> |\n| `<Detailed_Review_Report filename>` attachment | <OK \\| FAILED — <reason> \\| SKIPPED> |\n| Label `CopilotReviewed` | <OK \\| FAILED — <reason>> |\n```\n\n**Reminder:** A failed or skipped Jira post never affects the review verdict, score, report files, or `Result.txt`.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3.13 — Interactive Jira Post (Local / Non-CI/CD)\n\n**Trigger:** After temp file cleanup completes AND `CI/CD` is **NOT** in `ACTIVE_TAGS`. **Skip entirely when `CI/CD` is in `ACTIVE_TAGS`** — CI/CD uses Steps 3.11–3.12 automatically.\n\n**Use `vscode_askQuestions` for all user interactions in this step — never plain chat text. This renders clickable option buttons and labelled input fields directly in the chat UI.**\n\n**Initial prompt — use `vscode_askQuestions`:**\n```json\n[{\n  \"header\": \"post_to_jira\",\n  \"question\": \"Review complete. Would you like to post the results to a Jira ticket?\",\n  \"options\": [{\"label\": \"Yes\", \"recommended\": true}, {\"label\": \"No\"}],\n  \"allowFreeformInput\": false\n}]\n```\n\n- Response **\"No\"** → End review. No further action.\n- Response **\"Yes\"** → Proceed with the sub-steps below.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.13.1 — Resolve Jira Ticket ID\n\n**Use `vscode_askQuestions`:**\n\n- If `JIRA_ID` is already set in context:\n  ```json\n  [{\n    \"header\": \"jira_ticket_id\",\n    \"question\": \"Post results to Jira ticket <JIRA_ID>?\",\n    \"message\": \"Press Enter to confirm, or type a different ticket key (e.g., AOC-456).\",\n    \"options\": [{\"label\": \"<JIRA_ID>\", \"recommended\": true}]\n  }]\n  ```\n- If `JIRA_ID` is empty:\n  ```json\n  [{\n    \"header\": \"jira_ticket_id\",\n    \"question\": \"Enter the Jira ticket ID to post results to.\",\n    \"message\": \"Format: PROJECT-NUMBER  (e.g., BKC-1234)\"\n  }]\n  ```\n\nStore the entered/confirmed value as `JIRA_ID` in context. Validate format matches `[A-Z]{2,10}-\\d+`; if invalid, re-prompt once using the same `vscode_askQuestions` call.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.13.2 — Resolve Credentials\n\nCheck in order — stop at the first that succeeds:\n\n1. `ATLASSIAN_EMAIL` + `ATLASSIAN_API_TOKEN` env vars both set → use them. Skip to credential verification.\n2. `.github/.ami-credentials.json` exists (repo-level override) → use it. Skip to credential verification.\n3. `~/.ami-credentials.json` exists (`C:\\Users\\<you>\\` on Windows, `~` on Linux/Mac — shared across all workspaces) → use it. Skip to credential verification.\n4. None found → **credential file creation flow:**\n\n   **a) Ask using `vscode_askQuestions`:**\n   ```json\n   [{\n     \"header\": \"create_creds_file\",\n     \"question\": \"Credential file not found. Create ~/.ami-credentials.json?\",\n     \"message\": \"Creates C:\\\\Users\\\\<you>\\\\.ami-credentials.json (Windows) or ~/.ami-credentials.json (Linux/Mac). Shared across all VS Code workspaces — never inside any git repository.\",\n     \"options\": [{\"label\": \"Yes, create it\", \"recommended\": true}, {\"label\": \"No, cancel\"}],\n     \"allowFreeformInput\": false\n   }]\n   ```\n\n   - **\"No, cancel\"** → record `[JIRA-INTERACTIVE: SKIPPED — user declined credential setup]` and end Step 3.13.\n\n   - **\"Yes, create it\"** →\n     1. Use `create_file` to write `~/.ami-credentials.json` with placeholder content:\n        ```json\n        {\n          \"email\": \"your-atlassian-email@company.com\",\n          \"api_token\": \"your-api-token-here\",\n          \"base_url\": \"https://oneami.atlassian.net\"\n        }\n        ```\n        > Get your API token at: https://id.atlassian.com/manage-profile/security/api-tokens\n\n     2. Open the file in VS Code using `run_in_terminal`: `code ~/.ami-credentials.json`\n\n     3. Ask using `vscode_askQuestions`:\n        ```json\n        [{\n          \"header\": \"proceed_after_fill\",\n          \"question\": \"Fill in your credentials in the opened file, then click Proceed.\",\n          \"message\": \"Replace all three placeholder values:\\n• email — your Atlassian account email\\n• api_token — from https://id.atlassian.com/manage-profile/security/api-tokens\\n• base_url — your Jira instance URL (default is already correct for most users)\",\n          \"options\": [{\"label\": \"Proceed\", \"recommended\": true}, {\"label\": \"Cancel\"}],\n          \"allowFreeformInput\": false\n        }]\n        ```\n\n     - **\"Cancel\"** → record `[JIRA-INTERACTIVE: SKIPPED — user cancelled credential entry]` and end Step 3.13.\n     - **\"Proceed\"** → continue to credential verification below.\n\n**Credential verification:** Run `.github/scripts/jira_creds.py`. If output is `MISSING` → inform user which placeholder value was not replaced, re-show the Proceed/Cancel prompt once. If still `MISSING` → record `[JIRA-INTERACTIVE: SKIPPED — credential resolution failed]` and end Step 3.13.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.13.3 — Generate MR Comment Summary\n\nStep 3.11 was skipped (no `CI/CD` tag). Generate `MR_Comment_Summary.md` now by following **Step 3.11 instructions** verbatim, using the current review's verdict, scores, and findings already in context.\n\n---\n\n#### Step 3.13.4 — Post to Jira\n\nExecute **Steps 3.12.2 → 3.12.3 → 3.12.4 → 3.12.5** in order, using the `JIRA_ID` and credentials resolved in Steps 3.13.1–3.13.2. All existing scripts in `.github/scripts/` are used as-is.\n\n---\n\n## Review Dimensions §1–§13\n\n**Standards:** UEFI 2.10+, PI 1.8+, EDK II C Coding Standard 2.2+\n\nThese 13 review dimensions and 4 Abort Trigger gates govern per-CC code evaluation in Stage 2 Phase B. Each dimension is an ordered review gate evaluated sequentially.\n\n---\n\n### Dimension Applicability by File Type\n\n| Priority | File types | Applicable dimensions |\n|---|---|---|\n| P1 | SMM/StandaloneMM/SecureBoot/Crypto `.c`/`.h` | §1 (all triggers) · §2–§11 · §12 |\n| P2 | Standard DXE/PEI/Runtime `.c`/`.h` | §1 (all triggers) · §2–§11 · §12 |\n| P3 | `.inf`/`.dsc`/`.fdf`/`.dec` | §1 (Triggers #8, #12, #18 only) · §13 |\n| P4 | `.sdl`/`.mak`/`.cif`/`.veb` | §1 (Trigger #8 only) · §13 |\n| P4 | `.asl` | §1 (Trigger #12 if EDK2-owned) · §13 |\n| P4 | `.nasm`/`.asm` | §1 (Triggers #1, #2, #3, #15, #16 for SMM paths) · §13 |\n| P5 | `.vfr`/`.sd`/`.uni` | §13 only |\n| P6/P7 | Test/docs/scripts | §12 (copyright/`@file` only) |\n| P8 | Binary | No review (`[➡️ BINARY]`) |\n\n---\n\n### §1. Abort Trigger Gates (check FIRST)\n\nApply trigger groups in order. Skip a group if it does not apply to the current module type, file type, or changed hunk — note the skip only when it materially affected review scope. Any confirmed match produces **🛑 BLOCK** and must be recorded as `[🛑 CRITICAL – ABORT TRIGGER]`.\n\n#### Gate A — SMM Isolation and Lock-Window\n\n**Applies when:** file is SMM-related by MODULE_TYPE, path, library usage, or changed handler registration logic.\n\n| # | What to look for |\n|---|------------------|\n| 1 | SMRAM address/pointer as return value or `OUT` param to a non-SMM caller |\n| 2 | `LocateProtocol`, `HandleProtocol`, or `gBS->` call inside an SMM handler body |\n| 3 | CommBuffer pointer or size field written back to DXE-accessible memory by an SMM handler without sanitization — return-oriented data leak |\n| 15 | SMM handler or child dispatch handle registration (`Register`, `SmmRegisterProtocolNotify`, `InstallProtocolInterface`, or `SmmInstallProtocolInterface` for child protocols such as `EFI_SMM_SW_DISPATCH2_PROTOCOL`) after `EFI_SMM_READY_TO_LOCK_PROTOCOL` installed — covers both direct handler and child-dispatch lock bypass |\n| 16 | `AllocatePages` for SMRAM-resident code without configuring NX attributes via `EFI_MEMORY_ATTRIBUTES_PROTOCOL` — leaves SMM code pages executable, enabling code-injection if SMRAM is writable |\n\n#### Gate A-MM — Standalone MM Isolation\n\n**Applies when:** MODULE_TYPE = `MM_STANDALONE` or `MM_CORE_STANDALONE` or path contains `StandaloneMm/`, `MmStandalone/`, or `Standalone/`.\n\n| # | What to look for |\n|---|------------------|\n| MM-1 | `gSmst->` (traditional SMM services table) used inside a Standalone MM module — must use `gMmst->` (`EFI_MM_SYSTEM_TABLE`) instead; direct `gSmst->` access from `MM_STANDALONE` is an architectural violation = CRITICAL |\n| MM-2 | `EFI_MM_COMMUNICATE` CommBuffer dereferenced using `SmmCommunicateHeader.MessageLength` instead of `MmCommunicateHeader.MessageLength` — wrong header struct for Standalone MM path = MAJOR |\n| MM-3 | MM handler or child dispatch handle registered after `EFI_MM_READY_TO_LOCK_PROTOCOL` installed — same lock-window violation as Trigger #15, applied to Standalone MM context = CRITICAL |\n| MM-4 | `gBS->` or any Boot Services pointer accessed inside a Standalone MM handler — Boot Services are unavailable in Standalone MM execution context = CRITICAL |\n| MM-5 | `AllocatePages` for MMRAM-resident code without configuring NX attributes via `EFI_MEMORY_ATTRIBUTES_PROTOCOL` — leaves MM code pages executable, enabling code-injection if MMRAM is writable = CRITICAL |\n| MM-6 | `gMmst->MmLocateProtocol()` or `gMmst->MmHandleProtocol()` output pointer dereferenced without first verifying return is `EFI_SUCCESS` — null-protocol dereference inside Standalone MM handler = MAJOR |\n\n> **Gate A vs Gate A-MM:** Apply Gate A for `DXE_SMM_DRIVER`/`SMM_CORE`. Apply Gate A-MM for `MM_STANDALONE`/`MM_CORE_STANDALONE`. When MODULE_TYPE is unresolved and both SMM and Standalone MM are plausible, apply both gates and record `[DUAL-GATE-APPLIED: A + A-MM — MODULE_TYPE unresolved]`. When dual-gate fires produce overlapping findings on the **same line**, merge into a single finding citing both violations: `[DUAL-GATE: Gate A Trigger #N + Gate A-MM Trigger MM-N]`; severity is CRITICAL regardless. Do not emit two separate findings for the same line. **A dual-gate merged finding counts as ONE CRITICAL for Agent State, scoring, and verdict Rows 2–4 regardless of how many triggers contributed.**\n\n#### Gate B — CommBuffer and Untrusted-Size\n\n**Applies when:** changed code reads CommBuffer fields, dispatch contexts, DMA buffers, or untrusted size arithmetic.\n\n| # | What to look for |\n|---|------------------|\n| 4 | `CopyMem`/`SetMem`/array write where length comes from external input with no bounds check |\n| 5 | CommBuffer, DMA buffer, or dispatch pointer dereferenced before size/NULL check |\n| 6 | CommBuffer cast or field-index without `≥ sizeof` guard — **(a)** SW SMI handler casts `DispatchContext`/CommBuffer without checking `MessageLength ≥ sizeof(struct)`; **(b)** `SmmCommunicateHeader.MessageLength` used to cast or index CommBuffer without a `≥ sizeof` guard — apply whichever sub-point matches; never fire both on the same line |\n| 11 | `+`/`*`/`<<` on untrusted values fed to `CopyMem`/`SetMem`/`AllocatePool` size without SafeIntLib |\n| 17 | SMM handler reads CommBuffer fields a second time after initial validation instead of using an SMRAM-local copy — CommBuffer must be copied to an SMRAM-local struct before any code path that performs a **functional** field access (i.e., any access that influences control flow, output, or memory operations); non-functional reads inside `DEBUG()` macros are exempt when the module is not built with debug output in the release configuration; any subsequent functional field access that reads from the original DXE-accessible pointer is a TOCTOU exploit |\n\n> **Overlap note — CommBuffer family (#5, #6, #17):** Apply the most specific trigger only; never stack multiple triggers on the same line:\n> - **#5** — pointer/size dereferenced *before* any NULL/size check\n> - **#6** — cast or indexed without `≥ sizeof` guard\n> - **#17** — re-reads CommBuffer fields after validation instead of using the SMRAM-local copy\n\n#### Gate C — Runtime, S3, and Authenticated-Variable\n\n**Applies when:** changed code touches runtime callbacks, S3 boot script logic, or security database variables.\n\n| # | What to look for |\n|---|------------------|\n| 7 | `gBS->` call inside `ExitBootServices` callback or `DXE_RUNTIME_DRIVER` after `SetVirtualAddressMap`. **Also flag** `FreePool()`, `FreePages()`, or `FreeBuffer()` (MemoryAllocationLib wrappers or direct `gBS->` form) called inside an `ExitBootServices` callback — Boot Services memory management is unavailable at this point; calling these APIs causes unpredictable exceptions — CRITICAL. Detection: function registered via `CreateEventEx()` with `gEfiEventExitBootServicesGuid` or `CreateEvent(EVT_SIGNAL_EXIT_BOOT_SERVICES, ...)` whose body contains `FreePool`, `FreePages`, or `FreeBuffer` |\n| 10 | `EFI_S3_SAVE_STATE_PROTOCOL.Write()` called after `EndOfDxe`, or target region writable by DXE |\n| 13 | `SetVariable` to `db`/`dbx`/`dbt` (`EFI_IMAGE_SECURITY_DATABASE*`) without `EFI_VARIABLE_TIME_BASED_AUTHENTICATED_WRITE_ACCESS` attribute — bypasses image security database authentication |\n| 14 | `EFI_S3_SAVE_STATE_PROTOCOL` callback registration or `.Write()` call inside a `DXE_RUNTIME_DRIVER` or after `SmmReadyToLock` notification — S3 boot-script window violation |\n\n#### Gate D — Build, Ownership, and Image-Policy\n\n**Applies when:** patch changes SDL/build configuration, upstream-owned code, or image verification flow.\n\n| # | What to look for |\n|---|------------------|\n| 8 | `KEYS_DIR` or `DEBUG_MODE` token patched — follow chain to confirm the unsafe state: test keys still enabled in a release build (`DEBUG_MODE = 0`) |\n| 9 | Image verification returns `EFI_SUCCESS` unconditionally or skips signature check |\n| 12 | File under `Intel/`, `MdePkg/`, `MdeModulePkg/`, `SecurityPkg/`, `CryptoPkg/`, `StandaloneMmPkg/`, or `UefiCpuPkg/` modified without an AMI OVERRIDE marker. **Accepted syntax (any of the following is valid — do NOT fire if any variant is present):** For `.c`/`.h`: (1) `// AMI OVERRIDE START/END`, (2) `//APTIOV_OVERRIDE_START/END`, (3) `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:/END:`, (4) `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:/END:`, (5) any `//` comment matching regex `(AMI\\|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*START`. For `.inf`/`.dec`/`.dsc`/`.fdf`/`.mak`/`.cif`: same patterns with `#` comment prefix. Fire **only** when the changed hunk contains **no** override marker of any accepted variant. **AMI copyright banner exemption:** When a file is classified as ⚙️ **Third-party with AMI overrides** (file contains BOTH third-party and AMI copyright), the AMI copyright block itself (lines matching `Copyright.*AMI` or `//***` banner pattern) does NOT require wrapping in override markers — do NOT fire Trigger #12 for a hunk consisting solely of AMI copyright banner lines, provided at least one correctly-formed override block exists elsewhere in the same file. If the AMI copyright banner is the ONLY change in the file with no override markers anywhere → fire Trigger #12 (copyright-only change with zero markers = stealth modification). |\n| 18 | `InstallProtocolInterface` or `InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces` targeting `gEfiSecurityArchProtocolGuid` or `gEfiSecurity2ArchProtocolGuid` in a `DXE_DRIVER` outside `SecurityPkg/`. Exception: designated security handlers in `SecurityPkg/` or `AmiSecureBootPkg/` are exempt |\n\n**On match:** mark `[🛑 CRITICAL – ABORT TRIGGER]` and continue reviewing remaining CCs.\n\n**Trigger #8 evaluation chain:** (1) patched value → (2) project `.sdl` → (3) `CrbPkg/*.sdl` → (4) `AmiPkg/Configuration/Configuration.sdl`. Fire only when the unsafe combination is confirmed: test keys selected while `DEBUG_MODE = 0` (release build). If PCD cache marks the token `[BUILD-TIME-ONLY]`, rely exclusively on the SDL chain walk. If also unresolvable, emit `[TRIGGER-8-UNRESOLVABLE]` as INFO — do NOT block.\n\n> Abort Triggers (#1–#18) are fully evaluated through Gates A–D. Dimensions §2–§13 must NOT re-evaluate any Abort Trigger pattern. If a dimension check overlaps with a trigger, skip it — the trigger verdict stands.\n\n---\n\n### §2. Security\n\n- `BootOrder`/`BootXXXX` NV writes without auth check = MAJOR when Secure Boot path reachable\n- New GUIDs: collision check performed in Step 2.2 mandatory GUID uniqueness check\n- Keys/passwords must not appear in `DEBUG()` args = MAJOR\n- DMA buffers in protected memory; IOMMU/VT-d enabled before DMA access — missing = CRITICAL\n- `gBS->LocateProtocol()`, `gBS->OpenProtocol()`, `gBS->HandleProtocol()` output pointer dereferenced without first verifying return is `EFI_SUCCESS` = MAJOR (null-protocol dereference)\n- `LocatePpi()`/`GetPpiList()` output pointer used without `EFI_SUCCESS` check in PEI context = MAJOR\n\n### §3. Memory Safety\n\n- **`ExitBootServices` callback — `FreePool`/`FreePages`/`FreeBuffer` forbidden:** These APIs must not be called inside an `ExitBootServices` callback (`EVT_SIGNAL_EXIT_BOOT_SERVICES` / `gEfiEventExitBootServicesGuid`). Boot Services are unavailable at this point; calling memory-free APIs causes unpredictable exceptions — CRITICAL. Memory no longer needed after boot must be freed **before** `ExitBootServices` fires. **Agent must never suggest adding `FreePool`/`FreePages`/`FreeBuffer` inside an EBS callback as a fix.** If the lifecycle check below finds a missing free whose only plausible placement is inside the EBS callback, close as `FALSE POSITIVE` — do not open a finding.\n- Every `AllocatePool`/`AllocatePages` has matching `FreePool`/`FreePages` on all exit paths — missing = MAJOR. **EBS exception:** if the only location where the free is absent is an `ExitBootServices` callback body, treat as `FALSE POSITIVE` per rule above — never suggest adding the free call there.\n- No use-after-free, no dangling pointers, no protocol pointers held across uninstall — violation = CRITICAL\n- **`CR()`/`BASE_CR()` macro cast without Signature guard** — cast recovering outer struct via `CR()`/`BASE_CR()` must verify `Struct->Signature == EXPECTED_SIGNATURE` immediately before or after the macro; missing guard = MAJOR when the pointer is externally supplied or crosses a trust boundary (e.g., originating from a protocol caller, CommBuffer, or DXE↔SMM interface); missing guard = MINOR-A when the pointer originates from the module's own protocol installation and no external attacker path exists; if SMM-resident struct with DXE-accessible pointer origin = CRITICAL\n- No unsafe string APIs (`StrCpy`/`AsciiStrCpy`/`sprintf`) — use safe `*S` equivalents = MAJOR\n- `AsciiSPrint`/`UnicodeSPrint`/`AsciiVSPrint`/`UnicodeVSPrint`: `BufferSize` from untrusted arithmetic without SafeIntLib = MAJOR\n- Credential/key buffers zeroed with `ZeroMem` before `FreePool` — missing = MAJOR if buffer held password, key, or auth token\n- Local stack arrays > 4 KB in SMM or Standalone MM (`MM_STANDALONE`) = MAJOR\n- `GetVariable()` return checked `EFI_SUCCESS` before arithmetic on `DataSize` — using `DataSize` after failure = MAJOR\n- `GetVariable()` two-pass size pattern: verify (a) `DataSize > 0` before allocation and (b) second call returns `EFI_SUCCESS` before buffer dereference — either missing = MAJOR. *Cross-check:* if the variable is security-sensitive, also verify §4 `VariableLock->RequestToLock()` is present\n- **API migration — double pointer OUT parameters:** When `AllocatePool`/`AllocateZeroPool`/`AllocatePages`/`AllocateRuntimePool` replaces `gBS->AllocatePool(Type, Size, &Buffer)`, verify correct dereference: `VOID **OutputParam` → must write `*OutputParam = AllocatePool(...)` and check `*OutputParam == NULL`. Assigning to the local parameter copy leaves the caller's pointer unchanged = CRITICAL (memory leak + uninitialized pointer dereference)\n- **API migration — mixed allocation/deallocation APIs:** MemoryAllocationLib allocation paired with `gBS->FreePool`/`gBS->FreePages` = MAJOR. Correct pairings: MemoryAllocationLib → `FreePool`/`FreePages`; Boot Services → `gBS->FreePool`/`gBS->FreePages`. Partial migration in progress → flag as MAJOR with recommendation to complete consistently\n- **String pointer storage in struct members:** Struct members of type `CHAR8 *`, `CHAR16 *`, or `char *` assigned from allocated buffers must either (a) copy the string data with `AllocateCopyPool`/`AllocateZeroPool` + `AsciiStrCpyS`/`StrCpyS` or (b) transfer ownership by nulling the source pointer after assignment. Pattern: `Context->StringPtr = AllocatedBuffer; FreePool(AllocatedBuffer);` without prior copy = CRITICAL (dangling pointer). Correct patterns: (1) `Context->StringPtr = AllocateCopyPool(AsciiStrSize(Src), Src); FreePool(Src);` or (2) `Context->StringPtr = Src; Src = NULL;` (ownership transfer). Verify the stored pointer is freed in the struct's cleanup/destructor function; missing cleanup = MAJOR (memory leak)\n- **String pointer array cleanup:** For arrays like `CHAR8 *StringSet[]` or `CHAR16 *StringArray[]`, ownership must be explicit. If strings are heap-allocated copies (via `AllocateCopyPool`, `AllocatePool` + `StrCpyS`, or `StrDuplicate`-like helpers), each element must be freed individually in a loop before freeing the array itself: `for (i = 0; i < Count; i++) { FreePool(StringArray[i]); } FreePool(StringArray);` — missing per-element free = MAJOR (memory leak). If strings are borrowed references (pointers to static strings, .rodata, or external buffers not owned by this module), freeing individual elements = CRITICAL (use-after-free / double-free). Detection heuristic: trace string assignment origin — if from `AllocatePool`/`AllocateCopyPool`/`StrDuplicate`, ownership transferred; if from string literal, global buffer, or function returning `CONST CHAR*`, borrowed reference. Ambiguous cases → `Confidence: MEDIUM` with recommendation to document ownership contract\n\n### §4. Variable Service Security\n\n- Security-sensitive NV variables locked via `VariableLock->RequestToLock()` before `EndOfDxe` — missing = MAJOR\n- `GetVariable()` single-call with hardcoded buffer on untrusted variable = MAJOR (two-pass pattern → §3)\n- `EFI_VARIABLE_AUTHENTICATION_2`: `AuthInfo.Hdr.wCertificateType` must be `WIN_CERT_TYPE_EFI_GUID` — writing without valid descriptor = MAJOR\n- `EFI_VARIABLE_NON_VOLATILE + RUNTIME_ACCESS` audited for necessity; large NV write without `QueryVariableInfo` = MINOR\n\n### §5. S3 Resume Security\n\n- Pointers passed to `Write()` must not be stack variables or DXE-writable ACPI NVS buffers — violation = MAJOR\n- Hardware writes to TSEG/SMRR registers must be immediately followed by a lock step — missing = MAJOR\n\n### §6. Concurrency & Locking\n\n- `EFI_LOCK`: `EfiInitializeLock` before first use; every `EfiAcquireLock` has matching `EfiReleaseLock` — missing release = MAJOR\n- SMM: `SmmAcquireLock`/`SmmReleaseLock` around shared globals from multiple CPUs/APs — unprotected = MAJOR\n- Standalone MM (`MM_STANDALONE`): `SmmAcquireLock`/`SmmReleaseLock` are not available; use `EFI_SPIN_LOCK` (via `SynchronizationLib`) to protect shared globals accessed by MM handlers from multiple APs — unprotected `= MAJOR`\n- `EFI_SPIN_LOCK`: `InitializeSpinLock` before use; `AcquireSpinLock`/`ReleaseSpinLock` balanced — unbalanced = MAJOR\n- TPL: raising to lower TPL than current = MAJOR; `TPL_HIGH_LEVEL` outside interrupt context = MAJOR\n- `EFI_MM_MP_PROTOCOL.DispatchProcedure()`: shared globals from AP procedures must be guarded by `EFI_SPIN_LOCK` — unprotected = MAJOR (PI 1.8 §4.10.2)\n\n### §7. Spec Compliance (UEFI 2.10+ / PI 1.8+)\n\n- `[UEFI]` UEFI types only (`EFI_STATUS`, `UINTN`, `UINT8/16/32/64`, `BOOLEAN`, `VOID`, `EFI_HANDLE`) — no `int`/`char`/`void` = MINOR\n- `[UEFI]` `gRT->` callable post-`ExitBootServices` — runtime-safe memory only = MAJOR\n- `[UEFI]` Protocols installed/uninstalled properly (pointer lifetime → §3)\n- `[UEFI]` Variable services: proper attributes, size validated, GUID traceable (`EFI_STATUS` checking → §8)\n- `[PI]` PEI: PPIs only, no protocols; CAR limits apply; no temp RAM pointers in HOBs\n- `[PI]` DXE: `[Depex]` lists all required protocols/GUIDs\n- `[PI]` SMM: SMRAM locked after `SmmReadyToLock`; CommBuffers validated\n- `[PI]` Standalone MM (`MM_STANDALONE`): MMRAM locked after `EFI_MM_READY_TO_LOCK_PROTOCOL` installed; CommBuffers validated using `EFI_MM_COMMUNICATE_HEADER`; `gBS->` and all Boot Services unavailable; `gSmst->` must not be used — use `gMmst->` (`EFI_MM_SYSTEM_TABLE`) exclusively\n- `[PI]` HOBs: `GET_GUID_HOB_DATA_SIZE >= sizeof(<Type>)` validated by DXE consumers\n- `[PI]` Phase separation: PEI must not use DXE-only interfaces; runtime must not use boot-only interfaces\n\n### §8. Correctness & Lifecycle\n\n- Every `EFI_STATUS` return checked — not silently discarded = MAJOR\n- All error paths clean: no leaked resources, no held mutexes — violation = MAJOR\n- All `else`/`default` branches handle errors = MINOR — **FP guard:** before flagging, confirm the branch is not an intentional fallback path (e.g., a by-design default return or NO-OP documented in comments); if intent is by design, classify as INFO or skip\n- Re-entrancy guards for functions callable from nested events — missing = MAJOR\n- TPL safety: shared data at `TPL_APPLICATION` protected by TPL raise — unprotected = MAJOR\n- Every `CreateEvent`/`CreateEventEx` matched by `CloseEvent` on non-success paths and in `Stop()`/unload — missing = MAJOR\n- `ExitBootServices` callback must not call `gBS->`, `AllocatePool`, or `FreePool` — violation = MAJOR\n- `OpenProtocol(BY_DRIVER)` in `Start()` matched by `CloseProtocol()` in `Stop()` — mismatch = MAJOR\n- Buffers from `AppendDevicePath`/`DuplicateDevicePath` freed with `FreePool` on all exit paths — missing = MAJOR\n\n### §9. PEI-Specific Depth\n\n- Local arrays > 1 KB in any PEI function = MAJOR (CAR stack ~64–128 KB; risks overflow)\n- Pre-memory `AllocatePool` results must not be referenced after `MemoryDiscoveredPpiNotify` — stale reference = MAJOR\n- `BuildGuidDataHob` size must equal `sizeof(<struct>)` exactly — over/under = MAJOR\n- `BuildGuidDataHob` alignment: if struct natural alignment ≥ 8 bytes and `sizeof(struct)` is not a multiple, HOB data area is not naturally aligned for X64 consumer casts — undefined behavior = MAJOR\n- `GetFirstGuidHob()` result NULL-checked; `GET_GUID_HOB_DATA_SIZE` validated ≥ `sizeof(expected struct)` before cast — missing = MAJOR\n- `PeiServicesGetBootMode()` consulted before S3/recovery-specific init = MINOR\n\n### §10. Capsule / FMP Security\n\n- `LowestSupportedImageVersion` honored; any bypass = CRITICAL (anti-rollback)\n- `CheckImage()` return gated before `SetImage()` — `SetImage()` without prior success = MAJOR\n- `UpdateImageTypeId` matched against `GetImageInfo()` `ImageTypeId` — silent mismatch = MAJOR\n- Capsule payload size validated against actual buffer length before parsing — missing = MAJOR\n- `CAPSULE_FLAGS_PERSIST_ACROSS_RESET` set only when reset is required — otherwise = MINOR\n\n### §11. General Algorithmic & Functional Logic\n\nReview functional correctness of changed `.c`/`.h` code independent of UEFI/PI spec mandates. Apply Pre-Finding Gate before opening any finding. Findings here are typically MINOR unless they cause data corruption or incorrect control flow affecting firmware reliability. Tag findings `[LOGIC]`.\n\n> **§8 / §11 tie-breaker:** If a correctness issue also involves `EFI_STATUS` semantics, protocol lifetime, event/lock balance, or resource lifecycle, classify it under §8 (Correctness & Lifecycle) and do not duplicate it under §11.\n\n- **Off-by-one errors:** Loop bounds (`<` vs `<=`), buffer index, array sizing — incorrect bound = MAJOR if buffer accessed\n- **Inverted or wrong condition:** `==` vs `!=`, `>` vs `>=`, negated flag, wrong enum in `switch`/`if` — causes incorrect branch = MAJOR\n- **Dead / unreachable code:** `return`/`break`/unconditional assignment renders subsequent lines unreachable — MINOR; if it masks error path = MAJOR — **FP guard:** an explicit early-return guard (e.g., `if (Ptr == NULL) { return EFI_INVALID_PARAMETER; }`) or a documented intentional fallback is not dead code; do NOT flag it even if the happy path always executes after it\n- **Stale or wrong variable used:** Copy-paste error with wrong variable — MAJOR if data corruption results\n- **Incorrect bit manipulation:** Wrong shift, missing mask, bit cleared instead of set — MAJOR if affects hardware register or security flag; MINOR otherwise\n- **State machine correctness:** Missing transition, unreachable terminal state, skipped required intermediate — MAJOR if firmware stalls or mis-sequences init\n- **Integer wrap / truncation (non-security):** Narrowing cast or decrement below zero on `UINTN`/`UINT32` — MINOR if no security path; MAJOR if controls allocation or copy\n- **Redundant or contradictory logic:** Condition always true/false, duplicate `if` branches — MINOR\n- **Incorrect return value:** Returns value not matching documented contract — MAJOR\n- **Uninitialized variable used:** Local variable read before assignment on some code path — MAJOR if influences control flow or output\n- **Algorithm correctness:** Sorting/searching/checksum/table-walk loops: verify algorithm matches stated intent — MAJOR if incorrect result silently propagates\n- **Preprocessor conditional scope violation:** A variable, label, or function *defined* inside an `#if`/`#ifdef`/`#ifndef` block but *referenced* outside the enclosing `#endif` — causes a compilation error when the condition is not met = MAJOR `[LOGIC]`. Detection: when a diff hunk introduces a symbol definition inside a conditional block, `grep_search` the file for uses of that symbol name outside the enclosing `#if`/`#endif` pair; if found → MAJOR. **FP guard:** if the same symbol is also declared or defined unconditionally elsewhere in the file (e.g., a guard-free fallback declaration), do NOT flag.\n\n### §12. EDK II C Coding Standard 2.2+\n\n- **Naming:** PascalCase functions · `g` prefix globals · ALL_CAPS macros/constants = MINOR\n- **Indentation:** 2 spaces per level; no tabs = MINOR\n- **Comments:** Doxygen `/** ... **/` for file/function docs; `//` inline = MINOR\n- **No implicit fall-through** in `switch` — end each `case` with `break`, `return`, or `// FALL THROUGH` = MINOR; if it masks a security branch = MAJOR\n- **Magic numbers:** named `#define` macros only = MINOR\n- **ASSERT:** via `DebugLib`; never sole security gate (stripped by `MDEPKG_NDEBUG`) = MAJOR. A **sole security gate** means `ASSERT()` is the only check preventing an unsafe operation — e.g., `ASSERT(Buffer != NULL)` immediately before `CopyMem(Dst, Buffer, Size)` with no prior `if (Buffer == NULL) return;` runtime guard. ASSERTs protecting internal invariants structurally unreachable from external attacker-controlled input are not security gates; classify those as INFO.\n- **Parameter qualifiers:** `CONST`/`IN`/`OUT`/`IN OUT` match actual usage; `IN` param must not be modified = MINOR\n- **Copyright year** ≥ commit year; `@file` block present = INFO\n- **Include order:** `<Uefi.h>` or `<Pei.h>` first, then `<Token.h>`, AMI libs, EDK2 libs, protocols, platform headers = INFO\n- **Code Organization & Placement** — review only high-signal structural cases. Aggregate into single `[🔷 MINOR-B]` finding tagged `[CODE-ORG-AGGREGATE]`:\n   - Function prototypes/declarations mid-file (after first `{`) = MINOR `[CODE-ORG]`\n   - `#include` directives not at file top (after `#define`/`typedef`/`struct`/`extern`/function body) = MINOR `[CODE-ORG]`\n   - Global variable declarations after first function definition = MINOR `[CODE-ORG]`\n   - All other placement issues: better handled by linting/CI unless they hide a security guard or cause build error\n- **INF section rules:** see §13 for `[Packages]` ordering, section validation, and DSC cross-check\n\n### §13. File-Type Specific (non-`.c`/`.h` files only)\n\n#### `.inf` files\n\n- `[Defines]` complete · `[Sources]` lists changed files · `[Packages]` MdePkg first\n- `[LibraryClasses]`/`[Protocols]`/`[Ppis]`/`[Guids]` match actual usage (cross-check DSC) — **FP guard for section classification:** before flagging a GUID identifier as in the wrong section, read the owning package `.dec` file to confirm its declaration section (`[Protocols]`, `[Ppis]`, or `[Guids]`); the INF section must match the `.dec` declaration — do NOT rely on naming conventions alone; if `.dec` is inaccessible, tag `[INF-SECTION-UNVERIFIED]` and downgrade to INFO\n- `[Depex]` covers all required protocols and notify-install callbacks\n- `[Pcd]`/`[PcdEx]` entries match actual usage — before flagging an entry as unused, apply the two-step check:\n  - Step 1: grep the module's `.c` source for `PcdGet*()`/`PcdSet*()` on that PCD name\n  - Step 2: if Step 1 returns no matches, **grep the headers transitively included by the module** for `_PCD_VALUE_<PcdName>` or `_PCD_SIZE_<PcdName>` — EDK2 emits these as compile-time array-dimension constants into `AutoGen.h` only when the PCD is declared in `[Pcd]`; a header expression such as `UINT32 Table[_PCD_VALUE_PcdFoo]` is a valid passive consumer that mandates the `[Pcd]` entry\n  - If Step 2 finds a match → `FALSE POSITIVE`; do NOT open a finding. Only flag unused when **both** steps return no matches.\n- INF-DELTA mismatches from Stage 1 cache: apply the three-tier severity as defined in Step 3.1.7 (CRITICAL → security-critical; MAJOR → default; MINOR → call path unreachable)\n\n#### `.sdl` files\n\n> 📖 **SDL skill — load on demand only:** When `SDL-TOKEN`, `SDL-ELINK`, `SDL-MODULE`, `SDL-PCDMAP`, or `SDL-LIBMAP` CCs are present in the Stage 1 registry, load the `/sdl-handling` skill (`.github/skills/sdl-handling/SKILL.md`) for authoritative Aptio V field names, syntax rules, validation checks, and DSC/FDF translation tables. **Do not load this skill when no SDL CCs are registered — it is not needed and increases review time unnecessarily.**\n\n**Authoritative Aptio V field names (skill is the source of truth — these override the SDL spec document names):**\n\n| Object | Correct field name in Aptio V SDL | Spec-doc alias — appears only in NDA field tables, NOT in real SDL; do NOT require this name in submitted code and do NOT flag submitted code that uses the correct left-column name |\n|--------|--------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `INFComponent` | `ModuleTypes` (plural) | `Module_Type` |\n| `LIBRARYMAPPING` | `ModuleTypes` (plural) | `Module_Type` |\n| `PcdMapping` | `GuidSpace` | `Guid` |\n| `PcdMapping` | `PcdType` (full `\"Pcds\"` prefix required) | `Pcd_Type` |\n| `PcdMapping` | `DataType` | `Data_Type` |\n| `FFS_FILE` | `Apriori_Index` must be a quoted string | bare integer |\n\n**False positive guards — do NOT open findings for these valid Aptio V SDL patterns:**\n\n| Pattern observed | Correct interpretation — not a defect |\n|-----------------|---------------------------------------|\n| Multiple `Token = \"A\" \"=\" \"1\"` lines on one SDL object | AND-logic multi-condition — not a duplicate field violation |\n| Two `PcdMapping` blocks sharing the same `Name` but inverse `Token` conditions | Valid else-branch pattern — not a TOKEN uniqueness or duplicate-block violation |\n| `FFS_FILE` block with no `Module_Type` field | `Module_Type` is **mutually exclusive** with `INFComponent`, `File_Stmt`, and `OpRom` per NDA spec — when `INFComponent` is present (normal Aptio V case), `Module_Type` must be absent; do NOT flag this absence as a missing required field |\n| `LIBRARYMAPPING` `Instance = \"Pkg.ComponentName\"` dot format | Required format — not a path error |\n\n- TOKEN uniqueness and correct override layer · PcdMapping type/guards correct · MODULE path valid · `INFComponent.Name` unique across all SDL files · `INFComponent.File` path relative to Package root\n- LibraryMapping `Class` / `Instance` (`\"Package.ComponentName\"` dot format) / `ModuleTypes` correct\n- ELINK: no duplicate Name+Parent; missing trailing comma on function-name entry = MAJOR (hard build error); verify return type + param types against cached prototype\n  - Confirmed signature mismatch = MAJOR; escalate to CRITICAL when hook runs in security-boundary-sensitive path — determined by chain name keywords (`Smm`/`StandaloneMm`/`SecureBoot`/`Auth`/`Capsule`) **or** registered function's MODULE_TYPE being `DXE_SMM_DRIVER`, `SMM_CORE`, `MM_STANDALONE`, `MM_CORE_STANDALONE`, `DXE_RUNTIME_DRIVER` or similar\n  - `VOID*`↔typed-pointer = MAJOR\n  - Typedef not found = MINOR — `[ELINK-PROTOTYPE-UNVERIFIED: manual build verification required]`\n- `DISABLE_CAPSULE_AUTHENTICATION=1` = MAJOR · `SDL_REGRESSION` entries = MAJOR\n- For `KEYS_DIR` / `DEBUG_MODE` TOKEN changes: apply Trigger #8 (§1 Gate D)\n- Security-sensitive ELINK chains: verify hook does not bypass upstream security\n- **TOKEN rename or removal — consumer dependency check:** When a diff deletes a `TOKEN` block or changes a TOKEN `Name` field, extract the old name from the removed diff lines (`-    Name  = \"OLD_TOKEN_NAME\"`). Run `grep_search` workspace-wide across `.c`, `.h`, `.sdl`, `.mak`, and `.asl` files for the old token name. Consumers to detect: `#if OLD_TOKEN_NAME`, `#ifdef OLD_TOKEN_NAME`, `defined(OLD_TOKEN_NAME)`, `$(OLD_TOKEN_NAME)` in `.mak`/`.sdl` `Value` fields, `Token = \"OLD_TOKEN_NAME\"` SDL conditions. **Any match = MAJOR** — the old name is still consumed; a rename without a global update causes consumers to silently evaluate to `0` (undefined macro = 0 in the C preprocessor) or fail SDL dependency resolution with no compile error, making it a silent behavioral regression. No consumers found → PASS. Tag `[TOKEN-DEPENDENCY: <N> consumer(s) found — <file:line list>]` or `[TOKEN-DEPENDENCY: CLEAR — no consumers of old name detected]`.\n- **ELINK chain-root rename or removal — orphan child check:** When a diff deletes or renames an ELINK that has no `Parent` field (`InvokeOrder = ReplaceParent` — a chain root), extract the old `Name` value. Run `grep_search` in `.sdl` files for `Parent  = \"<old-name>\"`. Also check `.c` files for the old generated macro name (typically `<UPPER_SNAKE_CASE_OF_NAME>` used in `extern` or array declarations). **Any match = MAJOR** — child ELINK entries point to a non-existent root; the SDL build tool generates an empty or broken dispatch list, silently dropping all registered hooks. Tag `[ELINK-ROOT-ORPHAN: <N> child(ren) still reference old root \"<old-name>\"]`.\n- **ELINK leaf-entry removal — hook removal check:** When a diff removes an ELINK that has a `Parent` field (a leaf/contributor entry registering a function into a chain), verify the removed hook is not the sole registered entry for a security-sensitive chain (keywords in chain name: `Smm`, `StandaloneMm`, `SecureBoot`, `Auth`, `Capsule`, `Password`, `Key`). Sole security hook removed → **MAJOR**. Non-security or non-sole hook removed → **MINOR-A**. Tag `[ELINK-HOOK-REMOVED: <function-name> unregistered from chain \"<parent-name>\"]`.\n\n#### `.dec` files\n\n- `[Defines]` complete · `[Includes]` paths valid · GUID/PCD entries unique = MAJOR\n\n#### `.fdf` files\n\n- FV names match DSC · no region overlap · `AmiTrustedFvPkg` boundaries intact · security modules in correct FV\n- Boundary or security-module violation = CRITICAL; other = MAJOR\n\n#### `.vfr`/`.sd`/`.uni` files\n\n- Varstore type/size matches C struct · Question IDs unique = MAJOR · INTERACTIVE+callback pairing correct = MAJOR\n- No orphaned `suppressif` · Security options not inadvertently hidden = MAJOR · String tokens present in `.uni` = MINOR\n- `efivarstore` binding: verify `VariableLock->RequestToLock()` exists before `EndOfDxe` — missing lock = MAJOR when the variable carries `RUNTIME_ACCESS` attribute or contains security-sensitive data (boot order, auth tokens, keys); missing lock = MINOR-A when the variable lacks `RUNTIME_ACCESS` and is a non-security HII staging buffer whose writes are confined to the boot-services phase; verify backing variable uses `EFI_VARIABLE_NON_VOLATILE` — volatile-backed varstore silently loses data on warm reset = MAJOR\n\n#### `.asl` files\n\n- `_STA` bits 5–31 not set = MINOR\n- `OperationRegion` field access width matches field alignment — mismatch = MAJOR\n- `_OSC` input `Buffer` ≥ 16 bytes; undersized dereference = MAJOR\n- `Notify()` opcode validated for device type; out-of-range = MINOR\n- `_PRS` end-tag present; `_CRS` buffer size consistent with descriptor list — truncated = MAJOR\n- No hardcoded MMIO/PCI addresses conflicting with bridge `_CRS` or DRHD entries = MAJOR\n- `_DSM` implementation: `Arg2` (function index) range-checked before dispatch; `Arg3` (function-argument package) structure validated before element access — missing validation = MAJOR; `Arg0` (UUID buffer) matched against the expected GUID before any `_DSM` handler is invoked — missing UUID check allows wrong-handler invocation = MAJOR\n- `OperationRegion` field `AccessType` must be compatible with the field's `BitOffset` and `BitLength` — e.g., `ByteAcc` field with `BitOffset` not on an 8-bit boundary, or `WordAcc` without 16-bit alignment = MAJOR\n- `Notify()` target device must be defined and reachable within the current ASL namespace scope — referencing an undefined or out-of-scope device node = MAJOR\n\n#### `.nasm`/`.asm` files\n\n- Stack alignment preserved per x64/IA32 calling convention (16-byte for x64, 4-byte for IA32) — misalignment = MAJOR\n- Callee-saved registers (RBX, RBP, RDI, RSI, R12-R15) preserved across function calls — missing preservation = MAJOR\n- For SMM entry/handler code (`SmiEntry`, `SmiException`, `*Smm*.nasm`): no `gBS->` or Boot Services calls — CRITICAL (§1 Trigger #2); SMRAM pointers not leaked to non-SMM code — CRITICAL (§1 Trigger #1); handler registered after `ReadyToLock` — CRITICAL (§1 Trigger #15)\n- Control-flow integrity: RET instructions paired with CALL; no arbitrary JMP to computed addresses without validation — unpaired = MAJOR\n- Interrupt flag (`CLI`/`STI`) manipulation justified and paired — unbalanced = MAJOR for SMM/exception handlers\n- Memory operand sizes match declared data types — size mismatch = MAJOR\n- CET (Control-flow Enforcement Technology) compliance when `CET_ENABLED`: shadow stack operations correct; ENDBR64/ENDBR32 present at indirect call targets — missing = MINOR when CET disabled; MAJOR when CET enabled per INF `[Defines]` or build flags\n- For S3 resume code: temporary stack usage bounds-checked; no persistent pointers to CAR/temp memory stored in HOBs or variables — unbounded stack = MAJOR\n\n#### `.veb` files\n\nThe `.veb` file is the project manifest that maps every `.cif` component to its source-control label. Each entry has the format:\n`\"<path>.cif\"= \"SS: AptioV;$/<source-path>;<ModuleName>_<LabelNumber>\"`\nThe label is the last `;`-delimited token. Multiple `.cif` entries for the same module share the same label. Skip any entry whose value is `Private`.\n\n**Case 1 — Commit message consistency:** Collect all added `+` diff lines. Extract each unique module label suffix (text after the last `;`, e.g. `NvmeRstPassword_11`). Parse the commit message for those label identifiers. Any changed label that is **not mentioned** anywhere in the commit message → **MAJOR**. Tag `[VEB-LABEL-COMMIT: <N> unexpected entries changed — <list of unmentioned labels>]`. If all changed labels are covered → PASS.\n\n> **Multi-module sync commits:** A commit that bumps multiple packages (e.g., Intel RC sync) must list every changed label in the commit message. Unlisted labels still fire MAJOR.\n\n**Case 2 — SDL version token match:** For each unique changed module label:\n1. Determine the package SDL — e.g., `NvmeRstPassword_11` → `AmiChipsetModulePkg/SB/NvmeRstPassword/NvmeRstPassword.sdl`; `AmiTrustedFvPkg_22` → `AmiTrustedFvPkg/AmiTrustedFvPkg.sdl`. Use the `.cif` path prefix to locate the owning SDL.\n2. `grep_search` the package SDL for any TOKEN block with `_VERSION` or `_REVISION` in its Name and a `Value` field.\n3. **Compare:** Extract the numeric label suffix from the `.veb` entry (e.g., `11` from `NvmeRstPassword_11`). Compare with the token `Value`.\n   - **Match → PASS.** Tag `[VEB-VERSION-TOKEN: CONSISTENT — <TOKEN_NAME> = <value>]`.\n   - **Mismatch → MAJOR.** Tag `[VEB-VERSION-TOKEN: MISMATCH — .veb label <N> but <TOKEN_NAME> = <M>]`.\n   - **False-positive guard:** If the token name ends in `_REVISION` (not `_VERSION`) **and** the token's Help text contains a cross-reference note mapping revision to label (e.g., `\"Rev59 = SVN label 55\"` or `\"Rev<X> = label<Y>\"`), then a numeric difference between the label and the token value is **intentional and documented**. In this case downgrade to **INFORMATIONAL** and tag `[VEB-VERSION-TOKEN: REVISION-OFFSET-NOTED — Help text documents deliberate offset; label <N>, token <TOKEN_NAME> = <M>]`. Do NOT fire MAJOR.\n   - **No version token found → INFORMATIONAL.** Tag `[VEB-VERSION-TOKEN: NO-TOKEN — no _VERSION or _REVISION SDL token found in <sdl-path>]`. This is expected for Intel RC packages (e.g., `RaptorLakePlatSamplePkg`, `ClientOneSiliconPkg`) which use composite version strings and have no integer SDL token.\n   - **Complex label strings** (e.g., `RaptorLakePlatSamplePkg_0C.00.43.42(7113.00)_066`) — no integer SDL token is expected; skip Case 2 and emit `[VEB-VERSION-TOKEN: NO-TOKEN — Intel RC composite label, no SDL token expected]`.\n\n#### `.dsc`/`.mak`/`.cif`/`.nsh` files\n\n- Copyright year (see §12) · general correctness only\n- For `.dsc` `[PcdsFixedAtBuild]` hunks: if `PcdCpuSmmRestrictedMemoryAccess` is present, confirm it is not `FALSE` in a release build (`DEBUG_MODE = 0`) — raise MAJOR\n"

       4| # Mermaid AI Skills
       5| 
       6| When the user asks to create, edit, or visualize any diagram, use the Mermaid
       7| VS Code extension tools and commands described below.
       8| 
       9| ## Workflow
      10| 
      11| 1. Determine the diagram type and generate Mermaid syntax.
      12| 2. Write the diagram to a `.mmd` file in the project.
      13| 3. Validate syntax: correct first-line keyword, arrow types, balanced brackets.
      14| 4. Preview via the Mermaid extension — open the `.mmd` file (auto-preview) or run
      15|    **MermaidChart: Preview Diagram** (`mermaidChart.preview`).
      16| 
      17| ## LM Tools — call these for every diagram interaction
      18| 
      19| - `mermaid-diagram-validator` — validate Mermaid syntax before presenting any diagram
      20| - `mermaid-diagram-preview` — render a live preview inside VS Code after generating
      21| - `get-syntax-docs-mermaid` — fetch correct syntax docs for any diagram type
      22| 
      23| ## VS Code Commands
      24| 
      25| Invoke via Command Palette or the VS Code command API (GitHub Copilot in VS Code only).
      26| Do not invent command IDs. Prefer writing/editing `.mmd` files when a command is not needed.
      27| 
      28| ### Diagram editing & preview
      29| - **Preview** (`mermaidChart.preview`) — preview the active Mermaid editor (`.mmd` / `.mermaid` must be open).
      30| - **Create Diagram** (`mermaidChart.createMermaidFile`) — creates a demo flowchart and opens preview side by side.
      31| - **Repair Diagram** (`mermaidChart.repairDiagram`) — Mermaid AI repair for the active diagram; uses Mermaid AI credits — tell the user before running.
      32| - **Improve Diagram** (`mermaidChart.improveDiagram`) — uses Copilot / LM API; suggests layout + styling variants for the active diagram.
      33| 
      34| ### Generate diagrams (GitHub Copilot required)
      35| - **Generate Diagram from Code** (`mermaidChart.generateDiagramFromCode`)
      36| - **Generate Cloud Diagram** (`mermaidChart.generateCloudDiagram`)
      37| - **Generate ER Diagram** (`mermaidChart.generateERDiagram`)
      38| - **Generate Docker Diagram** (`mermaidChart.generateDockerDiagram`)
      39| - **Open AI Chat** (`mermaidChart.openCopilotChat`)
      40| 
      41| ### Mermaid Chart cloud
      42| - **Login** (`mermaidChart.login`) / **Logout** (`mermaidChart.logout`)
      43| - **Connect Diagram** (`mermaidChart.connectDiagramToMermaidChart`) — link a local diagram to Mermaid Chart.
      44| - **Sync Diagram** (`mermaidChart.syncDiagramWithMermaid`) — only for diagrams already connected (frontmatter has `id:`). Example:
      45|   ```yaml
      46|   ---
      47|   id: cbd9e9ba-a2cb-47c5-a98e-8c28a753428d
      48|   ---
      49|   ```
      50| 
      51| ### Review Mermaid Sync
      52| For diagrams updated by the Mermaid Chart GitHub Sync app (or pre-commit regenerate):
      53| - **Review Mermaid Sync** (`mermaidChart.reviewAppCommits`) — start / open the review flow.
      54| - **Regenerate with Mermaid AI** (`mermaidChart.regenerateDiagramWithMermaidAI`) — regenerate from source references.
      55| Do not manually rewrite diagrams managed by this workflow. Accept/reject/diff UI actions stay in the extension UI.
      56| 
      57| ### Install / update this pack
      58| - **MermaidChart: Install AI Skills…** (`mermaidChart.installAiSkills`)
      59| 
      60| ## @mermaid-chart slash commands
      61| 
      62| | Command | Purpose |
      63| |---|---|
      64| | `/generate_diagram_from_code` | General diagram from any source file |
      65| | `/generate_execution_sequence` | Sequence diagram from code flow |
      66| | `/generate_er_diagram` | ER diagram from schema / models |
      67| | `/generate_cloud_architecture_diagram` | Cloud / CI-CD architecture |
      68| | `/generate_docker_diagram` | Architecture from Dockerfiles |
      69| | `/generate_c4_topdown_architecture` | C4 top-down architecture |
      70| | `/analyze_code_ownership` | Code ownership diagram |
      71| | `/generate_dependency_diagram` | Dependency / security visualisation |
      72| 
      73| ## Rules
      74| 
      75| 1. Always call `mermaid-diagram-validator` before showing any diagram.
      76| 2. Always call `mermaid-diagram-preview` after generating a diagram.
      77| 3. Use `get-syntax-docs-mermaid` before generating an unfamiliar diagram type.
      78| 4. Prefer `@mermaid-chart` slash commands for complex generation.
      79| 5. Write diagrams to `.mmd` files; never return unvalidated Mermaid syntax.
      80| 6. Warn the user before Repair (Mermaid AI credits).
      81| 7. Cooperate with the Sync workflow — do not manually regenerate managed diagrams.
