       1| # AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards (INFORMATIONAL)
       2| 
       3| **Source:** AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards  
       4| **Compliance Level:** INFORMATIONAL - Style recommendations only  
       5| **Scope:** Firmware-specific style guidance extending Module Developer's Guide
       6| 
       7| ---
       8| 
       9| ## Validation Scope: AMI Code Only (INFORMATIONAL)
      10| 
      11| These standards are **INFORMATIONAL** style recommendations for AMI-owned code. Violations do NOT impact the final review verdict.
      12| 
      13| ### Relationship to Module Developer's Guide
      14| 
      15| | Standard | Compliance Level | Impact |
      16| |----------|------------------|--------|
      17| | **Module Developer's Guide** | MANDATORY | Violations impact verdict (MAJOR/MINOR) |
      18| | **Firmware Coding Standards** (this document) | INFORMATIONAL | Violations tracked for post-merge improvement |
      19| 
      20| **All Firmware Coding Standards findings use INFO severity.**
      21| 
      22| ---
      23| 
      24| ## 1. Comment Style and Documentation
      25| 
      26| ### Function Header Comments
      27| 
      28| **Guideline:** All functions should have complete Doxygen-style header comments.
      29| 
      30| ```c
      31| /**
      32|   Initialize the NVRAM driver.
      33| 
      34|   This function performs the following:
      35|   - Allocates NVRAM buffers
      36|   - Registers variable services protocol
      37|   - Installs NVRAM access protocol
      38| 
      39|   @param[in]  ImageHandle   The firmware allocated handle for the EFI image
      40|   @param[in]  SystemTable   A pointer to the EFI System Table
      41| 
      42|   @retval EFI_SUCCESS       NVRAM initialized successfully
      43|   @retval EFI_OUT_OF_RESOURCES  Failed to allocate buffers
      44|   @retval EFI_DEVICE_ERROR  NVRAM hardware error
      45| 
      46| **/
      47| EFI_STATUS
      48| EFIAPI
      49| NvramDxeInit (
      50|   IN EFI_HANDLE        ImageHandle,
      51|   IN EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE  *SystemTable
      52|   )
      53| ```
      54| 
      55| **Observations (INFO severity):**
      56| - Missing `@param` tags
      57| - Missing `@retval` descriptions
      58| - Missing function purpose description
      59| - Missing detailed behavior notes
      60| 
      61| ### Inline Comments
      62| 
      63| **Guideline:** Complex logic should have explanatory comments.
      64| 
      65| ✅ **Good:**
      66| ```c
      67| // Check if variable is authenticated before allowing delete
      68| if (!(Attributes & EFI_VARIABLE_AUTHENTICATED_WRITE_ACCESS)) {
      69|   return EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION;
      70| }
      71| ```
      72| 
      73| ⚠️ **Needs Improvement:**
      74| ```c
      75| // Check flags
      76| if (!(Attr & 0x20)) {  // INFO: Magic number, unclear purpose
      77|   return EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION;
      78| }
      79| ```
      80| 
      81| ### TODO/FIXME/HACK Markers
      82| 
      83| **Guideline:** Use standardized markers for known issues.
      84| 
      85| ```c
      86| // TODO: Optimize buffer allocation for S3 path
      87| // FIXME: Race condition when multiple threads access the variable
      88| // HACK: Temporary workaround for silicon errata, remove in next release
      89| ```
      90| 
      91| ---
      92| 
      93| ## 2. Source File Structure
      94| 
      95| ### C File Organization
      96| 
      97| **Guideline:** C source files must follow this strict order:
      98| 
      99| 1. **File Header** (copyright + `@file` block)
     100| 2. **Include Statements** (grouped and ordered)
     101| 3. **Constants, Macros, and Type Definitions**
     102| 4. **Variable and External Declarations**
     103| 5. **Function Definitions**
     104| 
     105| **Rules:**
     106| - Function prototypes/declarations must appear before function definitions
     107| - Group related declarations together
     108| - No mixed ordering (e.g., function definition → variable → function definition)
     109| - Function definitions should appear in logical order (initialization → helpers → main logic)
     110| 
     111| ✅ **Good Structure:**
     112| ```c
     113| //***********************************************************************
     114| // File Header
     115| //***********************************************************************
     116| 
     117| // Include(s)
     118| #include <Uefi.h>
     119| #include <Library/BaseLib.h>
     120| 
     121| // Constant Definition(s)
     122| #define MAX_SIZE  256
     123| 
     124| // Type Definition(s)
     125| typedef struct {
     126|   UINT32  Value;
     127| } MY_STRUCT;
     128| 
     129| // Function Prototype(s)
     130| EFI_STATUS HelperFunction(VOID);
     131| EFI_STATUS MainFunction(VOID);
     132| 
     133| // Variable Declaration(s)
     134| STATIC UINT32  mCounter = 0;
     135| 
     136| // Function Definition(s)
     137| EFI_STATUS
     138| HelperFunction (
     139|   VOID
     140|   )
     141| {
     142|   // Implementation
     143| }
     144| 
     145| EFI_STATUS
     146| MainFunction (
     147|   VOID
     148|   )
     149| {
     150|   // Implementation
     151| }
     152| ```
     153| 
     154| ⚠️ **Poor Structure (INFO):**
     155| ```c
     156| // Function definition before declaration
     157| EFI_STATUS SomeFunction(VOID) { ... }  // INFO: Declare before define
     158| 
     159| // Variable after function
     160| STATIC UINT32 mVar;  // INFO: Variables should come before functions
     161| 
     162| // Another function
     163| EFI_STATUS AnotherFunction(VOID) { ... }
     164| ```
     165| 
     166| **Observations (INFO severity):**
     167| - Mixed ordering of declarations and definitions
     168| - Function definitions before declarations
     169| - Variables scattered throughout file
     170| - Related functions not grouped
     171| 
     172| ---
     173| 
     174| ## 3. Header File Rules
     175| 
     176| ### No Code in Headers
     177| 
     178| **Guideline:** Header files (`.h`) must contain ONLY declarations, never code implementations.
     179| 
     180| ❌ **Prohibited in Headers:**
     181| ```c
     182| // MyHeader.h
     183| void MyFunction() {  // INFO: Function definition in header
     184|   DoSomething();
     185| }
     186| 
     187| STATIC UINT32 mVariable = 0;  // INFO: Variable definition in header
     188| ```
     189| 
     190| ✅ **Correct:**
     191| ```c
     192| // MyHeader.h
     193| void MyFunction(VOID);  // Declaration only
     194| 
     195| extern UINT32 mVariable;  // External declaration
     196| ```
     197| 
     198| ### Include Guards
     199| 
     200| **Guideline:** All header files must use include guards.
     201| 
     202| ✅ **Required Pattern:**
     203| ```c
     204| #ifndef _MY_HEADER_H_
     205| #define _MY_HEADER_H_
     206| 
     207| // Header content
     208| 
     209| #endif  // _MY_HEADER_H_
     210| ```
     211| 
     212| **Observations (INFO severity):**
     213| - Missing include guards
     214| - Incorrect guard naming (should match filename)
     215| - Missing closing `#endif` comment
     216| 
     217| ### Self-Contained Headers
     218| 
     219| **Guideline:** Headers should be self-contained and include all dependencies.
     220| 
     221| ✅ **Good:**
     222| ```c
     223| // MyProtocol.h
     224| #include <Uefi.h>  // Needed for EFI_STATUS
     225| 
     226| EFI_STATUS InstallMyProtocol(VOID);
     227| ```
     228| 
     229| ⚠️ **Needs Improvement:**
     230| ```c
     231| // MyProtocol.h
     232| EFI_STATUS InstallMyProtocol(VOID);  // INFO: Missing <Uefi.h> for EFI_STATUS
     233| ```
     234| 
     235| ### Minimize Header Includes
     236| 
     237| **Guideline:** Include only what is directly needed in the header.
     238| 
     239| - Avoid including headers that are only needed by the `.c` implementation
     240| - Use forward declarations when possible
     241| - Separate public vs private definitions (use separate headers if needed)
     242| 
     243| ✅ **Good:**
     244| ```c
     245| // MyPublic.h - Public interface
     246| typedef struct _MY_PROTOCOL MY_PROTOCOL;  // Forward declaration
     247| 
     248| EFI_STATUS InstallProtocol(MY_PROTOCOL *Protocol);
     249| ```
     250| 
     251| ---
     252| 
     253| ## 4. Source Line Formatting
     254| 
     255| ### One Statement Per Line
     256| 
     257| **Guideline:** Each statement should be on its own line.
     258| 
     259| ❌ **Avoid:**
     260| ```c
     261| Status = Init(); if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) return Status;  // INFO: Split into multiple lines
     262| ```
     263| 
     264| ✅ **Good:**
     265| ```c
     266| Status = Init();
     267| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     268|   return Status;
     269| }
     270| ```
     271| 
     272| ### Operator Spacing
     273| 
     274| **Guideline:** Consistent spacing around operators improves readability.
     275| 
     276| ✅ **Good Spacing:**
     277| ```c
     278| Result = Value1 + Value2;       // Space around binary operators
     279| Pointer->Member;                // No space around ->
     280| Structure.Field;                // No space around .
     281| Array[Index];                   // No space before [
     282| Function(Arg1, Arg2);           // Space after comma
     283| for (i = 0; i < Max; i++);      // Space after semicolon
     284| ```
     285| 
     286| ⚠️ **Inconsistent (INFO):**
     287| ```c
     288| Result=Value1+Value2;           // INFO: Add spaces
     289| Pointer -> Member;              // INFO: Remove spaces
     290| Function(Arg1,Arg2);            // INFO: Add space after comma
     291| ```
     292| 
     293| ### Variable Declarations
     294| 
     295| **Guideline:** One variable per line (except related simple variables).
     296| 
     297| ✅ **Preferred:**
     298| ```c
     299| UINT32  BufferSize;
     300| UINT32  MaxSize;
     301| UINT8   *Buffer;
     302| ```
     303| 
     304| ⚠️ **Avoid:**
     305| ```c
     306| UINT32 BufferSize, MaxSize, *Pointer;  // INFO: Split into separate lines
     307| ```
     308| 
     309| **Observations (INFO severity):**
     310| - Multiple variables declared on one line
     311| - Inconsistent spacing in declarations
     312| - Mixed types on same line
     313| 
     314| ---
     315| 
     316| ## 5. Naming Quality
     317| 
     318| ### Meaningful Names
     319| 
     320| **Guideline:** Use descriptive, unambiguous names that convey purpose.
     321| 
     322| ❌ **Avoid Ambiguous Names:**
     323| ```c
     324| UINT32  Size1, Size2;           // INFO: What do these sizes represent?
     325| UINT32  Var1, Var2, Var3;       // INFO: Non-descriptive
     326| UINT32  Temp, Tmp, T;           // INFO: Overly abbreviated
     327| ```
     328| 
     329| ✅ **Better:**
     330| ```c
     331| UINT32  InputBufferSize;
     332| UINT32  OutputBufferSize;
     333| UINT32  MaxTransferSize;
     334| ```
     335| 
     336| ### Avoid Similar Names
     337| 
     338| **Guideline:** Prevent confusion from similar-looking identifiers.
     339| 
     340| ⚠️ **Confusing (INFO):**
     341| ```c
     342| UINT32  BufferSize;             // Lower camel case
     343| UINT32  buffersize;             // All lowercase - INFO: Too similar
     344| UINT32  BUFFERSIZE;             // All caps - INFO: Case-only difference
     345| UINT32  Buffer_Size;            // Underscore variant - INFO: Inconsistent
     346| ```
     347| 
     348| **Observations (INFO severity):**
     349| - Case-only differences between identifiers
     350| - Similar names with minor variations
     351| - Single-letter differences (e.g., `DataPtr` vs `DataPrt`)
     352| 
     353| ### Appropriate Abbreviations
     354| 
     355| **Guideline:** Use well-known abbreviations; avoid cryptic short forms.
     356| 
     357| ✅ **Acceptable Abbreviations:**
     358| - `Max`, `Min`, `Num`, `Ptr`, `Addr`, `Len`, `Idx`
     359| - `Init`, `Cfg`, `Tmp`, `Mgr`, `Ctrl`
     360| 
     361| ⚠️ **Overly Abbreviated (INFO):**
     362| ```c
     363| UINT32  BufSz;                  // INFO: Use BufferSize
     364| UINT32  NmElmnts;               // INFO: Use NumElements
     365| EFI_STATUS  Sts;                // INFO: Use Status
     366| ```
     367| 
     368| **Observations (INFO severity):**
     369| - Unclear abbreviations
     370| - Inconsistent abbreviation style within same file
     371| - Names too short to be meaningful
     372| 
     373| ---
     374| 
     375| ## 6. Third-Party Code Modification (Porting Markers)
     376| 
     377| ### AMI Porting Markers - REQUIRED
     378| 
     379| **Guideline:** ALL modifications to third-party or EDK2 code MUST be wrapped with AMI porting markers.
     380| 
     381| **Required Pattern:**
     382| ```c
     383| //*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***
     384| // Modified code here
     385| //*** AMI PORTING END ***
     386| ```
     387| 
     388| **Applies to:**
     389| - EDK2 packages (`MdePkg`, `MdeModulePkg`, `SecurityPkg`, `CryptoPkg`, `UefiCpuPkg`)
     390| - Intel packages (`Intel/`)
     391| - Any third-party code not owned by AMI
     392| 
     393| ✅ **Correct Usage:**
     394| ```c
     395| // Original Intel/EDK2 code
     396| void OriginalFunction()
     397| {
     398|   // Original implementation
     399|   
     400|   //*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***
     401|   // AMI-specific validation added
     402|   if (!ValidateAmiRequirement()) {
     403|     return EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION;
     404|   }
     405|   //*** AMI PORTING END ***
     406|   
     407|   // Rest of original code
     408| }
     409| ```
     410| 
     411| ❌ **Missing Markers (INFO):**
     412| ```c
     413| // Modified third-party code without markers
     414| void ThirdPartyFunction()
     415| {
     416|   // Original code
     417|   CustomAmiFunction();  // INFO: AMI modification not marked
     418|   // Original code
     419| }
     420| ```
     421| 
     422| ### Marker Placement Rules
     423| 
     424| 1. **Wrap only AMI changes** - Do not include original code inside markers
     425| 2. **One marker pair per change** - Separate distinct modifications
     426| 3. **Add brief description** - Explain why modification was needed
     427| 4. **Keep markers minimal** - Only changed lines, not entire functions
     428| 
     429| ✅ **Good:**
     430| ```c
     431| Status = AllocateBuffer();
     432| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     433|   //*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***
     434|   // Added logging for debug tracking
     435|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "[AMI] Buffer allocation failed: %r\n", Status));
     436|   //*** AMI PORTING END ***
     437|   return Status;
     438| }
     439| ```
     440| 
     441| ### Alternative Marker Names
     442| 
     443| These are also acceptable (project-specific):
     444| - `//*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***` / `//*** AMI PORTING END ***` (preferred)
     445| - `// AMI OVERRIDE START` / `// AMI OVERRIDE END`
     446| - `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` / `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:`
     447| 
     448| **Observation (INFO severity):**
     449| - Modifications to third-party code without porting markers
     450| - Incorrect marker syntax
     451| - Missing explanation in marker comments
     452| - Markers wrapping too much unmodified code
     453| 
     454| ---
     455| 
     456| ## 7. Code Readability
     457| 
     458| ### Line Length
     459| 
     460| **Guideline:** Prefer lines under 120 characters.
     461| 
     462| **Observation:** Lines exceeding 120 characters reduce readability (INFO severity).
     463| 
     464| ✅ **Good:**
     465| ```c
     466| Status = gBS->InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces(
     467|                 &Handle,
     468|                 &gEfiNvramAccessProtocolGuid,
     469|                 &mNvramAccessProtocol,
     470|                 NULL
     471|                 );
     472| ```
     473| 
     474| ⚠️ **Consider Refactoring:**
     475| ```c
     476| Status = gBS->InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces(&Handle, &gEfiNvramAccessProtocolGuid, &mNvramAccessProtocol, &gEfiNvramControlProtocolGuid, &mNvramControlProtocol, NULL);  // INFO: Line too long
     477| ```
     478| 
     479| ### Function Length
     480| 
     481| **Guideline:** Functions should be under 200 lines.
     482| 
     483| **Observation:** Functions exceeding 200 lines suggest refactoring opportunities (INFO severity).
     484| 
     485| **Suggestion:** Break into smaller, focused functions:
     486| - Extract complex logic into helper functions
     487| - Separate initialization from processing
     488| - Create subfunctions for error handling paths
     489| 
     490| ### Nesting Depth
     491| 
     492| **Guideline:** Prefer nesting depth under 5 levels.
     493| 
     494| ⚠️ **Deep Nesting (INFO):**
     495| ```c
     496| if (Condition1) {
     497|   if (Condition2) {
     498|     if (Condition3) {
     499|       if (Condition4) {
     500|         if (Condition5) {
     501|           // INFO: Consider refactoring (5+ levels deep)
     502|         }
     503|       }
     504|     }
     505|   }
     506| }
     507| ```
     508| 
     509| ✅ **Better - Early Returns:**
     510| ```c
     511| if (!Condition1) return EFI_INVALID_PARAMETER;
     512| if (!Condition2) return EFI_NOT_READY;
     513| if (!Condition3) return EFI_DEVICE_ERROR;
     514| if (!Condition4) return EFI_ACCESS_DENIED;
     515| // Actual logic at minimal nesting
     516| ```
     517| 
     518| ### Variable Scope Minimization
     519| 
     520| **Guideline:** Declare variables in the smallest scope possible.
     521| 
     522| ✅ **Good:**
     523| ```c
     524| EFI_STATUS MyFunction(VOID)
     525| {
     526|   EFI_STATUS  Status;
     527|   
     528|   // ... some code ...
     529|   
     530|   {
     531|     UINT32  TempValue;  // Scoped to this block only
     532|     TempValue = CalculateSomething();
     533|     Status = ProcessValue(TempValue);
     534|   }
     535|   
     536|   return Status;
     537| }
     538| ```
     539| 
     540| ---
     541| 
     542| ## 8. Error Handling Patterns
     543| 
     544| ### Consistent Error Handling
     545| 
     546| **Guideline:** Use consistent error handling patterns throughout a module.
     547| 
     548| ✅ **Consistent Pattern:**
     549| ```c
     550| Status = Step1();
     551| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     552|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Step1 failed: %r\n", Status));
     553|   goto Exit;
     554| }
     555| 
     556| Status = Step2();
     557| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     558|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Step2 failed: %r\n", Status));
     559|   goto Exit;
     560| }
     561| 
     562| Exit:
     563|   // Cleanup
     564|   return Status;
     565| ```
     566| 
     567| ### Resource Cleanup
     568| 
     569| **Guideline:** Always clean up resources in error paths.
     570| 
     571| ✅ **Good - Cleanup on Error:**
     572| ```c
     573| Buffer = AllocatePool(Size);
     574| if (Buffer == NULL) {
     575|   return EFI_OUT_OF_RESOURCES;
     576| }
     577| 
     578| Status = ProcessBuffer(Buffer);
     579| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     580|   FreePool(Buffer);  // Cleanup before return
     581|   return Status;
     582| }
     583| 
     584| FreePool(Buffer);
     585| return EFI_SUCCESS;
     586| ```
     587| 
     588| ### Error Message Clarity
     589| 
     590| **Guideline:** Debug messages should clearly identify the error source.
     591| 
     592| ✅ **Good:**
     593| ```c
     594| DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "[NVRAM] Failed to allocate buffer (Size=%d): %r\n", BufferSize, Status));
     595| ```
     596| 
     597| ⚠️ **Needs Improvement:**
     598| ```c
     599| DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Error: %r\n", Status));  // INFO: Add context (module, operation)
     600| ```
     601| 
     602| ---
     603| 
     604| ## 9. Performance Considerations
     605| 
     606| ### Unnecessary Memory Allocations
     607| 
     608| **Guideline:** Avoid allocations in performance-critical paths.
     609| 
     610| ⚠️ **Optimization Opportunity (INFO):**
     611| ```c
     612| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
     613|   Buffer = AllocatePool(Size);  // INFO: Consider allocating once before loop
     614|   ProcessBuffer(Buffer);
     615|   FreePool(Buffer);
     616| }
     617| ```
     618| 
     619| ✅ **Better:**
     620| ```c
     621| Buffer = AllocatePool(Size);
     622| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
     623|   ProcessBuffer(Buffer);
     624| }
     625| FreePool(Buffer);
     626| ```
     627| 
     628| ### Repeated Calculations
     629| 
     630| **Guideline:** Cache calculated values instead of recalculating.
     631| 
     632| ⚠️ **Repeated Calculation (INFO):**
     633| ```c
     634| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
     635|   Process(Data[Index] * sizeof(ELEMENT_TYPE));  // INFO: Calculate once
     636| }
     637| ```
     638| 
     639| ✅ **Better:**
     640| ```c
     641| ElementSize = sizeof(ELEMENT_TYPE);
     642| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
     643|   Process(Data[Index] * ElementSize);
     644| }
     645| ```
     646| 
     647| ### String Operations in Loops
     648| 
     649| **Guideline:** Minimize string operations in tight loops.
     650| 
     651| ⚠️ **Performance Note (INFO):**
     652| ```c
     653| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
     654|   StrCpy(Buffer, L"Prefix");  // INFO: Move string ops outside loop if possible
     655|   StrCat(Buffer, Names[Index]);
     656|   ProcessString(Buffer);
     657| }
     658| ```
     659| 
     660| ---
     661| 
     662| ## 10. Maintainability
     663| 
     664| ### Code Duplication
     665| 
     666| **Guideline:** Extract common code into reusable functions.
     667| 
     668| ⚠️ **Duplication Detected (INFO):**
     669| ```c
     670| // In Function1:
     671| Status = ValidateInput(Input);
     672| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     673|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Invalid input: %r\n", Status));
     674|   return Status;
     675| }
     676| 
     677| // In Function2:
     678| Status = ValidateInput(Input);  // INFO: Extract to helper function
     679| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
     680|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Invalid input: %r\n", Status));
     681|   return Status;
     682| }
     683| ```
     684| 
     685| ### Magic Numbers
     686| 
     687| **Guideline:** Replace magic numbers with named constants.
     688| 
     689| ⚠️ **Magic Number (INFO):**
     690| ```c
     691| if (Size > 0x10000) {  // INFO: Use named constant
     692|   return EFI_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL;
     693| }
     694| ```
     695| 
     696| ✅ **Better:**
     697| ```c
     698| #define MAX_BUFFER_SIZE  0x10000  // 64 KB
     699| 
     700| if (Size > MAX_BUFFER_SIZE) {
     701|   return EFI_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL;
     702| }
     703| ```
     704| 
     705| ### Complex Boolean Expressions
     706| 
     707| **Guideline:** Simplify complex conditions for clarity.
     708| 
     709| ⚠️ **Complex Expression (INFO):**
     710| ```c
     711| if ((Flags & FLAG_A) && (Flags & FLAG_B) && !(Flags & FLAG_C) && 
     712|     (Status == EFI_SUCCESS || Status == EFI_WARN_RESET_REQUIRED)) {
     713|   // INFO: Consider extracting to named boolean or helper function
     714| }
     715| ```
     716| 
     717| ✅ **Better:**
     718| ```c
     719| BOOLEAN IsValidState(UINT32 Flags, EFI_STATUS Status)
     720| {
     721|   if (!(Flags & FLAG_A) || !(Flags & FLAG_B)) return FALSE;
     722|   if (Flags & FLAG_C) return FALSE;
     723|   if (Status != EFI_SUCCESS && Status != EFI_WARN_RESET_REQUIRED) return FALSE;
     724|   return TRUE;
     725| }
     726| 
     727| if (IsValidState(Flags, Status)) {
     728|   // Clear logic
     729| }
     730| ```
     731| 
     732| ---
     733| 
     734| ## 11. Firmware-Specific Patterns
     735| 
     736| ### Boot Path Optimization
     737| 
     738| **Guideline:** Optimize boot path for minimal delay.
     739| 
     740| **Observations (INFO):**
     741| - Defer non-critical initialization to later boot phases
     742| - Minimize PEI phase memory allocations
     743| - Avoid unnecessary protocol installs in early boot
     744| - Cache frequently accessed data
     745| 
     746| **Example:**
     747| ```c
     748| // INFO: Consider deferring feature init to BDS phase
     749| if (IsBootPhase(DXE_CORE)) {
     750|   // Defer non-critical features
     751|   RegisterProtocolNotify(&gEfiBdsArchProtocolGuid, DeferredInit);
     752| } else {
     753|   // Critical path only
     754|   InitializeCriticalHardware();
     755| }
     756| ```
     757| 
     758| ### Memory Footprint
     759| 
     760| **Guideline:** Minimize memory usage, especially in PEI.
     761| 
     762| **Observations (INFO):**
     763| - Large static arrays suggest pool allocation
     764| - Temporary buffers should be freed promptly
     765| - Consider pool vs page allocation based on size
     766| - PEI HOBs should be minimal
     767| 
     768| **Example:**
     769| ```c
     770| // INFO: Large array increases footprint
     771| STATIC UINT8 mLargeBuffer[64 * 1024];  // 64KB static allocation
     772| 
     773| // Consider:
     774| UINT8  *Buffer = AllocatePool(RequiredSize);  // Allocate only what's needed
     775| ```
     776| 
     777| ### S3 Resume Path Safety
     778| 
     779| **Guideline:** Ensure S3 resume path uses only S3-safe resources.
     780| 
     781| **Observations (INFO):**
     782| - No runtime variable access in S3 path (use boot script)
     783| - No protocol dependencies in S3 resume
     784| - Hardware state must be restored from saved boot script
     785| - SMM handlers must be S3-aware
     786| 
     787| **Example:**
     788| ```c
     789| // INFO: Variable access not S3-safe
     790| if (IsS3Resume()) {
     791|   // Use boot script data, not fresh variable reads
     792|   RestoreFromBootScript();
     793| } else {
     794|   GetVariable(L"Config", ...);
     795| }
     796| ```
     797| 
     798| ---
     799| 
     800| ## 12. Additional Style Recommendations
     801| 
     802| ### Whitespace
     803| 
     804| **Guideline:** Consistent whitespace improves readability.
     805| 
     806| - Blank line between function definitions
     807| - Blank line between logical code blocks
     808| - Space after keywords: `if (`, `while (`, `for (`
     809| - No trailing whitespace
     810| 
     811| ### Bracket Style
     812| 
     813| **Guideline:** Follow consistent bracket placement.
     814| 
     815| ✅ **Preferred (UEFI Style):**
     816| ```c
     817| if (Condition)
     818| {
     819|   // Code
     820| }
     821| ```
     822| 
     823| **Acceptable:**
     824| ```c
     825| if (Condition) {
     826|   // Code
     827| }
     828| ```
     829| 
     830| **Choose one and be consistent within a file.**
     831| 
     832| ### Typedef vs Struct
     833| 
     834| **Guideline:** Use typedefs for complex structures.
     835| 
     836| ✅ **Good:**
     837| ```c
     838| typedef struct {
     839|   UINT32  Field1;
     840|   UINT16  Field2;
     841| } MY_STRUCTURE;
     842| 
     843| MY_STRUCTURE  MyVar;  // Clean usage
     844| ```
     845| 
     846| ---
     847| 
     848| ## Validation Checklist (All INFO Severity)
     849| 
     850| When reviewing for Firmware Coding Standards:
     851| 
     852| ☐ **Comments**
     853|   - [ ] Function headers complete with @param/@retval
     854|   - [ ] Complex logic has explanatory comments
     855|   - [ ] TODO/FIXME properly marked
     856| 
     857| ☐ **File Structure**
     858|   - [ ] C files follow correct order (header → includes → constants → variables → functions)
     859|   - [ ] Function declarations before definitions
     860|   - [ ] No mixed ordering of sections
     861|   - [ ] Related declarations grouped together
     862| 
     863| ☐ **Header Files**
     864|   - [ ] No code implementations in headers (declarations only)
     865|   - [ ] Include guards present and correctly named
     866|   - [ ] Headers are self-contained
     867|   - [ ] Unnecessary includes minimized
     868| 
     869| ☐ **Line Formatting**
     870|   - [ ] One statement per line
     871|   - [ ] Consistent operator spacing
     872|   - [ ] One variable per line (exceptions for related simple vars)
     873|   - [ ] No trailing whitespace
     874| 
     875| ☐ **Naming Quality**
     876|   - [ ] Names are meaningful and descriptive
     877|   - [ ] No ambiguous names (Size1, Var2, etc.)
     878|   - [ ] No case-only name differences
     879|   - [ ] Abbreviations are clear and consistent
     880| 
     881| ☐ **Porting Markers**
     882|   - [ ] AMI modifications to third-party code wrapped with `//*** AMI PORTING BEGIN/END ***`
     883|   - [ ] Markers include brief explanation
     884|   - [ ] Only changed code inside markers
     885|   - [ ] Correct syntax used
     886| 
     887| ☐ **Readability**
     888|   - [ ] Lines under 120 characters (where reasonable)
     889|   - [ ] Functions under 200 lines
     890|   - [ ] Nesting depth under 5 levels
     891|   - [ ] Variables scoped minimally
     892| 
     893| ☐ **Error Handling**
     894|   - [ ] Consistent error handling pattern
     895|   - [ ] All error paths clean up resources
     896|   - [ ] Error messages are clear and specific
     897| 
     898| ☐ **Performance**
     899|   - [ ] No unnecessary allocations in loops
     900|   - [ ] Calculations cached when repeated
     901|   - [ ] String operations minimized in tight loops
     902| 
     903| ☐ **Maintainability**
     904|   - [ ] No significant code duplication
     905|   - [ ] Magic numbers replaced with constants
     906|   - [ ] Complex expressions simplified
     907| 
     908| ☐ **Firmware-Specific**
     909|   - [ ] Boot path optimized (deferred initialization)
     910|   - [ ] Memory footprint considered
     911|   - [ ] S3 resume path uses safe resources only
     912| 
     913| ---
     914| 
     915| ## Reference
     916|

     921| - Module Developer's Guide (MANDATORY) - `.github/skills/ami-module-developer-standards/`
     922| - UEFI/PI/EDK2 specs - covered in main review stages
     923| 
     924| This document covers **firmware-specific style recommendations only** - all findings are informational and do not impact review verdict.

       8| # AMI Firmware Coding Standards Validation (INFORMATIONAL)
       9| 
      10| ## Purpose
      11| Validate reviewed code against **informational** AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards that provide additional style guidance beyond Module Developer's Guide requirements.
      12| 
      13| **Compliance Level:** INFORMATIONAL - Violations do NOT impact the final review verdict. Findings are tracked for post-merge cleanup.
      14| 
      15| ## When to Use
      16| - **After Module Developer's Guide validation** (Step 3.8 complete)
      17| - To check firmware-specific style and best practices
      18| - Only on files that were already reviewed and passed Module Developer Guide validation
      19| 
      20| ## Scope
      21| This skill checks **firmware-specific style recommendations** only. UEFI/PI/EDK2 spec compliance and Module Developer Guide mandatory standards are already covered.
      22| 
      23| ## Standards Reference
      24| See [Firmware Coding Standards](./Firmware_Coding_Standards.md) for complete firmware-specific style recommendations.
      25| 
      26| ## Validation Procedure
      27| 
      28| ### Step 1: Identify Files in Scope
      29| Review only files that were checked in Module Developer Guide validation (Step 3.8). Do not review files that were skipped or excluded.
      30| 
      31| ### Step 2: Classify File Ownership (AMI vs Third-Party)
      32| 
      33| Use the same classification logic as Module Developer Guide:
      34| 
      35| **Classification Rules:**
      36| 
      37| | Condition | Classification | Validation Scope |
      38| |-----------|----------------|------------------|
      39| | File contains AMI copyright banner (`Copyright (c) 1985-20XX, AMI`) | ✅ **AMI-owned** | Entire file |
      40| | File contains third-party copyright AND contains AMI override markers | ⚙️ **Third-party with AMI overrides** | AMI override sections only |
      41| | File contains NO copyright header | ✅ **AMI-owned** (default assumption) | Entire file |
      42| | File contains only third-party copyright, NO AMI override markers | ❌ **Third-party only** | Skip validation |
      43| 
      44| **AMI Override Marker Patterns (case-insensitive):**
      45| 
      46| | Start Marker | End Marker |
      47| |--------------|------------|
      48| | `// AMI OVERRIDE START` | `// AMI OVERRIDE END` |
      49| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:` |
      50| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_END:` |
      51| 
      52| ### Step 3: Extract AMI Code Sections
      53| 
      54| Same extraction logic as Module Developer Guide validation (entire file vs override sections only).
      55| 
      56| ### Step 4: Check Firmware Coding Standards
      57| 
      58| For each AMI code section, verify firmware-specific style recommendations:
      59| 
      60| 1. **Comment Style** (INFORMATIONAL)
      61|    - Function header comments completeness
      62|    - Code comment clarity and usefulness
      63|    - TODO/FIXME/HACK marker usage
      64| 
      65| 2. **Code Readability** (INFORMATIONAL)
      66|    - Line length recommendations (prefer < 120 characters)
      67|    - Function length guidelines (prefer < 200 lines)
      68|    - Nesting depth (prefer < 5 levels)
      69|    - Variable scope minimization
      70| 
      71| 3. **Error Handling Patterns** (INFORMATIONAL)
      72|    - Consistent error handling style
      73|    - Resource cleanup in error paths
      74|    - Error message clarity
      75| 
      76| 4. **Performance Considerations** (INFORMATIONAL)
      77|    - Unnecessary memory allocations
      78|    - Repeated calculations that could be cached
      79|    - String operations in loops
      80| 
      81| 5. **Maintainability** (INFORMATIONAL)
      82|    - Code duplication detection
      83|    - Magic number usage (prefer named constants)
      84|    - Complex boolean expressions (suggest simplification)
      85| 
      86| 6. **Firmware-Specific Patterns** (INFORMATIONAL)
      87|    - Boot path optimization hints
      88|    - Memory footprint considerations
      89|    - S3 resume path safety
      90| 
      91| ### Step 5: Report Findings
      92| 
      93| Append findings to the review report under a new section:
      94| 
      95| ```markdown
      96| ---
      97| 
      98| ## AMI Firmware Coding Standards (INFORMATIONAL)
      99| 
     100| Files validated against AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards style recommendations.
     101| 
     102| ### Summary
     103| - Files reviewed for style: X
     104| - Informational observations: Y
     105| 
     106| ### Findings
     107| 
     108| **[AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c]** (AMI-owned)
     109| - Comment Style: Function header missing parameter description
     110|   - Location: Line 147
     111|   - Severity: INFO (style recommendation)
     112|   - Suggestion: Add `@param` Doxygen tags for parameters
     113| - Code Readability: Function exceeds recommended length (215 lines)
     114|   - Location: Lines 200-415
     115|   - Severity: INFO (maintainability)
     116|   - Suggestion: Consider refactoring into smaller functions
     117| 
     118| **[Intel/ClientOneSiliconPkg/Cpu/PeiCpuPolicyLib.c]** (Third-party with AMI overrides)
     119| - [Third-party file — reviewing AMI override sections only: lines 245-260]
     120| - Performance: Repeated calculation in loop
     121|   - Location: Line 252 (inside `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` block)
     122|   - Severity: INFO (optimization opportunity)
     123|   - Suggestion: Calculate `BufferSize = sizeof(BUFFER) * Count` once before loop
     124| 
     125| ### Firmware Coding Standards Verdict
     126| ✅ **STYLE COMPLIANT** / ℹ️ **STYLE OBSERVATIONS** (informational only)
     127| 
     128| > **Note:** Firmware Coding Standards violations are **INFORMATIONAL ONLY** and do not affect the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK). Findings are tracked for post-merge cleanup or next revision.
     129| ```
     130| 
     131| **Reporting Guidelines:**
     132| 
     133| 1. **All findings are INFO severity** - no MAJOR/MINOR/CRITICAL
     134| 2. **Use "Suggestion:" instead of "Fix:"** - these are recommendations, not requirements
     135| 3. **Group by file classification** (same as Module Developer Guide)
     136| 4. **For AMI override sections:** Include line range notation
     137| 5. **If no findings:** Write: `> All reviewed files follow firmware coding style recommendations.`
     138| 
     139| ### Step 6: Final Notes
     140| - **Does NOT impact** the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK)
     141| - Firmware Coding Standards violations are **INFORMATIONAL ONLY**
     142| - Findings are suggestions for code quality improvement
     143| - **AMI code only:** Only AMI-owned files and AMI override sections validated
     144| - **Third-party code skipped:** Pure third-party code not validated
     145| - **Post-merge tracking:** All findings go to post-merge improvement backlog
     146| 
     147| ## Severity Levels
     148| 
     149| All Firmware Coding Standards findings use **INFO** severity only:
     150| 
     151| | Category | Severity | Examples |
     152| |----------|----------|----------|
     153| | Comment Style | INFO | Missing parameter docs, unclear comments |
     154| | Code Readability | INFO | Long functions, deep nesting, long lines |
     155| | Error Handling | INFO | Inconsistent error handling patterns |
     156| | Performance | INFO | Optimization opportunities, repeated calculations |
     157| | Maintainability | INFO | Code duplication, magic numbers, complex expressions |
     158| | Firmware-Specific | INFO | Boot path optimization, memory footprint hints |
     159| 
     160| **No findings impact verdict or score.**

       1| # AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide Standards (MANDATORY)
       2| 
       3| **Source:** AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide  
       4| **Compliance Level:** MANDATORY - Violations impact review verdict  
       5| **Scope:** AMI-specific organization standards extending UEFI 2.10+, PI 1.8+, EDK II C Coding Standard 2.2+
       6| 
       7| ---
       8| 
       9| ## Violation Severity Levels
      10| 
      11| Module Developer Guide violations are classified by severity and impact the final review verdict:
      12| 
      13| | Severity | Impact on Verdict | Examples |
      14| |----------|-------------------|----------|
      15| | **MANDATORY-MAJOR** | Added to main review MAJOR count; may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK | Raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`), missing AMI OVERRIDE markers in Intel/EDK2 files, wrong customization layer |
      16| | **MANDATORY-MINOR** | Added to main review MINOR count; impacts quality score | Copyright year outdated, function naming case incorrect, section order wrong, include order violations |
      17| | **INFO** | Informational only; no verdict impact | Suggestions for improvement, optional enhancements |
      18| 
      19| **Verdict Impact Rules:**
      20| - MANDATORY-MAJOR findings are treated as MAJOR findings in the main review
      21| - MANDATORY-MINOR findings are treated as MINOR findings in the main review
      22| - Verdict is recalculated after Module Developer Guide validation
      23| - If new findings push verdict from APPROVE → CONDITIONAL or BLOCK, `Result.txt` is updated
      24| 
      25| ---
      26| 
      27| ## Validation Scope: AMI Code Only (MANDATORY Compliance)
      28| 
      29| These standards are **MANDATORY** for AMI-owned code. Violations impact the final review verdict.
      30| 
      31| ### File Classification
      32| 
      33| | File Type | How to Identify | Validation Scope |
      34| |-----------|----------------|------------------|
      35| | **AMI-owned** | Contains `Copyright (c) 1985-YYYY, AMI` | **Entire file** |
      36| | **Third-party with AMI overrides** | Third-party copyright + AMI override markers | **AMI override sections only** |
      37| | **No copyright** | No copyright header found | **Entire file** (default to AMI) |
      38| | **Third-party only** | Third-party copyright, no AMI markers | **Skip validation** |
      39| 
      40| ### AMI Override Markers in Third-Party Files
      41| 
      42| When AMI modifies third-party files (Intel, EDK2, etc.), changes are wrapped in override markers. **Only code between these markers is validated** against AMI organization standards.
      43| 
      44| **Standard Marker Patterns:**
      45| 
      46| | Start Marker | End Marker | Usage |
      47| |--------------|------------|-------|
      48| | `// AMI OVERRIDE START` | `// AMI OVERRIDE END` | General EDK2/Intel file modifications |
      49| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:` | Server platform overrides |
      50| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_END:` | Server platform RC overrides |
      51| 
      52| **Pattern Recognition (case-insensitive):**
      53| ```regex
      54| Start: ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*START
      55| End:   ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*END
      56| ```
      57| 
      58| **Example: Third-Party File with AMI Overrides**
      59| 
      60| ```c
      61| /** @file
      62|   Copyright (c) 2020 - 2024, Intel Corporation. All rights reserved.
      63|   SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause-Patent
      64| **/
      65| 
      66| #include <Uefi.h>
      67| 
      68| // ⬇️ Third-party code - NOT validated against AMI standards
      69| void IntelFunction() {
      70|   int bufferSize = 100;  // Raw C types OK in Intel code
      71| }
      72| 
      73| // ⬇️ AMI override section - VALIDATED against AMI standards
      74| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add custom validation
      75| BOOLEAN gAmiValidationEnabled = TRUE;  // Must use UEFI types + 'g' prefix
      76| 
      77| EFI_STATUS AmiCustomValidation(VOID) {  // Must use PascalCase
      78|   return EFI_SUCCESS;
      79| }
      80| // AMI OVERRIDE END
      81| // ⬆️ End of AMI code
      82| 
      83| // ⬇️ Third-party code resumes - NOT validated
      84| void AnotherIntelFunction() {
      85|   // Intel code continues
      86| }
      87| ```
      88| 
      89| **In this example:**
      90| - Lines outside override markers: **Not validated** (Intel code)
      91| - Lines between `// AMI OVERRIDE START` and `// AMI OVERRIDE END`: **Validated** against all applicable AMI standards (naming, types, patterns)
      92| - File header (copyright): **Not validated** (remains Intel copyright)
      93| 
      94| ---
      95| 
      96| ## 1. File Header Format
      97| 
      98| **Applies to:** AMI-owned files only. Third-party files with AMI overrides keep their original header.
      99| 
     100| Every AMI source file (.c, .h) must use the AMI copyright banner followed by a Doxygen `@file` block:
     101| 
     102| ```c
     103| //***********************************************************************
     104| //*                                                                     *
     105| //*                 Copyright (c) 1985-20XX, AMI.                       *
     106| //*                                                                     *
     107| //*      All rights reserved. Subject to AMI licensing agreement.       *
     108| //*                                                                     *
     109| //***********************************************************************
     110| 
     111| /** @file ModuleName.c
     112|     Brief description of the file's purpose
     113| **/
     114| ```
     115| 
     116| ### Requirements
     117| - Copyright year must include current year (20XX updated annually) - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if outdated
     118| - `@file` block must have file name matching actual file name - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if missing or incorrect
     119| - Brief description must be present and meaningful (not "Module file") - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if generic
     120| 
     121| ---
     122| 
     123| ## 2. Source File Organization
     124| 
     125| Files are organized with `//------` separator lines into sections in this **strict order**:
     126| 
     127| ### Section Order (Mandatory)
     128| 
     129| 1. **Include(s)**
     130| 2. **Constant, Macro and Type Definition(s)**
     131|    - Sub-groups: Constant, Macro, Type, Function Prototype
     132| 3. **Variable and External Declaration(s)**
     133|    - Sub-groups: Variable, GUID, Protocol/PPI, External, Function definitions
     134| 
     135| ### Example Structure
     136| 
     137| ```c
     138| //***********************************************************************
     139| // Copyright & @file block
     140| //***********************************************************************
     141| 
     142| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     143| // Include(s)
     144| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     145| #include <Uefi.h>
     146| #include <Token.h>
     147| #include <AmiDxeLib.h>
     148| 
     149| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     150| // Constant, Macro and Type Definition(s)
     151| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     152| 
     153| // Constant Definition(s)
     154| #define MAX_BUFFER_SIZE  256
     155| 
     156| // Macro Definition(s)
     157| #define GET_ELEMENT(x)  ((x) & 0xFF)
     158| 
     159| // Type Definition(s)
     160| typedef struct {
     161|   UINT32  Field1;
     162|   UINT16  Field2;
     163| } MY_STRUCTURE;
     164| 
     165| // Function Prototype(s)
     166| EFI_STATUS InitializeModule(VOID);
     167| 
     168| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     169| // Variable and External Declaration(s)
     170| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     171| 
     172| // Variable Declaration(s)
     173| STATIC UINT8  mLocalBuffer[MAX_BUFFER_SIZE];
     174| 
     175| // GUID Definition(s)
     176| EFI_GUID gMyProtocolGuid = MY_PROTOCOL_GUID;
     177| 
     178| // Protocol/PPI Declaration(s)
     179| MY_PROTOCOL  *gMyProtocol = NULL;
     180| 
     181| // External Declaration(s)
     182| extern UINT32  gExternalValue;
     183| 
     184| // Function Definition(s)
     185| EFI_STATUS
     186| InitializeModule (
     187|   VOID
     188|   )
     189| {
     190|   // Implementation
     191| }
     192| ```
     193| 
     194| ---
     195| 
     196| ## 3. Naming Conventions
     197| 
     198| ### Raw C Types (MANDATORY-MAJOR)
     199| 
     200| **NEVER use raw C types** — they cause ABI incompatibility, platform-dependent sizes, and runtime failures.
     201| 
     202| | ❌ Prohibited (Raw C) | ✅ Required (UEFI) | Reason |
     203| |---------------------|-------------------|---------|
     204| | `int` | `UINT32` or `INT32` | Size varies (16/32 bits) |
     205| | `unsigned` | `UINT32` or `UINTN` | Platform-dependent |
     206| | `char` | `CHAR8` | Sign varies by compiler |
     207| | `void` | `VOID` | UEFI convention |
     208| | `long` | `UINT64` or `INT64` | Size varies (32/64 bits) |
     209| | `short` | `UINT16` or `INT16` | Not portable |
     210| 
     211| **Examples of MANDATORY-MAJOR violations:**
     212| ```c
     213| // ❌ WRONG - Raw C types
     214| int bufferSize = 100;
     215| char name[64];
     216| unsigned index;
     217| void *ptr;
     218| extern int VfrBin[];
     219| 
     220| // ✅ CORRECT - UEFI types
     221| UINT32 BufferSize = 100;
     222| CHAR8 Name[64];
     223| UINTN Index;
     224| VOID *Ptr;
     225| extern UINT8 VfrBin[];
     226| ```
     227| 
     228| **Severity:** **MANDATORY-MAJOR** — Treated as MAJOR finding in main review; may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK verdict.
     229| 
     230| ---
     231| 
     232| ### Functions
     233| 
     234| | Pattern | Convention | Example |
     235| |---------|-----------|---------|
     236| | General functions | PascalCase | `InitializeDriver`, `ProcessBuffer` |
     237| | Entry points | `<Module>Init` or `<Module>EntryPoint` | `CrbDxeInit`, `SmbiosPeiInit` |
     238| | Static/local functions | PascalCase (prefix optional) | `ValidateInput`, `LocalHelper` |
     239| 
     240| ### Variables
     241| 
     242| | Pattern | Convention | Example |
     243| |---------|-----------|---------|
     244| | Global variables | `g` prefix + PascalCase | `gBootScript`, `gAmiCrbInfoPpi` |
     245| | Registration variables | `g<Name>Reg` | `gAmiConOutStartedProtocolReg` |
     246| | Module-scope static | `m` prefix + PascalCase | `mLocalBuffer`, `mInitialized` |
     247| | Local variables | camelCase or PascalCase | `BufferSize`, `status` |
     248| 
     249| ### Constants and Macros
     250| 
     251| - ALL_CAPS with underscores: `MAX_BUFFER_SIZE`, `ENABLE_DEBUG_MODE`
     252| - Token names in SDL: ALL_CAPS with underscores: `DEBUG_MODE`, `CRB_BOARD`
     253| 
     254| ### Types
     255| 
     256| - UEFI types: `EFI_STATUS`, `VOID`, `UINT8`, `UINT16`, `UINT32`, `UINT64`, `UINTN`, `BOOLEAN`, `EFI_HANDLE`
     257| - Custom types: PascalCase with all-caps suffix: `MY_PROTOCOL`, `SETUP_DATA`, `GPIO_CONFIG`
     258| - Pointer types: use UEFI style: `EFI_HANDLE *Handle` (not `EFI_HANDLE* Handle`)
     259| 
     260| ---
     261| 
     262| ## 4. Include Order
     263| 
     264| Includes must follow this order:
     265| 
     266| ```c
     267| #include <Uefi.h>       // or <Pei.h> for PEI modules - FIRST
     268| #include <Token.h>       // auto-generated SDL token definitions
     269| #include <AmiDxeLib.h>   // AMI DXE library (or <AmiPeiLib.h> for PEI)
     270| #include <Library/PcdLib.h>       // EDK2 standard libraries
     271| #include <Library/BaseMemoryLib.h>
     272| #include <Protocol/SomeProtocol.h> // Protocol / PPI headers
     273| #include <SetupVariable.h>         // Platform-specific headers
     274| ```
     275| 
     276| ### Rules
     277| 1. **Phase header first:** `<Uefi.h>` or `<Pei.h>` or `<Base.h>`
     278| 2. **AMI headers next:** `<Token.h>`, `<AmiDxeLib.h>` / `<AmiPeiLib.h>`
     279| 3. **EDK2 libraries:** `<Library/...>`
     280| 4. **Protocol/PPI/GUID headers:** `<Protocol/...>`, `<Ppi/...>`, `<Guid/...>`
     281| 5. **Platform headers last:** Setup, board-specific
     282| 
     283| ---
     284| 
     285| ## 5. INF File Structure
     286| 
     287| INF file sections must appear in this order:
     288| 
     289| ```ini
     290| [Defines]
     291|   INF_VERSION                    = 0x00010005
     292|   BASE_NAME                      = ModuleName
     293|   FILE_GUID                      = XXXXXXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXXXXXXXXXX
     294|   MODULE_TYPE                    = DXE_DRIVER
     295|   VERSION_STRING                 = 1.0
     296|   ENTRY_POINT                    = ModuleInit
     297| 
     298| [Sources]
     299|   ModuleFile.c
     300|   ModuleHeader.h
     301| 
     302| [Packages]
     303|   MdePkg/MdePkg.dec              # MUST be first
     304|   AmiModulePkg/AmiModulePkg.dec
     305|   AmiChipsetModulePkg/AmiChipsetModulePkg.dec
     306| 
     307| [LibraryClasses]
     308|   UefiDriverEntryPoint           # Entry point library
     309|   UefiBootServicesTableLib
     310|   BaseMemoryLib
     311| 
     312| [Protocols]
     313|   gEfiSomeProtocolGuid           # PRODUCED
     314|   gEfiOtherProtocolGuid          # CONSUMED
     315| 
     316| [Guids]
     317|   gEfiEventGroupGuid
     318| 
     319| [Pcd]
     320|   gEfiMdePkgTokenSpaceGuid.PcdSomeValue
     321| 
     322| [Depex]
     323|   gEfiSomeProtocolGuid AND gEfiOtherProtocolGuid
     324| ```
     325| 
     326| ### Requirements
     327| - `MdePkg/MdePkg.dec` must be **first** in `[Packages]` - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if not first
     328| - Entry-point library must be **first** in `[LibraryClasses]` - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if not first
     329| - Section order must match above - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if out of order
     330| - Comment PRODUCED vs CONSUMED for protocols/PPIs when not obvious
     331| 
     332| ---
     333| 
     334| ## 6. AMI Override Marker Policy
     335| 
     336| `// AMI OVERRIDE START/END` markers are **required only** when modifying EDK2 or Intel-owned files. They are **never** used in AMI-owned packages.
     337| 
     338| ### When Markers are Required
     339| 
     340| | Package path | Markers required? | Violation if missing |
     341| |---|---|---|
     342| | `Intel/` (any Intel package) | ✅ **YES** | **MANDATORY-MAJOR** |
     343| | `MdePkg/`, `MdeModulePkg/`, `SecurityPkg/`, `CryptoPkg/`, `UefiCpuPkg/` | ✅ **YES** | **MANDATORY-MAJOR** |
     344| | `Ami*Pkg/`, `CrbPkg/`, `AmiCrbPkg/`, vendor packages | ❌ **NO** | N/A |
     345| 
     346| ### Syntax (EDK2 / Intel files only)
     347| 
     348| ```c
     349| // AMI OVERRIDE START — <brief description> (<YYYY-MM-DD>)
     350| ... changed code ...
     351| // AMI OVERRIDE END
     352| ```
     353| 
     354| **Example:**
     355| ```c
     356| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add SMRAM boundary validation (2026-05-15)
     357| if (Address < mSmramBase || Address > mSmramEnd) {
     358|   return EFI_ACCESS_DENIED;
     359| }
     360| // AMI OVERRIDE END
     361| ```
     362| 
     363| ---
     364| 
     365| ## 7. SDL Token Patterns
     366| 
     367| ### Token Declaration
     368| 
     369| ```sdl
     370| TOKEN
     371|     Name  = "MY_FEATURE_ENABLE"
     372|     Value  = "1"
     373|     Help  = "Enable my feature support"
     374|     TokenType = Boolean
     375|     TargetMAK = Yes
     376|     TargetH = Yes
     377| End
     378| ```
     379| 
     380| ### Token Override (Higher Layer)
     381| 
     382| ```sdl
     383| TOKEN
     384|     Name  = "MY_FEATURE_ENABLE"
     385|     Value  = "0"
     386|     TokenType = Boolean
     387|     TargetMAK = Yes
     388|     TargetH = Yes
     389| End
     390| ```
     391| 
     392| ### PCD Mapping
     393| 
     394| ```sdl
     395| PcdMapping
     396|     Name  = "PcdMyValue"
     397|     GuidSpace  = "gMyTokenSpaceGuid"
     398|     PcdType  = "PcdsFixedAtBuild"
     399|     DataType  = "Uint32"
     400|     Value  = "$(MY_TOKEN_VALUE)"
     401|     TargetDSC = Yes
     402| End
     403| ```
     404| 
     405| ---
     406| 
     407| ## 8. ELINK Patterns
     408| 
     409| ### ELINK Chain Root (Owning Module)
     410| 
     411| ```sdl
     412| ELINK
     413|     Name  = "MyHandlerList"
     414|     InvokeOrder = ReplaceParent
     415| End
     416| ```
     417| 
     418| ### ELINK Registration (Contributor)
     419| 
     420| ```sdl
     421| ELINK
     422|     Name  = "MyHookFunction,"          # Note trailing comma
     423|     Parent  = "MyHandlerList"
     424|     InvokeOrder = AfterParent
     425| End
     426| ```
     427| 
     428| ### Function Typedef (Owning Module Header)
     429| 
     430| ```c
     431| typedef VOID (MY_HANDLER_HOOK) (UINT8 Data, UINT64 Parameter);
     432| ```
     433| 
     434| ### Consumption (Owning Module .c)
     435| 
     436| ```c
     437| #include <MyModuleELinks.h>
     438| 
     439| extern MY_HANDLER_HOOK MY_HANDLER_LIST EndOfList;
     440| MY_HANDLER_HOOK* MyHandlers[] = {MY_HANDLER_LIST NULL};
     441| ```
     442| 
     443| ---
     444| 
     445| ## 9. Customization Layers
     446| 
     447| Changes should target the **outermost applicable layer**:
     448| 
     449| ```
     450| ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     451| │  <project>.sdl           (project overrides)    │
     452| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     453| │  <VendorPkg>/            (vendor/OEM custom)    │
     454| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     455| │  CrbPkg/ or AmiCrbPkg/   (board reference)      │
     456| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     457| │  AmiModulePkg/           (AMI core - modify OK) │
     458| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     459| │  Intel/                  (use AMI OVERRIDE)     │
     460| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
     461| │  MdePkg / MdeModulePkg   (use AMI OVERRIDE)     │
     462| └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
     463| ```
     464| 
     465| ### Rules
     466| - Prefer SDL token overrides over code patches
     467| - Use ELINK hooks instead of modifying original module source
     468| - Vendor package customizations override CRB package
     469| - Never modify `Build/` or `Conf/` files (auto-generated)
     470| 
     471| ---
     472| 
     473| ## 10. Module Type Specific Patterns
     474| 
     475| ### PEI Module Entry Point
     476| 
     477| ```c
     478| EFI_STATUS
     479| EFIAPI
     480| MyPeiInit (
     481|   IN       EFI_PEI_FILE_HANDLE  FileHandle,
     482|   IN CONST EFI_PEI_SERVICES     **PeiServices
     483|   )
     484| ```
     485| 
     486| ### DXE Driver Entry Point
     487| 
     488| ```c
     489| EFI_STATUS
     490| EFIAPI
     491| MyDxeInit (
     492|   IN EFI_HANDLE        ImageHandle,
     493|   IN EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE  *SystemTable
     494|   )
     495| ```
     496| 
     497| ### SMM Driver Entry Point
     498| 
     499| ```c
     500| EFI_STATUS
     501| EFIAPI
     502| MySmmInit (
     503|   IN EFI_HANDLE        ImageHandle,
     504|   IN EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE  *SystemTable
     505|   )
     506| ```
     507| 
     508| ---
     509| 
     510| ## 11. Common AMI-Specific Patterns
     511| 
     512| ### Setup Variable Access
     513| 
     514| ```c
     515| #include <SetupVariable.h>
     516| 
     517| SETUP_DATA  SetupData;
     518| UINTN       VariableSize = sizeof(SETUP_DATA);
     519| 
     520| Status = pRS->GetVariable(
     521|            L"Setup",
     522|            &gSetupGuid,
     523|            NULL,
     524|            &VariableSize,
     525|            &SetupData
     526|            );
     527| ```
     528| 
     529| ### Boot Script Saving
     530| 
     531| ```c
     532| #include <AmiCspLib.h>
     533| 
     534| BOOT_SCRIPT_S3_IO_WRITE_MACRO(
     535|   gBootScript,
     536|   EfiBootScriptWidthUint32,
     537|   IoAddress,
     538|   1,
     539|   &Value
     540|   );
     541| ```
     542| 
     543| ### NVRAM Read/Write
     544| 
     545| ```c
     546| #include <AmiDxeLib.h>
     547| 
     548| Status = pRS->SetVariable(
     549|            L"MyVariable",
     550|            &gMyVariableGuid,
     551|            EFI_VARIABLE_NON_VOLATILE | EFI_VARIABLE_BOOTSERVICE_ACCESS,
     552|            sizeof(MyData),
     553|            &MyData
     554|            );
     555| ```
     556| 
     557| ---
     558| 
     559| ## Validation Checklist
     560| 
     561| When reviewing code for AMI organization standards compliance:
     562| 
     563| ☐ **File Header**
     564|   - [ ] AMI copyright banner present
     565|   - [ ] Current year in copyright
     566|   - [ ] `@file` block with correct filename and description
     567| 
     568| ☐ **File Organization**
     569|   - [ ] Sections in correct order (Include, Constant/Macro/Type, Variable/External)
     570|   - [ ] `//------` separators present
     571|   - [ ] Include order follows AMI pattern
     572| 
     573| ☐ **Naming**
     574|   - [ ] Functions use PascalCase
     575|   - [ ] Entry points use `<Module>Init` pattern
     576|   - [ ] Global variables use `g` prefix
     577|   - [ ] Constants use ALL_CAPS
     578|   - [ ] No raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`)
     579| 
     580| ☐ **INF Structure**
     581|   - [ ] Sections in correct order
     582|   - [ ] `MdePkg/MdePkg.dec` first in `[Packages]`
     583|   - [ ] Entry-point library first in `[LibraryClasses]`
     584| 
     585| ☐ **Customization Layer**
     586|   - [ ] Changes in appropriate layer (outermost)
     587|   - [ ] AMI OVERRIDE markers used correctly (Intel/EDK2 only)
     588|   - [ ] SDL tokens preferred over code patches
     589| 
     590| ☐ **AMI Patterns**
     591|   - [ ] ELINK registrations have trailing comma
     592|   - [ ] Setup variable access uses proper pattern
     593|   - [ ] Boot script macros used correctly
     594|
