       1| ---
       2| name: ami-module-developer-standards
       3| description: 'AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer Guide standards validation (MANDATORY). Use when validating mandatory organization standards that impact review verdict after UEFI code review completion.'
       4| user-invocable: false
       5| disable-model-invocation: false
       6| ---
       7| 
       8| # AMI Module Developer's Guide Standards Validation (MANDATORY)
       9| 
      10| ## Purpose
      11| Validate reviewed code against **mandatory** AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide standards that extend beyond UEFI 2.10+, PI 1.8+, and EDK II C Coding Standard 2.2+ requirements.
      12| 
      13| **Compliance Level:** MANDATORY - Violations impact the final review verdict and may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK.
      14| 
      15| ## When to Use
      16| - **At the end of UEFI code review** (after Stage 3 verdict is written)
      17| - To check AMI-specific organization coding standards
      18| - Only on files that were already reviewed for UEFI/PI/EDK2 compliance
      19| 
      20| ## Scope
      21| This skill checks **AMI-specific mandatory** patterns only. UEFI/PI/EDK2 spec compliance is already covered by the main review stages.
      22| 
      23| ## Standards Reference
      24| See [AMI Module Developer Standards](./Module_Developer_Standards.md) for complete mandatory organization requirements.
      25| 
      26| ## Validation Procedure
      27| 
      28| ### Step 1: Identify Files in Scope
      29| Review only files that were checked in the main review (listed in `cc_registry.md` with Review Status ≠ `NA`). Do not review files that were skipped or excluded.
      30| 
      31| ### Step 2: Classify File Ownership (AMI vs Third-Party)
      32| 
      33| For each in-scope file, determine ownership by reading the file header (first 50 lines):
      34| 
      35| **Classification Rules:**
      36| 
      37| | Condition | Classification | Validation Scope |
      38| |-----------|----------------|------------------|
      39| | File contains AMI copyright banner (`Copyright (c) 1985-20XX, AMI`) | ✅ **AMI-owned** | Entire file |
      40| | File contains third-party copyright (Intel, Microsoft, EDK2, etc.) AND contains AMI override markers | ⚙️ **Third-party with AMI overrides** | AMI override sections only |
      41| | File contains NO copyright header | ✅ **AMI-owned** (default assumption) | Entire file |
      42| | File contains only third-party copyright, NO AMI override markers | ❌ **Third-party only** | Skip validation |
      43| 
      44| **AMI Override Marker Patterns (case-insensitive):**
      45| 
      46| Detect AMI-owned code sections in third-party files using these marker patterns:
      47| 
      48| | Start Marker | End Marker | Purpose |
      49| |--------------|------------|---------|
      50| | `// AMI OVERRIDE START` | `// AMI OVERRIDE END` | General EDK2/Intel overrides |
      51| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:` | Server platform overrides |
      52| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_END:` | Server platform RC overrides |
      53| | Any marker matching: `//<PREFIX>OVERRIDE*START` where PREFIX contains "AMI" or "APTIOV" | Corresponding `END` marker | Project-specific AMI override tags |
      54| 
      55| **Detection Pattern:**
      56| ```regex
      57| Start: ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*START
      58| End:   ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*END
      59| ```
      60| 
      61| **Copyright Detection Patterns:**
      62| - AMI: `Copyright (c) 1985-YYYY, AMI` or `Copyright.*AMI`
      63| - Intel: `Copyright.*Intel Corporation`
      64| - Microsoft: `Copyright.*Microsoft Corporation`
      65| - EDK2/TianoCore: `Copyright.*TianoCore`
      66| 
      67| ### Step 3: Extract AMI Code Sections
      68| 
      69| For each file, extract the relevant code based on classification:
      70| 
      71| **AMI-owned files (entire file validation):**
      72| - Read full file content
      73| - Validate all sections against AMI organization standards
      74| - Apply all checks from Step 4
      75| 
      76| **Third-party files with AMI overrides (section-only validation):**
      77| - Parse file line-by-line
      78| - Extract ONLY lines between AMI override START/END markers (inclusive)
      79| - Validate extracted sections only
      80| - Report findings with original file line numbers
      81| - Record: `[Third-party file — validating AMI override sections only]`
      82| 
      83| **Third-party files without AMI code:**
      84| - Skip validation entirely
      85| - Record: `[SKIP: Third-party file, no AMI code sections detected]`
      86| 
      87| **Examples:**
      88| 
      89| ```c
      90| // Intel copyright header
      91| // ...
      92| void IntelFunction() {
      93|   // Skip this - third-party code
      94| }
      95| 
      96| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add custom validation
      97| BOOLEAN gAmiCustomFlag = FALSE;  // ← Validate this (AMI code)
      98| 
      99| EFI_STATUS ValidateCustom() {   // ← Validate this (AMI code)
     100|   return EFI_SUCCESS;
     101| }
     102| // AMI OVERRIDE END
     103| 
     104| void AnotherIntelFunction() {
     105|   // Skip this - third-party code
     106| }
     107| ```
     108| 
     109| ### Step 4: Check Organization Standards
     110| 
     111| For each AMI code section (entire file OR extracted override blocks), verify:
     112| 
     113| 1. **File Header Format** (AMI-owned files only)
     114|    - AMI copyright banner present and current year (2026)
     115|    - Doxygen `@file` block with brief description
     116|    - Skip for third-party files with overrides (header is third-party)
     117| 
     118| 2. **Source File Organization** (all AMI code)
     119|    - Sections in correct order with `//------` separators (AMI files only)
     120|    - Include order follows AMI pattern (when includes are in AMI section)
     121| 
     122| 3. **Naming Conventions** (all AMI code) - MANDATORY
     123|    - Functions: PascalCase
     124|    - Entry points: `<Module>Init` pattern
     125|    - Global variables: `g` prefix + PascalCase
     126|    - Registration variables: `g<Name>Reg` pattern
     127|    - Constants/Macros: ALL_CAPS
     128|    - **No raw C types** (`int`, `char`, `void`) - **MANDATORY-MAJOR violation if present**
     129| 
     130| 4. **INF File Structure** (AMI-owned INF files only)
     131|    - Sections in AMI-specified order
     132|    - `MdePkg/MdePkg.dec` listed first in `[Packages]`
     133|    - Skip for third-party INF files
     134| 
     135| 5. **AMI-Specific Patterns** (all AMI code)
     136|    - SDL token usage
     137|    - ELINK patterns (when modified)
     138|    - Customization layer compliance
     139| 
     140| ### Step 5: Report Findings
     141| 
     142| Append findings to the review report under a new section. Distinguish file classification in reporting:
     143| 
     144| ```markdown
     145| ## AMI Module Developer's Guide Standards (MANDATORY)
     146| 
     147| Files validated against AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide mandatory standards (`.github/skills/ami-module-developer-standards/`).
     148| 
     149| ### Summary
     150| - AMI-owned files validated: X
     151| - Third-party files with AMI overrides validated: Y
     152| - Third-party files skipped (no AMI code): Z
     153| 
     154| ### Findings
     155| 
     156| **[AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c]** (AMI-owned)
     157| - File Header: Missing current year in copyright (2025 vs 2026)
     158|   - Location: Line 3
     159|   - Severity: **MANDATORY-MINOR** (impacts verdict score)
     160|   - Fix: Update copyright year to 2026
     161| - Raw C Type: `int` used instead of UEFI type
     162|   - Location: Line 147
     163|   - Severity: **MANDATORY-MAJOR** (blocking - ABI incompatibility risk)
     164|   - Fix: Replace `int bufferSize` with `UINT32 BufferSize`
     165| 
     166| **[Intel/ClientOneSiliconPkg/Cpu/LibraryPrivate/PeiCpuPolicyLib/PeiCpuPolicyLib.c]** (Third-party with AMI overrides)
     167| - [Third-party file — validating AMI override sections only: lines 245-260, 312-340]
     168| - Naming Convention: Global variable missing `g` prefix
     169|   - Location: Line 247 (inside `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` block)
     170|   - Severity: **MANDATORY-MINOR** (impacts verdict score)
     171|   - Fix: Rename `AmiCustomBuffer` to `gAmiCustomBuffer`
     172| 
     173| **[Intel/ServerSiliconPkg/Mem/Library/MemRcLib/MemRc.c]** (Third-party only)
     174| - [SKIP: Third-party file, no AMI code sections detected]
     175| 
     176| ### Module Developer Standards Verdict
     177| ✅ **COMPLIANT** / ⚠️ **NON-COMPLIANT (impacts main verdict)**
     178| 
     179| **Severity Impact on Main Review Verdict:**
     180| - **MANDATORY-MAJOR**: Added to main review MAJOR count → may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK
     181| - **MANDATORY-MINOR**: Added to main review MINOR count → impacts score calculation
     182| - **INFO**: Informational only, no verdict impact
     183| 
     184| > **Note:** Module Developer Guide violations are **MANDATORY** and affect the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK). All MANDATORY-MAJOR findings must be fixed before merge. MANDATORY-MINOR findings contribute to the overall quality score.
     185| ```
     186| 
     187| **Reporting Guidelines:**
     188| 
     189| 1. **Group by file classification:**
     190|    - AMI-owned files first
     191|    - Third-party files with AMI overrides second
     192|    - Skipped third-party files last
     193| 
     194| 2. **For AMI override sections:**
     195|    - Always include line range of override block in brackets: `[Third-party file — validating AMI override sections only: lines X-Y, Z-W]`
     196|    - Report finding location as absolute line number in original file
     197|    - Note which override block contains the finding
     198| 
     199| 3. **For skipped files:**
     200|    - Use single-line skip notation: `[SKIP: Third-party file, no AMI code sections detected]`
     201|    - Do not open findings for pure third-party code
     202| 
     203| 4. **If no findings:**
     204|    - Write: `> All reviewed files comply with AMI organization coding standards.`
     205|    - Still list skipped third-party files in summary
     206| 
     207| ### Step 6: Impact on Main Review Verdict
     208| - **DOES impact** the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK)
     209| - Module Developer Guide violations are **MANDATORY**
     210| - **MANDATORY-MAJOR** findings:
     211|   - Added to main review MAJOR count
     212|   - May change verdict from APPROVE → CONDITIONAL or BLOCK
     213|   - Must be fixed before merge
     214| - **MANDATORY-MINOR** findings:
     215|   - Added to main review MINOR count
     216|   - Contribute to quality score (affects verdict thresholds)
     217| - **Verdict recalculation:** If new MAJOR findings trigger verdict change, update `Result.txt` and append note to main verdict section
     218| - **AMI code only:** Only AMI-owned files and AMI override sections in third-party files are validated
     219| - **Third-party code skipped:** Pure third-party code (Intel, EDK2, Microsoft) is not validated
     220| - **Default assumption:** Files without copyright headers are treated as AMI-owned
     221| 
     222| ## File Classification Examples
     223| 
     224| ### Example 1: AMI-owned file
     225| ```c
     226| //***********************************************************************
     227| //*                                                                     *
     228| //*                 Copyright (c) 1985-2026, AMI.                       *
     229| //*                                                                     *
     230| //***********************************************************************
     231| /** @file NvramDxe.c
     232|     NVRAM driver implementation
     233| **/
     234| ```
     235| **Classification:** ✅ AMI-owned → Validate entire file
     236| 
     237| ### Example 2: Third-party with AMI overrides
     238| ```c
     239| /** @file
     240|   Copyright (c) 2020 - 2024, Intel Corporation. All rights reserved.
     241|   SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause-Patent
     242| **/
     243| 
     244| void IntelFunction() {
     245|   // Third-party code - skip
     246| }
     247| 
     248| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add custom initialization
     249| EFI_STATUS AmiCustomInit() {
     250|   // AMI code - validate this section only
     251| }
     252| // AMI OVERRIDE END
     253| 
     254| void AnotherIntelFunction() {
     255|   // Third-party code - skip
     256| }
     257| ```
     258| **Classification:** ⚙️ Third-party with AMI overrides → Validate override sections only (lines between START/END markers)
     259| 
     260| ### Example 3: Third-party without AMI code
     261| ```c
     262| /** @file
     263|   Copyright (c) 2020 - 2024, Intel Corporation. All rights reserved.
     264|   SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause-Patent
     265| **/
     266| 
     267| void IntelFunction() {
     268|   // No AMI override markers found
     269| }
     270| ```
     271| **Classification:** ❌ Third-party only → Skip validation
     272| 
     273| ### Example 4: No copyright (assume AMI)
     274| ```c
     275| #include <Uefi.h>
     276| 
     277| EFI_STATUS MyFunction() {
     278|   // No copyright header found
     279| }
     280| ```
     281| **Classification:** ✅ AMI-owned (default) → Validate entire file
     282| 
     283| ## Override Marker Variations
     284| 
     285| All these patterns are recognized as AMI override markers:
     286| 
     287| ```c
     288| // AMI OVERRIDE START
     289| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add validation
     290| // AMI OVERRIDE START: Fix memory leak
     291| //AMI OVERRIDE START
     292| //  AMI OVERRIDE START  
     293| 
     294| //APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:
     295| //APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:
     296| // APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START: Description
     297| 
     298| // AMI_CUSTOM_OVERRIDE_START:
     299| // AMI_PROJECT_OVERRIDE_START
     300| ```
     301| 
     302| **Detection is case-insensitive and whitespace-tolerant.**
     303| 
     304| ## Severity Classification Guidelines
     305| 
     306| ### MANDATORY-MAJOR (Blocking)
     307| - Raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`, `unsigned`) in UEFI code
     308| - Missing AMI OVERRIDE markers in Intel/EDK2 file modifications
     309| - Wrong customization layer (modifying Intel files without markers)
     310| - Missing `g` prefix on global variables in security-critical code
     311| - Entry point not following `<Module>Init` pattern
     312| 
     313| ### MANDATORY-MINOR (Score Impact)
     314| - Copyright year outdated
     315| - Function naming case incorrect (non-PascalCase)
     316| - File organization sections out of order
     317| - Include order not following AMI pattern
     318| - Missing `@file` Doxygen block
     319| - INF section order incorrect
     320| 
     321| ### INFO (Tracking Only)
     322| - Suggestions for code clarity
     323| - Optional improvements
     324| - Style preferences
     325| 
     326| ## Progressive Loading
     327| The skill loads lightweight validation logic first, then references [Module_Developer_Standards.md](./Module_Developer_Standards.md) only when detailed standard text is needed for citation.

       1| ---
       2| name: build-log-analyzer
       3| description: 'Find build failure root causes in build.log files. Use when: build failed, why did build fail, check build.log, find errors in log, build error, tsc error, compile error, AMI build failed, EDKII build error, npm build error, make error, what broke the build, build log analysis, 編譯失敗, build 失敗, 找錯誤, 看 log.'
       4| argument-hint: 'path to build.log (optional, auto-detects if omitted)'
       5| user-invocable: true
       6| ---
       7| 
       8| # Build Log Analyzer
       9| 
      10| ## Purpose
      11| Efficiently locate the **root cause failure point** in a build.log without loading the entire file.
      12| Uses streaming grep — safe for very large logs (>100 MB).
      13| 
      14| ## When to Use
      15| - User says "build failed" or "check build.log"
      16| - Need to find which module/file caused the first error
      17| - AMI/UEFI EDKII build log analysis
      18| - TypeScript `tsc` error extraction
      19| - npm / Node.js / make build failures
      20| 
      21| ## Procedure
      22| 
      23| ### Step 1 — Locate the log file
      24| Check these paths in order (stop at first match):
      25| 1. Argument provided by user
      26| 2. `Build.log` (workspace root, case-insensitive)
      27| 3. `build/Build.log`
      28| 4. `out/build.log`
      29| 5. Ask the user if none found
      30| 
      31| Use `file_search` with pattern `**/Build.log` or `**/build.log`.
      32| 
      33| ### Step 2 — Stream-scan for errors (DO NOT read the whole file)
      34| Run the PowerShell script: [find-errors.ps1](./scripts/find-errors.ps1)
      35| 
      36| ```powershell
      37| # Quick invocation:
      38| & "C:\Users\YCHsu\.github\skills\build-log-analyzer\scripts\find-errors.ps1" -LogPath "<path>"
      39| ```
      40| 
      41| Or use `grep_search` with these patterns (isRegexp=true):
      42| - `error [A-Z]|error:|ERROR|FAILED|Build FAILED|make\[.*\] \*\*\*`
      43| - For TypeScript: `error TS\d+:`
      44| - For EDKII/AMI: `ERROR - |Module .* failed|FAILED - `
      45| 
      46| ### Step 3 — Find the ROOT CAUSE (first error)
      47| 1. Identify the **earliest** error line in the file (lowest line number)
      48| 2. Read ±10 lines of context around that first error using `read_file`
      49| 3. Distinguish: root cause error vs cascading/downstream errors
      50| 
      51| ### Step 4 — Report
      52| Structure the output as:
      53| 
      54| ```
      55| ## Build Failure Summary
      56| 
      57| **Root Cause** (line N):
      58| <error message>
      59| <context>
      60| 
      61| **Error Count**: N errors total
      62| **Error Types**: [tsc | make | AMI module | linker | etc.]
      63| 
      64| **Cascading Errors** (if any): N additional errors likely caused by above
      65| ```
      66| 
      67| ## Error Pattern Reference
      68| 
      69| | Build System | Key Patterns |
      70| |---|---|
      71| | TypeScript tsc | `error TS\d+:`, `Found \d+ error` |
      72| | AMI/EDKII | `ERROR - `, `FAILED - stopping`, `make[N]: ***`, `Build FAILED` |
      73| | npm/Node | `npm ERR!`, `error:` |
      74| | make | `make\[.*\]: \*\*\* .* Error \d+`, `make: \*\*\* No rule` |
      75| | GCC/Clang | `error:.*\[-W`, `: error:` |
      76| | MSVC | `error C\d+:`, `LINK : fatal error` |
      77| 
      78| ## Important Rules
      79| - **NEVER** use `read_file` on the entire log — use `grep_search` + targeted `read_file` for context only
      80| - Report the **first** error, not the last
      81| - If >50 errors of the same type, report count + first 3 examples only
      82| - Always show the file path and line number of the root cause

FILE: .github\skills\build-log-analyzer\scripts\find-errors.ps1
LINE COUNT: 117

       1| <#
       2| .SYNOPSIS
       3|     Stream-scan a build.log for failure points without loading the entire file.
       4| .PARAMETER LogPath
       5|     Path to the build log. Defaults to auto-detect in current directory.
       6| .PARAMETER ContextLines
       7|     Lines of context to show around each error (default: 8).
       8| .PARAMETER MaxErrors
       9|     Maximum distinct errors to report (default: 10).
      10| .EXAMPLE
      11|     .\find-errors.ps1 -LogPath D:\AMI\Build.log
      12|     .\find-errors.ps1  # auto-detect
      13| #>
      14| param(
      15|     [string]$LogPath     = '',
      16|     [int]   $ContextLines = 8,
      17|     [int]   $MaxErrors    = 10
      18| )
      19| 
      20| Set-StrictMode -Version Latest
      21| $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
      22| 
      23| # ── Auto-detect log file ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      24| if (-not $LogPath) {
      25|     $candidates = @('Build.log','build.log','build\Build.log','out\build.log','logs\build.log')
      26|     foreach ($c in $candidates) {
      27|         $full = Join-Path (Get-Location) $c
      28|         if (Test-Path $full) { $LogPath = $full; break }
      29|     }
      30| }
      31| if (-not $LogPath -or -not (Test-Path $LogPath)) {
      32|     Write-Error "build.log not found. Pass -LogPath <path> or run from build directory."
      33|     exit 1
      34| }
      35| 
      36| $fileSize = (Get-Item $LogPath).Length
      37| Write-Host "Log: $LogPath  ($([math]::Round($fileSize/1KB, 1)) KB)" -ForegroundColor Cyan
      38| 
      39| # ── Error patterns ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      40| $patterns = @(
      41|     # TypeScript
      42|     'error TS\d+:',
      43|     # AMI / EDKII
      44|     'ERROR\s*-\s',
      45|     'FAILED\s*-\s*stopping',
      46|     'Build FAILED',
      47|     # make
      48|     'make\[?\d*\]?:\s*\*\*\*',
      49|     # GCC / Clang / MSVC
      50|     ':\s*error\s*[C\d]*:',
      51|     'LINK\s*:\s*fatal error',
      52|     # npm / Node
      53|     'npm ERR!',
      54|     # generic (lower priority — only if no specific match)
      55|     '^\s*ERROR\b',
      56|     'error:',
      57|     '\bFAILED\b'
      58| )
      59| $combinedPattern = ($patterns | ForEach-Object { "(?:$_)" }) -join '|'
      60| 
      61| # ── Stream scan with Select-String (no full load) ───────────────────────────
      62| Write-Host "Scanning for errors..." -ForegroundColor Yellow
      63| $matches_ = Select-String -Path $LogPath -Pattern $combinedPattern -CaseSensitive:$false |
      64|     Select-Object LineNumber, Line
      65| 
      66| if (-not $matches_ -or $matches_.Count -eq 0) {
      67|     Write-Host "No errors found in build log." -ForegroundColor Green
      68|     exit 0
      69| }
      70| 
      71| Write-Host "Found $($matches_.Count) matching lines. Showing root cause + top errors:`n" -ForegroundColor Red
      72| 
      73| # ── Read file lines for context (only the ranges we need) ───────────────────
      74| $allLines = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllLines($LogPath)
      75| $totalLines = $allLines.Length
      76| 
      77| function Show-Context {
      78|     param([int]$lineNo)  # 1-based
      79|     $start = [math]::Max(0, $lineNo - 1 - $ContextLines)
      80|     $end   = [math]::Min($totalLines - 1, $lineNo - 1 + $ContextLines)
      81|     for ($i = $start; $i -le $end; $i++) {
      82|         $prefix = if ($i -eq $lineNo - 1) { '>>> ' } else { '    ' }
      83|         $color  = if ($i -eq $lineNo - 1) { 'Red' } else { 'Gray' }
      84|         Write-Host ("{0}{1,5}: {2}" -f $prefix, ($i+1), $allLines[$i]) -ForegroundColor $color
      85|     }
      86| }
      87| 
      88| # ── Report root cause (first error) ─────────────────────────────────────────
      89| $first = $matches_[0]
      90| Write-Host "═══ ROOT CAUSE — Line $($first.LineNumber) ═══" -ForegroundColor Magenta
      91| Show-Context $first.LineNumber
      92| Write-Host ""
      93| 
      94| # ── Remaining errors (deduplicated by message prefix) ──────────────────────
      95| $reported = 1
      96| $seen = [System.Collections.Generic.HashSet[string]]::new()
      97| [void]$seen.Add($first.Line.Trim().Substring(0, [math]::Min(80, $first.Line.Trim().Length)))
      98| 
      99| $matches_ | Select-Object -Skip 1 | ForEach-Object {
     100|     if ($reported -ge $MaxErrors) { return }
     101|     $key = $_.Line.Trim().Substring(0, [math]::Min(80, $_.Line.Trim().Length))
     102|     if ($seen.Contains($key)) { return }
     103|     [void]$seen.Add($key)
     104| 
     105|     Write-Host "─── Error at Line $($_.LineNumber) ───" -ForegroundColor Yellow
     106|     Show-Context $_.LineNumber
     107|     Write-Host ""
     108|     $reported++
     109| }
     110| 
     111| # ── Summary ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     112| Write-Host "═══ Summary ═══" -ForegroundColor Cyan
     113| Write-Host "Total matching lines : $($matches_.Count)"
     114| Write-Host "Distinct errors shown: $reported"
     115| if ($matches_.Count -gt $MaxErrors) {
     116|     Write-Host "(Use -MaxErrors $($matches_.Count) to see all)" -ForegroundColor DarkYellow
     117| }

       1| Result: "---\nname: sdl-handling\ndescription: 'AMI System Description Language (SDL) handling for new driver/module integration and DSC/FDF diff porting. Covers INFComponent, LIBRARYMAPPING, PcdMapping, FFS_FILE, ELINK, TOKEN, and FD_AREA SDL objects. Use when integrating new modules or porting DSC/FDF changes from silicon vendor RC into AMI BIOS source trees.'\nargument-hint: '[INF file path, module name, or DSC/FDF diff context]'\n---\n\n# SDL Handling for RC Integration\n\nThis skill provides exact SDL syntax, porting rules, and decision logic for:\n1. Creating SDL entries for new INF modules (orphan handling)\n2. Translating DSC diff changes into SDL updates\n3. Translating FDF diff changes into SDL updates\n\nThe full AMI SDL specification is at [AMI_SDL_Specification.md](./AMI_SDL_Specification.md).\n\n> **All syntax below is verified against real AMI SDL files — field names and formats are exact.**\n\n---\n\n## SDL Object Quick Reference\n\n### INFComponent — Register an EDK II INF Module\n\n```\nINFComponent\n    Name        = \"ModuleBaseName\"         # unique name; referenced by LIBRARYMAPPING and FFS_FILE\n    File        = \"SubDir/Module.inf\"      # path relative to Package root (NOT full BIOS path)\n    Package     = \"PkgName\"               # EDK II package name\n    Arch        = \"X64\"                   # IA32 | X64 | ARM | AARCH64 | \"IA32 X64\"  (optional)\n    ModuleTypes = \"DXE_DRIVER\"            # PLURAL — never Module_Type\n    Path        = \"NAMED_PATH_ALIAS\"      # named path alias from PATH SDL object (optional)\n    Token       = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"          # three separate quoted strings: name, operator, value\nEnd\n```\n\n**Critical field rules:**\n- `ModuleTypes` is **plural** — never `Module_Type`\n- `Arch` is **optional** — omit if all architectures are supported\n- `Path` is a **named alias** (e.g. `\"SB_INIT_DIR\"`), not a filesystem path\n- `Token` condition = **three quoted strings**: `\"TOKEN_NAME\" \"OPERATOR\" \"VALUE\"`\n\n**Arch values from INF VALID_ARCHITECTURES:**\n- `X64` → `Arch = \"X64\"`\n- `IA32 X64` → `Arch = \"IA32 X64\"`\n- `IA32` → `Arch = \"IA32\"`\n- Not restricted / common → omit `Arch`\n\n---\n\n### LIBRARYMAPPING — Map Library Class to Implementation\n\n```\nLIBRARYMAPPING\n    Class       = \"LibraryClassName\"                # library class name from INF/DSC\n    Instance    = \"Package.INFComponentName\"        # dot-separated \"Package.Name\" format\n    ModuleTypes = \"PEIM\"                            # PLURAL; scope to module type (optional)\n    Arch        = \"IA32 X64\"                        # optional; space-separated architectures\n    Token       = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"                    # three quoted strings (optional)\nEnd\n```\n\n**Critical field rules:**\n- `Instance` uses **`\"Package.ComponentName\"`** dot-separated format — never just the base name\n- `ModuleTypes` is **plural** — never `Module_Type` (NDA field-table uses `Module_Type` but real Aptio V SDL uses `ModuleTypes`)\n- `Arch` is **optional** — present in real SDL files when architecture-scoping is needed; do NOT flag its presence as an error\n- No `Override` field — module-scoped library overrides are handled via `ModuleTypes` scoping\n\n---\n\n### PcdMapping — Declare a Platform Configuration Database Entry\n\n```\nPcdMapping\n    Name      = \"PcdName\"\n    GuidSpace = \"gXxxTokenSpaceGuid\"         # field is GuidSpace — NOT Guid\n    PcdType   = \"PcdsFixedAtBuild\"           # full EDK II section name WITH \"Pcds\" prefix\n    DataType  = \"BOOLEAN\"                    # CamelCase — NOT Data_Type\n    Value     = \"TRUE\"\n    Offset    = 00h                          # hex, required\n    Length    = 001h                         # hex, required\n    TargetDSC = Yes                          # emit to generated PLATFORM_DSC\n    Help      = \"Description\"               # optional\n    Token     = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"              # three quoted strings (optional)\nEnd\n```\n\n**Critical field rules:**\n- Object keyword is `PcdMapping` — **mixed case**, not `PCDMAPPING`\n- `GuidSpace` — **not** `Guid`\n- `PcdType` — **not** `Pcd_Type`; value includes full `\"Pcds\"` prefix\n- `DataType` — **not** `Data_Type`; use `\"Pointer\"` for `VOID*` string PCDs\n- `Offset` and `Length` — required fields in hex format\n\n**PcdType values (full EDK II section names):**\n\n| DSC Section | PcdType value |\n|---|---|\n| `[PcdsFeatureFlag]` | `\"PcdsFeatureFlag\"` |\n| `[PcdsFixedAtBuild]` | `\"PcdsFixedAtBuild\"` |\n| `[PcdsPatchableInModule]` | `\"PcdsPatchableInModule\"` |\n| `[PcdsDynamicDefault]` | `\"PcdsDynamicDefault\"` |\n| `[PcdsDynamicEx]` | `\"PcdsDynamicEx\"` |\n| `[PcdsDynamicHii]` | `\"PcdsDynamicHii\"` |\n| `[PcdsDynamicVpd]` | `\"PcdsDynamicVpd\"` |\n\n---\n\n### FFS_FILE — Place Module in a Firmware Volume\n\n```\nFFS_FILE\n    Name          = \"ModuleBaseNameFfs\"             # convention: append \"Ffs\"\n    FD_AREA       = \"FV_MAIN\"                       # must match an FD_AREA.Name in SDL\n    INFComponent  = \"Package.INFComponentName\"      # dot-separated \"Package.Name\" format\n    Index         = \"1\"                             # quoted integer OR '\"BEFORE\" \"OtherFfsName\"' / '\"AFTER\" \"OtherFfsName\"'\n    Apriori_Index = \"9\"                             # QUOTED string — not a bare integer\n    Help          = \"Optional description\"\n    Token         = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"                  # three quoted strings (optional)\nEnd\n```\n\n**For non-INF FFS entries (RAW binary, FREEFORM):**\n```\nFFS_FILE\n    Name      = \"SomeBinaryFfs\"\n    FD_AREA   = \"FV_BB\"\n    FILE_Stmt = \"AmiChipsetModulePkg/LegacyRom/Xhci/XhciRecoveryFirmware.txt\"\n    Token     = \"XHCI_RECOVERY_PATH_MODE\" \"=\" \"1\"\nEnd\n```\n\n**Critical field rules:**\n- `INFComponent` uses **`\"Package.ComponentName\"`** format — never just the base name\n- `Apriori_Index` is a **quoted string** — never a bare integer\n- **`Module_Type` is mutually exclusive with `INFComponent`, `File_Stmt`, and `OpRom`** (NDA spec) — when `INFComponent` is present (the normal case in Aptio V), `Module_Type` must be absent; do NOT flag this absence as a missing field\n- Multiple `Token` lines are AND-ed together\n\n**FV placement by MODULE_TYPE:**\n\n| MODULE_TYPE | FD_AREA |\n|---|---|\n| `SEC` | `FV_BB` |\n| `PEI_CORE`, `PEIM` | `FV_BB` or `FV_MAIN_PEI` |\n| `DXE_CORE`, `DXE_DRIVER`, `DXE_RUNTIME_DRIVER` | `FV_MAIN` |\n| `DXE_SMM_DRIVER` | `FV_MAIN` |\n| `UEFI_DRIVER` | `FV_MAIN` |\n| `UEFI_APPLICATION` | Exclude unless explicitly required |\n\n---\n\n### TOKEN — Define a Build-Time Configuration Value\n\n```\nTOKEN\n    Name      = \"FEATURE_NAME\"\n    Value     = \"1\"\n    Help      = \"Brief description of what this token controls\"\n    TokenType = Boolean               # Boolean | Integer  (unquoted)\n    TargetH   = Yes                   # emit #define to TOKEN.H   (C headers)\n    TargetMAK = Yes                   # emit to TOKEN.MAK          (nmake)\n    TargetEQU = Yes                   # emit to TOKEN.EQU          (ASM equates)\n    TargetASL = Yes                   # emit to TOKENS.ASL         (ACPI ASL)\n    Lock      = Yes                   # prevent SDL Wizard edits (optional)\n    Master    = Yes                   # master token — ONE per project only (optional)\n    Range     = \"0-1\"                 # valid value range (optional)\n    Token     = \"PARENT_FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\" # dependency condition (optional)\nEnd\n```\n\n**Target field guide:**\n\n| TokenType | Typical targets |\n|---|---|\n| `Boolean` | `TargetH = Yes` |\n| `Integer` | `TargetH = Yes`, `TargetMAK = Yes`, `TargetEQU = Yes` |\n| ASL-only | `TargetASL = Yes` |\n\n**Critical field rules:**\n- `TokenType` uses unquoted `Boolean` or `Integer` for hand-authored tokens; auto-generated tokens may use quoted `\"Boolean\"`\n- `Token` dependency condition — **three quoted strings**: `\"NAME\" \"OPERATOR\" \"VALUE\"`\n- `Master = Yes` — only one allowed per project; never duplicate\n\n---\n\n### ELINK — Register an Initialization Hook\n\n```\nELINK\n    Name        = \"FunctionName,\"         # TRAILING COMMA — C initializer list syntax\n    Parent      = \"ParentRoutineName\"\n    Priority    = 10                      # order among siblings (lower = runs first)\n    InvokeOrder = AfterParent             # AfterParent | BeforeParent\n    Arch        = \"X64\"                   # optional\n    Token       = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"          # three quoted strings (optional)\nEnd\n```\n\n**Critical field rules:**\n- `Name` ends with a **trailing comma** — it is inserted into a C comma-separated function pointer list\n- `Priority` controls execution order among sibling ELINKs\n- `InvokeOrder` controls position relative to the parent function call\n\n---\n\n## Token Condition Syntax — Universal Rule\n\n**Every SDL object** uses the same three-quoted-string format for conditional inclusion:\n\n```\nToken = \"TOKEN_NAME\" \"OPERATOR\" \"VALUE\"\n```\n\n| Operator string | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `\"=\"` | equal |\n| `\"!=\"` | not equal |\n| `\">=\"` | greater or equal |\n| `\"<=\"` | less or equal |\n| `\">\"` | greater than |\n| `\"<\"` | less than |\n\n**Multiple conditions** = multiple `Token` lines (ALL must be true — AND logic):\n```\nToken = \"FEATURE_A\" \"=\" \"1\"\nToken = \"PLATFORM_X\" \"!=\" \"0\"\n```\n\n**Else-branch** = second SDL object with inverse condition:\n```\nPcdMapping                          # DEBUG_MODE = 1 branch\n    Name      = \"PcdDebugPropertyMask\"\n    ...\n    Token     = \"DEBUG_MODE\" \"=\" \"1\"\nEnd\nPcdMapping                          # DEBUG_MODE = 0 branch\n    Name      = \"PcdDebugPropertyMask\"\n    ...\n    Token     = \"DEBUG_MODE\" \"=\" \"0\"\nEnd\n```\n\n---\n\n## DSC-to-SDL Translation Table\n\n| DSC Element | SDL Object | Key Field Mapping |\n|---|---|---|\n| `[LibraryClasses]` global | `LIBRARYMAPPING` | `Class` = lib name, `Instance` = `\"Pkg.CompName\"` |\n| `[LibraryClasses.common.DXE_DRIVER]` | `LIBRARYMAPPING` | + `ModuleTypes = \"DXE_DRIVER\"` |\n| `[Components]` INF entry | `INFComponent` + `FFS_FILE` | `Name` from BASE_NAME, `File` from INF path |\n| `[Components.X64]` | `INFComponent` | `Arch = \"X64\"` |\n| Component `<LibraryClasses>` override | `LIBRARYMAPPING` | `Instance = \"Pkg.CompName\"`, `ModuleTypes` scoped |\n| Component `<PcdsFixedAtBuild>` | `PcdMapping` | `PcdType = \"PcdsFixedAtBuild\"`, `TargetDSC = Yes` |\n| `[PcdsFixedAtBuild]` global | `PcdMapping` | `PcdType = \"PcdsFixedAtBuild\"`, `TargetDSC = Yes` |\n| `[PcdsDynamicDefault]` | `PcdMapping` | `PcdType = \"PcdsDynamicDefault\"` |\n| `[PcdsFeatureFlag]` | `PcdMapping` | `PcdType = \"PcdsFeatureFlag\"` |\n| `[PcdsDynamicHii]` | `PcdMapping` | `PcdType = \"PcdsDynamicHii\"` |\n| `!if $(FLAG) == 1` | `Token` line | `Token = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"` on affected SDL object |\n| `!ifdef FLAG` | `Token` line | `Token = \"FLAG\" \"=\" \"1\"` |\n| `!if $(A)==1 AND $(B)==1` | Two `Token` lines | One line per condition |\n| `!else` branch | Second SDL object | Inverse `Token` condition value |\n\n---\n\n## FDF-to-SDL Translation Table\n\n| FDF Element | SDL Object | Key Field Mapping |\n|---|---|---|\n| `[FV.FV_NAME]` section | `FD_AREA` | `Name = \"FV_NAME\"` |\n| `INF Pkg/Driver.inf` inside FV | `FFS_FILE` | `FD_AREA = \"FV_NAME\"`, `INFComponent = \"Pkg.Name\"` |\n| `APRIORI DXE { INF ... }` | `FFS_FILE` | + `Apriori_Index = \"N\"` (quoted string) |\n| `FILE RAW = {GUID} { SECTION RAW = f }` | `FFS_FILE` | `FILE_Stmt = \"path/to/file.txt\"` |\n| `SET gGuid.PcdName = Value` | `PcdMapping` or `TOKEN` | Update `Value` field |\n\n---\n\n## AMI Ownership Decision Logic\n\nBefore creating any SDL entry, always search AMI SDL first:\n\n```\nSearch AMI SDL for matching entry\n│\n├── FOUND\n│   ├── AMI value == OLD_RC value → AMI was tracking vendor → PORT to NEW_RC value\n│   └── AMI value != OLD_RC value → AMI has intentional customisation → PRESERVE AMI value\n│\n└── NOT FOUND → CREATE new SDL entry using NEW_RC value\n```\n\n---\n\n## FV Name Resolution (FV Names Don't Match)\n\nWhen NEW_RC FDF uses a different FV name than AMI:\n1. List INFs inside the vendor FV\n2. Search AMI SDL `FFS_FILE` for those INFs (via `INFComponent`)\n3. The `FD_AREA` on those existing entries = AMI's equivalent FV\n4. Use that AMI FV name for all new `FFS_FILE` entries\n\n---\n\n## Complete Token Condition Porting Example\n\n```\n# DSC !if guard:\n!if $(NEW_FEATURE_SUPPORT) == 1\n  SomePkg/SomeDriver/SomeDriver.inf\n!endif\n\n# → SDL INFComponent:\nINFComponent\n    Name        = \"SomeDriver\"\n    File        = \"SomeDriver/SomeDriver.inf\"\n    Package     = \"SomePkg\"\n    ModuleTypes = \"DXE_DRIVER\"\n    Token       = \"NEW_FEATURE_SUPPORT\" \"=\" \"1\"\nEnd\n\n# → SDL FFS_FILE:\nFFS_FILE\n    Name         = \"SomeDriverFfs\"\n    FD_AREA      = \"FV_MAIN\"\n    INFComponent = \"SomePkg.SomeDriver\"\n    Token        = \"NEW_FEATURE_SUPPORT\" \"=\" \"1\"\nEnd\n```\n\n---\n\n## Validation Rules\n\n1. `INFComponent.Name` — unique across all SDL files\n2. `INFComponent.File` — path relative to Package root must exist on disk\n3. `LIBRARYMAPPING.Instance` — must be `\"Package.Name\"` matching an existing `INFComponent.Name`\n4. `FFS_FILE.INFComponent` — must be `\"Package.Name\"` matching an existing `INFComponent.Name`\n5. `FFS_FILE.FD_AREA` — must match an existing `FD_AREA.Name` in SDL\n6. `TOKEN.Name` — unique across all SDL files; `Master = Yes` only once per project\n7. `Token` condition names — must reference existing `TOKEN.Name` values\n8. `PcdMapping.PcdType` — must include full `\"Pcds\"` prefix\n9. `FFS_FILE.Apriori_Index` — must be a **quoted string**, not a bare integer\n10. GUID values — **never modify**; only reference or add new ones\n"

       1| Result: "---\nname: unit-test-review\ndescription: 'EDK II / AMI unit test code review (MANDATORY when IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE). Covers framework structure standards, test case coverage assessment, and AMI coding standards for unit test source. Invoked automatically in Stage 3 Step 3.10 when unit test files are detected in the diff.'\nuser-invocable: false\ndisable-model-invocation: false\n---\n\n# Unit Test Code Review — AMI / EDK II Framework (MANDATORY when IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE)\n\n## Purpose\n\nValidate unit test source code in an MR against:\n1. **Framework structure standards** — correct use of the EDK II `UnitTestFrameworkPkg` / cmocka framework and AMI-specific structural rules\n2. **Test case coverage** — adequate scenario coverage against the production functions being tested\n3. **AMI coding standards** — AMI-specific style and hygiene rules that apply to unit test source\n\n**Standards source:** AMI unit test development rules (`copilot-instructions.md`, Rules #1–#20, Sections 3–4) and the standard EDK II `UnitTestFrameworkPkg` framework conventions.\n\n**Compliance level:**\n- Framework structure violations and coverage violations → scored into the main verdict (MAJOR violations can cause BLOCK)\n- AMI coding standards for unit tests → MINOR severity (per rule scope); do not block on style alone\n- **Severity cap: maximum MAJOR for all `HOST_APPLICATION` unit test findings** — `CRITICAL` requires confirmation that the unit test code is included in a firmware binary (verify FDF/DSC inclusion chain before assigning)\n\n---\n\n## When to Use\n\n- **Automatically** — when `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE` (set by Stage 1 Gate G9); invoked in Stage 3 Step 3.10\n- **Files in scope:** All files in the diff where the path contains `UnitTest/`, `GoogleTest/`, or the filename matches `*UnitTestHost.c`, `*UnitTestMock.c`, or `*UnitTestHost.inf`\n- **Skip:** Production source files (`.c`/`.h` under non-test paths), firmware INFs/DSCs/FDFs unrelated to unit test builds\n\n---\n\n## Validation Procedure\n\nApply the three sections below in order. Each produces findings that are appended to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` in Step 3.10. All findings are tagged `[UNIT-TEST]` to distinguish them from firmware findings.\n\n---\n\n## Section 1 — Framework Structure Standards\n\n### 1.1 — File Separation Rule (Rule #17 / File Separation Rule)\n\n**What to check:** Verify the test driver file layout is correct.\n\n| Rule | Violation | Severity |\n|------|-----------|----------|\n| `*UnitTestHost.c` must contain test cases and suite setup/teardown ONLY | Mock implementations (cmocka `__wrap_*`, stub functions, `will_return`/`expect_value` setup that belongs in the mock layer) present in `*UnitTestHost.c` | **MAJOR** |\n| `*UnitTestMock.c` must contain all mock implementations ONLY | Test cases (`UNIT_TEST_STATUS EFIAPI ...`) or suite setup/teardown functions present in `*UnitTestMock.c` | **MAJOR** |\n| One test driver per module — `*UnitTestHost.c` covers ALL `.c` files in the module's `[Sources]` | Multiple `*UnitTestHost.c` files for the same module without explicit justification (incompatible PEI vs. DXE contexts is the only valid exception; exception MUST be documented in a comment) | **MINOR-A** |\n| Additional production `.c` files added to the module must be added to the EXISTING `*UnitTestHost.inf [Sources]` — NOT a new `*UnitTestHost.c`/`.inf` pair | New `*UnitTestHost.c` or `*UnitTestHost.inf` created for a single new `.c` file in an existing module | **MINOR-A** |\n\n### 1.2 — INF Requirements\n\n**What to check:** Inspect each `*UnitTestHost.inf` and mock library INF in the diff.\n\n| Rule | Violation | Severity |\n|------|-----------|----------|\n| Test driver INF must have `MODULE_TYPE = HOST_APPLICATION` | `MODULE_TYPE` is any value other than `HOST_APPLICATION` | **MAJOR** |\n| `HOST_APPLICATION` INF must NOT have `[Depex]` section | `[Depex]` section present in a `HOST_APPLICATION` INF | **MAJOR** |\n| Mock library INF (`Mock*.inf`) must include `[BuildOptions]` with `MSFT:*_*_*_CC_FLAGS = /EHsc` | Missing `/EHsc` C++ exception flag on a mock INF that contains `.cpp` sources | **MINOR-A** |\n| Mock library INF `LIBRARY_CLASS` must equal `BASE_NAME` without the `Mock` prefix (e.g., `MockUefiLib.inf` → `LIBRARY_CLASS = UefiLib`) | `LIBRARY_CLASS` does not strip the `Mock` prefix | **MINOR-B** |\n| `BASE_NAME` of the test driver INF must contain the word `Test` (required for auto-discovery by test runner plugins) | `BASE_NAME` does not contain `Test` | **MINOR-B** |\n\n### 1.3 — Library Selection Hierarchy (3-Tier Rule)\n\n**What to check:** For every library class resolved in `*UnitTestHost.inf` and test DSC, verify the tier is correct.\n\n**Tier hierarchy (strictly enforced):**\n\n| Tier | When to use | Library prefix |\n|------|-------------|----------------|\n| **Tier 1 — PRIMARY** | Positive test paths — ALWAYS check first | `UnitTest*` (e.g., `UnitTestUefiBootServicesTableLib`, `UnitTestMemoryAllocationLib`, `UnitTestPeiServicesTablePointerLib`) |\n| **Tier 2 — GAP FILL** | ONLY when Tier 1 library does not exist, OR Tier 1 returns `EFI_NOT_AVAILABLE_YET` / `EFI_UNSUPPORTED` for the function being called | `Mock*` |\n| **Tier 3 — FAILURE INJECTION** | Negative tests only — forcing error returns | `Mock*` with `will_return(ErrorCode)` |\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| `Mock*` library used in positive test context when a functional `UnitTest*` library exists for the same class (Tier 1 skipped without justification) | **MAJOR** — positive test does not exercise real code path |\n| `Mock*` used as Tier 2 gap fill without documented evidence that the `UnitTest*` library returns a stub value (`EFI_NOT_AVAILABLE_YET` or `EFI_UNSUPPORTED`) | **MINOR-A** — gap fill claim unverified |\n| `Token.h` present as a static file in the test directory | **MAJOR** — `Token.h` is a build-generated file; static `Token.h` will conflict with the SDK-generated version. Correct pattern: `#ifndef CONSTANT_NAME / #define CONSTANT_NAME value / #endif` guards in `*UnitTestHost.c` |\n\n### 1.4 — Internal Function Mock Prohibition (Rule #9)\n\n**What to check:** For every mock or stub function in `*UnitTestMock.c` and every `expect_value`/`will_return` in `*UnitTestHost.c`, verify the target function is NOT defined in the same production `.c` file listed in `[Sources]`.\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| A function defined in the SAME production `.c` file (listed in `[Sources]`) is mocked or stubbed — regardless of mock mechanism (`__wrap_*`, cmocka stub, or `Mock*` library override) | **MAJOR** — mocking internal production functions makes the test functionally incorrect; real bugs in internal helpers are hidden from the test |\n\n**How to verify:** Check each mocked symbol name against the production source files listed in `[Sources]` of the test INF. If the symbol is defined in one of those files → MAJOR violation.\n\n### 1.5 — Suite Setup/Teardown Signatures (Rule #15)\n\n**What to check:** Every function registered as suite setup or suite teardown must have the correct signature.\n\n| Rule | Violation | Severity |\n|------|-----------|----------|\n| Suite setup/teardown prototype: `VOID EFIAPI FunctionName (VOID)` | Return type is `EFI_STATUS` instead of `VOID` — causes MSVC C4113 compiler error | **MAJOR** |\n| Suite setup/teardown prototype: `VOID EFIAPI FunctionName (VOID)` | Parameter list includes `IN UNIT_TEST_FRAMEWORK_HANDLE Framework` — wrong signature | **MAJOR** |\n| `InitUnitTestUefiSystemTableLibServices()` and `InitMockUefiSystemTableLibServices()` return `VOID` — must NOT be assigned | Return value assigned to an `EFI_STATUS` variable — causes MSVC C2186 compiler error | **MAJOR** |\n\n### 1.6 — Forward Declarations (Rule #16)\n\n**What to check:** In `*UnitTestHost.c`, check whether the test file calls any production functions that are defined in `.c` files listed under `[Sources]` of the test INF.\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| `*UnitTestHost.c` calls a production function by name (without going through a mock or protocol pointer) where that function is defined in a `[Sources]` `.c` file, AND no forward declaration matching the production function's exact signature is present near the top of `*UnitTestHost.c` | **MINOR-A** — MSVC C4013 warning causes silent `int` return type assumption for pointer-returning functions; can corrupt `EFI_EVENT`, `EFI_HANDLE`, and protocol pointer return values |\n\n---\n\n## Section 2 — Test Case Coverage\n\n### 2.1 — Test Case Count (Section 4 / Rules #4A–#4B)\n\n**What to check:** For each production function under test, count the test cases registered in `AddTestCase()` calls in `*UnitTestHost.c`.\n\n| Rule | Violation | Severity |\n|------|-----------|----------|\n| At least ONE positive test case per function | No positive test case for a production function in scope | **MAJOR** |\n| At least ONE negative test case per function | No negative test case for a production function in scope | **MAJOR** |\n| Exactly ONE positive test case per function (all success paths consolidated) | More than one positive test case for the same function without documented justification | **MINOR-A** — consolidate per Rule 4A |\n| Exactly ONE negative test case per function (all error paths consolidated) | More than one negative test case for the same function without documented justification | **MINOR-A** — consolidate per Rule 4B |\n\n> **Note on \"missing\" test cases:** A function is \"in scope\" when it appears in the production source files listed under `[Sources]` of the test INF AND is not a static internal helper (those are covered through the main function's execution flow per Rule #9/#13). Trivial getters, pure wrappers, and unreachable functions are exempt — this must be documented in the review.\n\n### 2.2 — Coverage Thresholds (Rule #12)\n\n**What to check:** If coverage metrics are available in the MR (e.g., from a CI pipeline report, a coverage XML artifact, or inline comments), verify the thresholds.\n\n| Threshold | Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|-----------|----------|\n| Code coverage ≥ 90% of executable lines | Coverage reported or estimated below 90% of executable lines | **MAJOR** |\n| Branch coverage ≥ 80% of **reachable** branches | Coverage reported or estimated below 80% of reachable branches | **MAJOR** |\n\n> **\"Reachable\" branches** exclude branches pre-classified as `UNREACHABLE` (dead code: always-false conditions, code after unconditional return, `ASSERT(FALSE)` paths) or `INFEASIBLE` (host environment limitation: hardware register access, physical bus operations, SMBIOS structures unavailable on host). If the MR does not classify its dead code, flag uncovered branches as MINOR-A only (cannot distinguish UNREACHABLE from oversight without production code analysis).\n\n> **When no coverage data is available:** Do not guess coverage values. Instead, identify obvious missing scenarios: check that each `if`/`else`, each non-`EFI_SUCCESS` return path, and each `NULL` pointer guard in the production function has at least one corresponding test scenario. Flag clearly missing scenarios as MINOR-A.\n\n### 2.3 — Mock Expectation Correctness (Rule #10)\n\n**What to check:** For every `expect_value()`, `expect_any()`, `will_return()`, or `expect_ptr()` call in `*UnitTestHost.c` or `*UnitTestMock.c`, verify the expectation matches the mock implementation.\n\n**The rule:** Before writing any expectation, the mock `.c` source must be read to find:\n- All `check_expected(ParamName)` calls → each one requires a matching `expect_value()` or `expect_any()` in the test\n- Whether the function calls `mock()` for its return value → if YES, `will_return()` is required; if NO, `will_return()` must NOT be added\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| `expect_value(MockFunc, Param, X)` added for a parameter that has NO `check_expected(Param)` in the mock source | **MAJOR** — causes \"still has values that haven't been checked\" test failure |\n| `will_return(MockFunc, X)` added when the mock source does NOT call `mock()` for its return value | **MAJOR** — causes \"still has values\" test failure |\n| Multiple `will_return(MockFunc, X)` calls for a single mock invocation without a corresponding second call in the test flow | **MAJOR** — causes \"still has values\" or unexpected return value |\n| `expect_value()`/`will_return()` copied from another test function without verifying the mock implementation | **MINOR-A** — high risk of incorrect expectations; requires explicit verification note |\n\n> **Review approach:** When this section identifies a suspected violation, read the mock `.c` source file to confirm before recording the finding. If the mock source is not in the diff and cannot be located in the workspace, record `[UNIT-TEST-MOCK-UNVERIFIED: <symbol> — mock source not found; expectation correctness unverifiable]` at MINOR-A.\n\n### 2.4 — Memory Allocation Failure Coverage (Rule #11)\n\n**What to check:** For each production function that calls `AllocatePool`, `AllocateZeroPool`, `ReallocatePool`, or `gBS->AllocatePool`, verify that the negative test case includes an allocation failure scenario.\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| Production function allocates memory AND has a non-trivial error path on allocation failure, BUT the negative test case does not include a `will_return(AllocatePool, 0)` or equivalent `NULL` injection | **MINOR-A** — allocation failure path untested |\n\n---\n\n## Section 3 — AMI Coding Standards for Unit Test Source\n\n### 3.1 — Inline Scenario Comments (Rule #20)\n\n**What to check:** Every distinct scenario block within a test case function body must be prefixed with an inline comment in the required format.\n\n**Required format:**\n```c\n// FunctionName: brief description of what this scenario tests\n<test code for that scenario>\n```\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| Scenario block present but no inline comment prefix | **MINOR-A** |\n| Inline comment present but does not follow `// FunctionName: description` format (e.g., uses a generic comment not tied to the function under test) | **MINOR-B** |\n\n**Forbidden inline comment patterns (Rule #19/#20):**\n\n| Forbidden pattern | Example | Severity |\n|-------------------|---------|----------|\n| Tier-rule annotations | `// Tier 1 — UnitTest*` · `// Tier 3 — failure injection` | **MINOR-B** |\n| Phase-label markers | `// Phase 2: setup` · `// Phase 3: negative path` | **MINOR-B** |\n| Rule number references | `// Per Rule #10 — mock expects parameter` | **MINOR-B** |\n| TODO / FIXME notes in committed test code | `// TODO: add more scenarios` | **MINOR-B** |\n| Commented-out code blocks | Blocks of `//`-prefixed non-comment code | **MINOR-B** |\n\n### 3.2 — No Standalone Brace Blocks (Rule #18)\n\n**What to check:** Scan each test case function body for standalone `{}` blocks that wrap sequential test scenario code without a controlling statement.\n\n| Violation | Example | Severity |\n|-----------|---------|----------|\n| Sequential scenario code wrapped in `{ ... }` without a controlling `if`/`for`/`while`/`switch` | `{ Status = Func(); UT_ASSERT_NOT_EFI_ERROR(Status); }` | **MINOR-B** |\n\n**Allowed `{}` uses:** function bodies, `if`/`else`/`for`/`while`/`switch` control bodies, struct/array initializers — do NOT flag these.\n\n### 3.3 — AMI Copyright Header (AMI standard)\n\n**What to check:** Every new `*UnitTestHost.c` and `*UnitTestMock.c` file added in the diff must contain the AMI copyright banner as the first non-blank block.\n\n| Violation | Severity |\n|-----------|----------|\n| Missing AMI copyright banner (`Copyright (c) 1985-YYYY, AMI`) on a new `*UnitTestHost.c` or `*UnitTestMock.c` | **MINOR-A** |\n\n### 3.4 — File Naming Conventions\n\n**What to check:** Verify that new unit test files follow the AMI naming pattern.\n\n| Rule | Violation | Severity |\n|------|-----------|----------|\n| Test host file: `<ModuleName>UnitTestHost.c` | Test host `.c` file does not end in `UnitTestHost.c` | **MINOR-B** |\n| Mock file: `<ModuleName>UnitTestMock.c` | Mock `.c` file does not end in `UnitTestMock.c` | **MINOR-B** |\n\n---\n\n## Section 4 — Severity Reference\n\n| Category | Specific Violation | Severity |\n|----------|--------------------|----------|\n| Framework | Mock implementations in `*UnitTestHost.c` / test cases in `*UnitTestMock.c` | MAJOR |\n| Framework | `MODULE_TYPE ≠ HOST_APPLICATION` on test driver | MAJOR |\n| Framework | `[Depex]` section present in `HOST_APPLICATION` INF | MAJOR |\n| Framework | `Mock*` used in positive test when functional `UnitTest*` exists (Tier 1 skipped) | MAJOR |\n| Framework | Static `Token.h` file created instead of `#ifndef` guards | MAJOR |\n| Framework | Internal production function mocked (Rule #9) | MAJOR |\n| Framework | Wrong suite setup/teardown signature (not `VOID EFIAPI ... (VOID)`) | MAJOR |\n| Framework | `InitUnitTestUefiSystemTableLibServices()` return assigned to `EFI_STATUS` | MAJOR |\n| Framework | Multiple test drivers per module without justification (Rule #17) | MINOR-A |\n| Framework | Missing `/EHsc` on mock INF `[BuildOptions]` | MINOR-A |\n| Framework | Missing forward declaration for production function (Rule #16) | MINOR-A |\n| Framework | Gap-fill Mock* use without UnitTest* stub verification (Tier 2 unverified) | MINOR-A |\n| Framework | `LIBRARY_CLASS` does not strip `Mock` prefix | MINOR-B |\n| Framework | `BASE_NAME` does not contain `Test` | MINOR-B |\n| Coverage | Code coverage < 90% of executable lines (Rule #12) | MAJOR |\n| Coverage | Branch coverage < 80% of reachable branches (Rule #12) | MAJOR |\n| Coverage | Positive test case missing for a function in scope | MAJOR |\n| Coverage | Negative test case missing for a function in scope | MAJOR |\n| Coverage | Wrong mock expectations (`expect_value` with no `check_expected`, `will_return` with no `mock()`) (Rule #10) | MAJOR |\n| Coverage | Multiple `will_return` for single call without multiple invocations | MAJOR |\n| Coverage | Allocation failure path not tested (Rule #11) | MINOR-A |\n| Coverage | More than 1 positive or 1 negative case per function not consolidated | MINOR-A |\n| Coverage | Expectations copied without mock source verification (Rule #10) | MINOR-A |\n| AMI Style | Missing inline scenario comment (Rule #20) | MINOR-A |\n| AMI Style | Missing AMI copyright banner on new test file | MINOR-A |\n| AMI Style | Inline comment wrong format (not `// FunctionName: description`) | MINOR-B |\n| AMI Style | Tier/phase/rule annotations in comments (Rule #19/#20) | MINOR-B |\n| AMI Style | TODO/FIXME in committed test code (Rule #19) | MINOR-B |\n| AMI Style | Standalone `{}` blocks around scenario code (Rule #18) | MINOR-B |\n| AMI Style | File naming convention violation | MINOR-B |\n\n---\n\n## Section 5 — Known Non-Violations (Do NOT Flag)\n\nThese patterns appear in unit test code and are **correct**. Do not flag any of the following:\n\n| Pattern | Why it is correct |\n|---------|------------------|\n| `MODULE_TYPE = HOST_APPLICATION` | Valid unit test driver type |\n| Missing `[Depex]` in a `HOST_APPLICATION` INF | `[Depex]` is not valid for `HOST_APPLICATION` |\n| `.cpp` source file extension | Valid for GoogleTest-based tests |\n| `extern \"C\" { #include <Uefi.h> }` in `.cpp` files | **Mandatory** — prevents C++ linkage errors for C headers |\n| `UT_ASSERT_EQUAL`, `UT_ASSERT_NOT_EFI_ERROR`, `UT_ASSERT_STATUS_EQUAL`, `UT_ASSERT_TRUE`, `UT_ASSERT_FALSE` | `UnitTestLib` assertion macros |\n| `will_return()`, `expect_value()`, `expect_any()`, `check_expected()` | cmocka mock framework calls |\n| `EXPECT_CALL()`, `WillOnce()`, `Return()`, `TEST()`, `TEST_F()` | GoogleTest / gMock framework calls |\n| `#include <Library/GoogleTestLib.h>`, `#include <Library/FunctionMockLib.h>`, `#include <cmocka.h>` | Framework dependency includes |\n| `MOCK_INTERFACE_DECLARATION`, `MOCK_INTERFACE_DEFINITION`, `MOCK_FUNCTION_DECLARATION`, `MOCK_FUNCTION_DEFINITION` | `FunctionMockLib` mock construction macros |\n| `using namespace testing;` in `.cpp` test files | GoogleTest namespace import — standard pattern |\n| `#ifndef TOKEN_CONSTANT / #define TOKEN_CONSTANT value / #endif` in `*UnitTestHost.c` | Correct Token.h constant replacement per Rule #6 |\n| No `EFIAPI` on C++ test functions | C++ linkage — `EFIAPI` calling convention is a C concept |\n| `InitUnitTestUefiSystemTableLibServices()` or `InitMockUefiSystemTableLibServices()` called as standalone statement (not assigned) | Correct usage — these return `VOID` |\n\n---\n\n## Section 6 — Output Format\n\nAppend the following section to `Detailed_Review_Report.md` and a condensed summary to `Review_Report.md` after Step 3.10 completes.\n\n### Detailed Report Section\n\n```markdown\n---\n\n## Unit Test Standards Review [UNIT-TEST]\n\nFiles reviewed against AMI EDK II Unit Test Framework standards (`.github/skills/unit-test-review/`).\nUnit test detection signal: `IS_UNIT_TEST_REVIEW = TRUE` — <N> unit test file(s) in diff.\n\n### Summary\n- Unit test files reviewed: <N>\n- Framework structure findings: <C>C / <M>M / <m>m / <i>I\n- Coverage findings: <C>C / <M>M / <m>m / <i>I\n- AMI coding standards findings: <C>C / <M>M / <m>m / <i>I\n\n### Goal 1 — Framework Structure Standards\n\n*(One entry per finding — use standard finding format with F-ID continuing from main review)*\n\n**F<N> — [UNIT-TEST] <Brief title>**\n- **File:** `path/to/UnitTestHost.c`\n- **Location:** [UnitTestHost.c:<line>](../../path/UnitTestHost.c#L<line>)\n- **Severity:** MAJOR / MINOR-A / MINOR-B\n- **Rule:** Rule #<N> / Section 1.<N>\n- **Finding:** <Description of the violation>\n- **Fix:** <Concrete action required>\n\n*(If no findings: \"> All framework structure rules satisfied.\")*\n\n### Goal 2 — Test Case Coverage\n\n*(One entry per finding)*\n\n**F<N> — [UNIT-TEST] <Brief title>**\n- **File:** `path/to/UnitTestHost.c`\n- **Location:** [UnitTestHost.c:<line>](../../path/UnitTestHost.c#L<line>)\n- **Severity:** MAJOR / MINOR-A\n- **Rule:** Rule #<N> / Section 2.<N>\n- **Finding:** <Description>\n- **Fix:** <Concrete action required>\n\n*(If no findings: \"> Test case coverage meets all requirements.\")*\n\n### Goal 3 — AMI Coding Standards (Unit Test)\n\n*(One entry per finding)*\n\n**F<N> — [UNIT-TEST] <Brief title>**\n- **File:** `path/to/UnitTestHost.c`\n- **Location:** [UnitTestHost.c:<line>](../../path/UnitTestHost.c#L<line>)\n- **Severity:** MINOR-A / MINOR-B\n- **Rule:** Rule #<N> / Section 3.<N>\n- **Finding:** <Description>\n- **Fix:** <Concrete action required>\n\n*(If no findings: \"> All AMI unit test coding standard rules satisfied.\")*\n\n### Unit Test Review Verdict\n\n> **Severity cap:** All findings are HOST_APPLICATION scope only. CRITICAL is not applicable unless unit test code is confirmed shipped inside a firmware binary.\n\n| Category | MAJOR | MINOR-A | MINOR-B |\n|----------|-------|---------|---------|\n| Framework Structure | <N> | <N> | <N> |\n| Coverage | <N> | <N> | <N> |\n| AMI Coding Standards | <N> | <N> | <N> |\n\n*(Findings are scored into the main verdict using the standard scoring table — each MAJOR = +5, each MINOR-A = +2, each MINOR-B = +1, ×2 multiplier for P1/P2 files)*\n```\n\n### Summary Report Section (`Review_Report.md`)\n\n```markdown\n## Unit Test Standards Review [UNIT-TEST]\n**Files reviewed:** <N> | **MAJOR:** <M> | **MINOR-A:** <mA> | **MINOR-B:** <mB>\n\n| F-ID | File | Rule | Severity | Finding |\n|------|------|------|----------|---------|\n| F<N> | `path/file.c` | Rule #<N> | MAJOR | One-line description |\n\n*(Omit table if no findings — write \"> All unit test standards satisfied.\")*\n\n> Unit test findings are scored into the main verdict. Severity cap: MAJOR maximum for HOST_APPLICATION code.\n```\n"
